# Facts read from other projects' sources

Every fact about another program that kubegap relies on, with where kubegap relies on it and the line of the project's source that states it, at the version kubegap pins in `lib/tools.py` (or supports). `references/development.md` says why: a sentence written from `--help`, from one cluster or from memory has already been wrong in this repository. `tests/test_sources.py` checks that every row has a `file:line@version` citation and that the kubegap location exists.

How to read a row: *Fact* is what kubegap assumes; *kubegap* is the function, step or check that assumes it; *Source* is the file and line at the tag, as `path:line@tag`; *Holds for* says which other versions were checked. A fact that could not be found in source is marked **unverified** with the reason. "Observed" in a row means it was also seen in a real run; a fact marked only observed is not verified.

Sources are fetched with `curl -fsSL https://raw.githubusercontent.com/<owner>/<repo>/<tag>/<path>` into a scratch folder that is never executed.

## Tool output files (`type: tool` steps of `collectors/core/collect.yaml`, issue #119)

| The cluster collector lists 41 resource types (`podsecuritypolicies` twice in the table) and keeps only objects with a `last-applied-configuration` annotation; every List is announced by `Retrieving: <resource>.<version>.<group>` and a failed List by `Failed to retrieve: <gvr>: <err>`, both at Debug, then skipped; the Info line `Retrieved N resources from collector name=Cluster` counts the annotated objects, so `Retrieved 0` is what a cluster managed by Helm or server-side apply prints after reading everything; an init failure logs `Failed to initialize collector` and the run still exits 0. The default log level is Info and `--log-level` sets it, so the step passes `--log-level=debug` and `Retrieving` minus `Failed to retrieve` is the read evidence (`lists_ok`, #132); a positive `Retrieved N` still counts for a log without List lines | `lib/collect.py:_run_tool_step` (api_read, lists_ok, lists_failed; #131, #132), step `core.kubent` | `pkg/collector/cluster.go:74-114,119-142@0.7.3` (`:121,124` the pair, `:129-130` the annotation filter); `cmd/kubent/main.go:45,49-56,180@0.7.3`; `pkg/config/config.go:38,53@0.7.3` | 0.7.3; observed on k3s (41 started, 13 failed, `Retrieved 185`) and EKS (41, 13, `Retrieved 162`); against a server that refuses the connection 41 started and 41 failed, exit 0 |
| The 41 resource types of the cluster collector hold no Secret, and the collector that reads Helm release Secrets is off with `--helm3=false`, so kubent never reads a Secret; it has no rule file after `deprecated-1-32.rego`, which is why pluto stays | step `core.kubent` (#154) | `pkg/collector/cluster.go:74-114@0.7.3`; `pkg/config/config.go:48@0.7.3` | 0.7.3, the newest release (2024-08-30) |
| Logs go to stderr through zerolog's ConsoleWriter with colours (`\x1b[36mname=\x1b[0mCluster`), removed with `lib/mask.py:strip_control` before the lines are read | `_run_tool_step`, `lib/mask.py:strip_control` | `cmd/kubent/main.go:180@0.7.3` | 0.7.3; observed |
| Exit 0 even with findings unless `-e/--exit-error`; 100 when a collector fails to retrieve | step `core.kubent` | `pkg/config/config.go:46@0.7.3`; `cmd/kubent/main.go:30-34, 128, 164-167@0.7.3` | 0.7.3 |

## Pod /metrics names of the components kubegap scrapes itself (`type: pod-metrics` steps, issue #120)

The names, labels, version ranges and `file:line@tag` of every metric a pod-metrics step collects and a parser reads live next to the step, in `collectors/<area>/pod-metrics.yaml`, and `lib/podmetrics.py` reads them: a parser asks for a logical key and gets the first spelling the running version emits. `tests/test_sources.py` requires a citation on every spelling, a well-formed `since`/`before`, a `source:` on every pod-metrics step, and that no parser spells a metric outside its table; `tests/test_podmetrics.py` checks that every name passes the step's `keep` filter and that a renamed metric is read under either spelling, never both. The rows below are the facts that are not per-name.

| Fact | kubegap | Source | Holds for |
|---|---|---|---|
| Karpenter 1.0.0 renamed or removed most metrics in one step and no release emits both spellings: `_total` suffixes on counters, `nodepool` → `nodepools`, `disruption` → `voluntary_disruption`, `provisioner_scheduling` → `scheduler`, `*_time_seconds` → `*_duration_seconds`; `karpenter_interruption_actions_performed` and `karpenter_consistency_errors` have no successor | `collectors/karpenter/pod-metrics.yaml`, `lib/karpenterctl.py:_pod_metrics` | `kubernetes-sigs/karpenter pkg/metrics/metrics.go:31-125@v0.37.0` against `:33-58@v1.0.0` and `:35-113@v1.15.1`; `aws/karpenter-provider-aws pkg/controllers/interruption/metrics.go:33-59@v0.37.0` against `:31-48@v1.0.0` | v0.37.0, v1.0.0, v1.1.0, v1.15.1 read |
| Karpenter 0.x `karpenter_nodeclaims_disrupted` carries `type` and `nodepool`, not `reason`; 1.x `karpenter_nodeclaims_terminated_total` carries no `reason` | `lib/karpenterctl.py:_pod_metrics` (label fallbacks) | `kubernetes-sigs/karpenter pkg/metrics/metrics.go:90-96@v0.37.0`; `:50-56@v1.15.1` | observed: the 0.37.0 cluster grouped every disruption under `-` until #120 |
| KEDA removed `keda_scaler_errors_total` and `keda_scaler_errors` in 2.17.0; `keda_scaled_object_errors_total` and `keda_scaler_detail_errors_total` exist since 2.14.0 | `collectors/keda/pod-metrics.yaml`, `lib/kedactl.py` | `kedacore/keda pkg/metricscollector/prommetrics.go:107@v2.16.0` present, absent at `:91-102@v2.17.0`; `:116, 134@v2.14.0` | v2.12.0 … v2.21.0 read |
| KEDA's gRPC client counters are go-grpc-middleware's with the namespace `keda_internal_metricsservice` | `lib/kedactl.py` | `kedacore/keda cmd/adapter/main.go:146@v2.17.2, :147@v2.19.0, :149@v2.21.0`; `grpc-ecosystem/go-grpc-middleware providers/prometheus/client_metrics.go:37-45@providers/prometheus/v1.0.1` | v2.17.2, v2.19.0, v2.21.0 |
| Grafana's user total is `grafana_stat_total_users` (singular); the plural spelling kubegap read until #120 never existed | `collectors/grafana/pod-metrics.yaml`, `lib/stackhealth.py:_grafana` | `grafana/grafana pkg/infra/metrics/metrics.go:465@v10.2.3, :495@v12.2.1, :490@v13.3.0` | v10.2.3, v12.2.1, v13.3.0 |
| Between 10.2.3 and 12.2.1 (the exact release was not read) Grafana added `secure_socks_ds_proxy_enabled` to `grafana_datasource_request_total`; the labels kubegap reads (`datasource`, `datasource_type`, `code`) are unchanged | `lib/stackhealth.py:_grafana` | `grafana/grafana pkg/infra/httpclient/httpclientprovider/datasource_metrics_middleware.go:21@v10.2.3, :22@v12.2.1 and @v13.3.0` | — |
| Argo CD 3.x added labels (`dry_run` on `argocd_app_sync_total` and `argocd_app_k8s_request_total`, `command` on `argocd_redis_request_total`); the labels kubegap reads are unchanged | `lib/argocdctl.py` | `argoproj/argo-cd controller/metrics/metrics.go:92-104@v2.13.0` against `:73-95@v3.6.0`; `server/metrics/metrics.go:28-33@v2.13.0` against `:30-35@v3.6.0` | v2.13.0, v3.2.0, v3.6.0 |
| cert-manager 1.21 moved the certificate gauges into a collector and renamed the ACME request label `path` to `action`; kubegap reads `status` only | `lib/certmanager.py` | `cert-manager/cert-manager pkg/metrics/metrics.go:133@v1.14.4` against `:114-123@v1.21.3`; `internal/collectors/certificate_collector.go:30-34@v1.21.3` | v1.14.4, v1.21.3 |
| The ALB controller's `aws_api_calls_total` carries `status_code` and `error_code`; the step keeps only the duration and retry histograms and derives the call count from `aws_api_call_duration_seconds_count` | step `alb-controller.controller-metrics`, `lib/albctl.py:_pod_metrics` | `kubernetes-sigs/aws-load-balancer-controller pkg/aws/metrics/instruments.go:36-39@v2.7.2`; `pkg/metrics/aws/instruments.go:46-49@v3.6.2` | v2.7.2, v3.6.2 |
| controller-runtime 0.24 adds a `controller` label to `workqueue_depth`; kubegap reads the values only | `lib/albctl.py:_pod_metrics` | `kubernetes-sigs/controller-runtime pkg/metrics/workqueue.go:41@v0.14.6` against `:21-22@v0.24.1` | — |
| A Prometheus counter vector has no series until its first increment, so a name defined in the source can be absent from a scrape | `lib/podmetrics.py:removed` (tells a removed metric from a missing series) | **unverified in source** (client_golang's `MetricVec`); observed: `grafana_datasource_request_total` absent on an idle Grafana 12.2.1, `keda_internal_metricsservice_grpc_client_*` absent on an idle KEDA 2.19.0 | — |
| The pod-metrics ports and selectors are no longer facts of this table: each step's `source:` in `collectors/<area>/collect.yaml` cites the flag default, the chart's container port name and the pod labels, and `lib/podmetrics.discover_targets` reads namespace, selector and port from the component's workload at discovery (the section for #130 and #136 below has the version boundaries: Karpenter 8000 to 8080 at 1.0.0, MetalLB 7472 to 9120 over TLS at 0.16.0) | every pod-metrics step, `lib/podmetrics.py:discover_targets` | the `source:` of each `type: pod-metrics` step in `collectors/*/collect.yaml`, e.g. `kubernetes-sigs/karpenter pkg/operator/options/options.go:216@v1.15.1` and `metallb/metallb speaker/main.go:99@v0.16.2`; `tests/test_sources.py` checks each step names its table | — |

## Log signatures (#129)

`logs.signatures` matches the patterns in `collectors/*/signatures.yaml` and `signatures/catalog.yaml` against the component pod logs (`lib/logs.py` `scan`). A pattern the component never prints never matches and the signature stays silent.

Every signature of `signatures/catalog.yaml` and `collectors/*/signatures.yaml` carries a `source:` list (one citation per alternative per version read) and has a row below; `tests/test_sources.py` requires both, and `tests/test_signature_lines.py` matches every pattern against lines built from the cited format strings (`tests/signature_lines.py`). The subsections are: the pod log formats `lib/logs.py` reads; CoreDNS; kubelet, containerd, kube-proxy, metrics-server, flannel, k3s and the `common.*` signatures; the AWS Load Balancer Controller, the EBS CSI driver, the VPC CNI, Karpenter (with the log facts `lib/scaling.py` reads) and external-dns; Argo CD, external-secrets, KEDA and cert-manager; Prometheus, Alertmanager, Grafana, Loki, promtail, Fluent Bit and Elasticsearch/OpenSearch; ingress-nginx, Traefik and MetalLB.

Three facts hold across the subsections and explain most of the corrections. (1) The two journal collectors keep only klog `E`/`W` lines and logrus or zap `warn|error|fatal` lines (`collectors/eks-platform/collect.yaml` `kubelet-journal`, `containerd-journal`; `collectors/k3s-platform/collect.yaml` `k3s-journal-errors`), plus, since this issue, two quoted kubelet `I` phrases (`"Eviction manager: `, `"Container runtime network not ready"`; `"Probe failed"` was tried and dropped, see the kubelet subsection); a phrase a daemon prints only at `I`/info level is otherwise never in the file whatever `--v` is. (2) A Kubernetes Event is not a log line: the kubelet's recorder logs events only at `--v=3` (`kubernetes/kubernetes staging/src/k8s.io/client-go/tools/record/event.go:386-389@v1.32.13`, `:392-395@v1.37.2`; `cmd/kubelet/app/server.go:566@v1.32.13` `StartStructuredLogging(3)`), controller-runtime's recorder at `V(1)` (`kubernetes-sigs/controller-runtime pkg/internal/recorder/recorder.go:102-104@v0.17.3`, `:115-117@v0.25.1`), and Karpenter never logs them (`kubernetes-sigs/karpenter pkg/events/recorder.go:86@v0.37.0`); the EKS kubelet runs `--v=2` (observed) and k3s `v=0`, so event reasons (`Evicted`, `SystemOOM`, `FailedMount`, `FailedCreatePodSandBox`, `ImageGCFailed`, `Unhealthy`, `DisruptionBlocked`, `AllocationFailed`) were removed from the log patterns and are read from `events.json` by `lib/events.py` (`TRACKED`). (3) Several components do not write the phrase to the pod log at the default level at all: the VPC CNI daemon writes to a node file, Prometheus logs per-target scrape failures at debug, Loki logs push rejections at debug and only promtail's client prints the 429 body, Alertmanager routes memberlist to debug, the EBS CSI driver demoted its AWS API error line to `V(3)` at v1.54.0; those rows say so and the signature's `meaning` says what silence means.

### Pod log formats and what `lib/logs.py` reads

`lib/logs.py` has no format classifier: `scan` applies the signature patterns to the raw line, `ERROR_RE` and `NOT_ERROR_RE` (`lib/logs.py:34-47`) decide whether an unmatched line counts as an unclassified error, `_ts_of`/`_utc_str` read the first timestamp, `_strip_prefix` removes the kubectl and journal prefixes and `normalize_unclassified` builds the key under which equal errors collapse (with `lib/controlplane.py` `_normalize_msg` and `KLOG_RE`). Every format the two clusters produce was read at the source below; `tests/test_logs.py` holds one info line and one error line built from each.

| Fact | kubegap | Source | Holds for |
|---|---|---|---|
| `kubectl logs --prefix` writes `[pod/<pod>/<container>] ` once per newline-terminated line (`prefixingWriter.Write` on each `ReadBytes('\n')` result); `--timestamps` is the kubelet's fixed-width `2006-01-02T15:04:05.000000000Z07:00`, written once per logical line even when containerd split it into partial records | PREFIX_RE, `_strip_prefix` | `kubernetes/kubernetes staging/src/k8s.io/kubectl/pkg/cmd/logs/logs.go:453,468-477,496-503@v1.32.13`, `:481,496-505,524-531@v1.37.2`; `staging/src/k8s.io/cri-client/pkg/logs/logs.go:54,59,252-253,328@v1.32.13`, `:52,57,244-245,324@v1.37.2` | v1.32.13, v1.37.2 |
| The CRI log timestamp is containerd's `time.Now()` in the node's local zone, so pods of one cluster mix `Z` and `+09:00`; Go prints `Z` only for offset 0 and `time.Parse` keeps a foreign offset. `_ts_of` keeps the offset and `_utc_str` normalises `first`/`last` to UTC | `_ts_of`, `_utc_str` (before #129 the offset was dropped and a `+09:00` line was read nine hours late) | `containerd/containerd pkg/cri/io/logger.go:39,135@v1.7.29`, `internal/cri/io/logger.go:38,134@v2.1.5`; `k3s-io/containerd internal/cri/io/logger.go:38,134@v2.1.4-k3s1`; `golang/go src/time/time.go:1343,1351@go1.24.13`, `src/time/format.go:783-800,1012@go1.24.13` | v1.7.29, v2.1.5, v2.1.4-k3s1 |
| `journalctl -o short-iso` writes `%Y-%m-%dT%H:%M:%S%z <hostname> <identifier>[<pid>]: <message>`; glibc's `%z` is `+hhmm` without a colon | `_strip_prefix` second regex; the EKS node steps | `systemd/systemd src/shared/logs-show.c:330,353,358,367,378@v219`, `:379,516,534,543,550@v249`, `:404,547,565,574,581@v252`; `bminor/glibc time/strftime_l.c:1408-1415@glibc-2.34` | v219 (Amazon Linux 2), v249 (Ubuntu 22.04), v252 (Amazon Linux 2023); the distro-to-version mapping is **unverified** (not read from a package source; the three tags print the same form) |
| The k3s journal step removes the journal prefix itself (`sed` on the `short-iso` line) so `k3s-errors-24h.log` starts at the klog header; the stats step uses the default `short` output with its own `sed`, whose hostname class now accepts `-` and capitals | `collectors/k3s-platform/collect.yaml` `k3s-journal-errors`, `k3s-journal-stats` | `logs-show.c:333,340@v219`, `:400@v249`, `:425@v252` (`%b %d %H:%M:%S`) | all three |
| klog text header: `[IWEF]mmdd hh:mm:ss.uuuuuu <pid right-aligned in 7> <file>:<line>] `; the file may carry digits (`cronjob_controllerv2.go`) or a directory with `--add_dir_header`; structured lines are `"msg" err="…" key="value"` with `strconv.Quote` on strings and errors (so `failed to "StartContainer"` is written `failed to \"StartContainer\"`) | KLOG_RE (`(\S+\.go)`), KLOG_TS_RE, `_normalize_msg` | `kubernetes/klog internal/buffer/buffer.go:32,117-155@v2.130.1`, `@v2.140.0`; `klog.go:658-665,817-821@v2.130.1`, `:682-689,840-850@v2.140.0`; `internal/serialize/keyvalues.go:256-270@v2.130.1`, `:276@v2.140.0`; `internal/severity/severity.go:39@v2.130.1` | v2.130.1 (kubernetes 1.32, 1.33), v2.140.0 (1.37) |
| klog JSON (`--logging-format=json`) has `ts` (float milliseconds), `caller`, `msg`, `err`, `v` and no level key, so neither regex can read a level from it: an info line with an error word counts as an unclassified error | documented gap; neither cluster uses it (observed) | `kubernetes/kubernetes staging/src/k8s.io/component-base/logs/json/json.go:66-73,94,103-107@v1.32.13`, same `@v1.37.2` | v1.32.13, v1.37.2 |
| go-kit logfmt: `key=value` in call order, values quoted when they hold a space, `=` or `"`; Prometheus 2.x orders `ts caller level msg` (level prepended inside the filter), Loki and promtail `level ts caller msg`; the level values are `error`, `warn`, `info`, `debug`; go-kit's JSON logger marshals a map, so MetalLB's keys come out sorted (`caller`, `error`, `level`, `msg`, `ts`) | ERROR_RE `level=(error|fatal)`, `NOT_ERROR_RE` `level=(info|debug|warn|warning)`, the JSON rules | `go-kit/log logfmt_logger.go:41-51@v0.2.1`, `log.go:24-29,52-70,151-152@v0.2.1`, `level/level.go:24-25,233-238@v0.2.1`, `json_logger.go:25,34-36@v0.2.1`, `value.go:102-104@v0.2.1`; `go-logfmt/logfmt encode.go:235-248@v0.6.0`; `prometheus/common promlog/log.go:33-36,115-126@v0.45.0`, `promlog/flag/flag.go:40-44@v0.45.0`; `grafana/loki pkg/util/log/log.go:28,46-50,72@v2.6.1`, `:44,162-164,186@v3.7.9`; `grafana/dskit log/gokit.go:43-51@8d1c6d34bb5a`; `metallb/metallb internal/logging/logging.go:58,74-79,124@v0.14.5` | go-kit v0.2.0, v0.2.1; Prometheus v2.49.1; Alertmanager v0.26.0; Loki v2.6.1, v3.7.9; MetalLB v0.14.5, v0.16.2 |
| slog text (Prometheus 3.x, Alertmanager 0.28 and later through `promslog`): `time=<RFC3339 with 3 fraction digits, local zone> level=INFO|WARN|ERROR source=<file:line> msg="…"`; `promslog` keeps slog's upper-case level and no longer forces UTC | the same rules, case-insensitive | `golang/go src/log/slog/handler.go:174-183,277-299,363-368,593-603@go1.24.13`, `src/log/slog/level.go:71-75@go1.24.13`, `src/log/slog/text_handler.go:141-160@go1.24.13`; `prometheus/common promslog/slog.go:215,224-226,236,260-283@v0.67.1` (same at v0.67.5, v0.70.1), `promslog/flag/flag.go:46-52@v0.67.1`; `prometheus/prometheus cmd/prometheus/main.go:217,351,590@v3.7.3`; `prometheus/alertmanager cmd/alertmanager/main.go:109,183,190@v0.29.0`, `:95-102@v0.34.2` | Prometheus v3.7.3, v3.15.1; Alertmanager v0.29.0, v0.34.2 |

| CoreDNS text: `[ERROR] `, `[WARNING] `, `[INFO] `, `[FATAL] ` then `plugin/<name>: ` for a plugin logger; v1.15.0 adds `-log-format json` (details in the CoreDNS subsection below) | ERROR_RE word rule (`[ERROR]` carries the word) | `coredns/coredns plugin/pkg/log/log.go:101@v1.11.3`, `plugin/pkg/log/plugin.go:15@v1.11.3`, `coremain/run.go:33@v1.15.0` | v1.11.3, v1.12.3, v1.15.0 |
| The klog header has no year; `_ts_of` fills the current year, and a month/day after today's is read as last year's (a January scan over December lines) | `_ts_of` | `kubernetes/klog internal/buffer/buffer.go:133-142@v2.130.1` | — |

Corrections found in source (the format side; the signature corrections are in each subsection):

- `_ts_of` dropped the fraction and offset of the ISO timestamp, so `_parse_iso` read a `+09:00` pod line as UTC (nine hours late in the boot-window test and in `first`/`last`); it now returns the timestamp as written and `first`/`last` are UTC.
- `KLOG_RE` refused file names with a digit, so one `cronjob_controllerv2.go` line filled the top-30 unclassified list with 21 copies; the file name is now `\S+\.go`.
- Only kubectl's timestamp was removed before counting unclassified errors; every self-timestamped format kept a per-second value in the key, so identical errors never collapsed. `normalize_unclassified` now blanks any ISO or `Y/m/d H:M:S` timestamp and the epoch float.
- Warning lines were excluded only in the JSON spelling; logfmt `level=warn`, slog `level=WARN`, logrus `level=warning`, fluent-bit `[ warn]`, nginx `[warn]`, zap console `\tINFO\t`, Elasticsearch 7 `"level": "INFO"` and ECS `"log.level"` info lines with an error word all counted as errors. One rule now covers every format read.
- nginx `[crit]`/`[alert]`/`[emerg]` and zerolog ` ERR ` lines without an error word were not counted at all; both are error levels and are now in `ERROR_RE`.
- Traefik's default console output carries ANSI colour codes, which no pattern could cross; `scan` strips them first.
- The k3s stats step's `sed` accepted only `[a-z0-9]+` as a hostname, so on hosts with `-` or capitals the journal prefix survived and the per-format counts never applied.


### CoreDNS

Versions read: v1.11.3 (EKS add-on `v1.11.3-eksbuild.1` on the EKS cluster), v1.12.3 (the image in `manifests/coredns.yaml@v1.33.5+k3s1`, the k3s cluster), v1.15.0 (newest release). Go strings were read at go1.21.0 (the `go` line of `go.mod@v1.11.3`) and go1.26.0 (`go.mod@v1.15.0`).

How a CoreDNS line looks. Text format is the default: the level as `[ERROR] `, `[WARNING] `, `[INFO] ` or `[FATAL] ` (`plugin/pkg/log/log.go:101@v1.11.3`), then `plugin/<name>: ` for a plugin logger (`plugin/pkg/log/plugin.go:15@v1.11.3`, `:15@v1.12.3`, `:16@v1.15.0`), then the message. v1.15.0 added `-log-format json` (`coremain/run.go:33@v1.15.0`); in JSON mode the plugin name goes into a `"plugin"` field (`plugin/pkg/log/json.go:105@v1.15.0`) and `plugin/<name>: ` is not in the line, so `coredns.loop` and the `plugin/errors` parts of the patterns below do not match JSON logs. Neither cluster's manifest passes the flag (`manifests/coredns.yaml:137@v1.33.5+k3s1` passes only `-conf`). The EKS add-on's arguments were observed on the cluster, not read in source: the add-on manifest is not published.

| Signature | What kubegap relies on | Source | Holds for |
|---|---|---|---|
| `coredns.servfail` | When the plugin chain returns an error with rcode 2, the `errors` plugin logs `plugin/errors: 2 <name> <type>: <error>`: the rcode is printed as a number, `%d`, and only when `err != nil` | `plugin/errors/errors.go:84-97@v1.11.3`, `:97@v1.12.3`, `:118@v1.15.0` | all three, text format |
| | Two SERVFAILs the client receives never reach that line: one the upstream resolver answered (`forward` writes the reply and returns `0, nil`) and one the `kubernetes` plugin returns before its cache has synced (`BackendError` writes the message and returns `RcodeSuccess, nil`). Without the `log` plugin they are not observable in the logs; `coredns.summary.query_log` records whether it is on | `plugin/forward/forward.go:198-199@v1.11.3`; `plugin/kubernetes/handler.go:71@v1.11.3`, `plugin/backend_lookup.go:493-501@v1.11.3` | read at v1.11.3 |
| | The `forward` plugin returns `dns.RcodeServerFailure` (2) with the upstream error, or with `no healthy proxies` when every upstream is down, so upstream failures reach the `errors` line above; the pattern leaves timeouts and unreachable upstreams to `coredns.upstream-timeout` so one outage is one finding | `plugin/forward/forward.go:202,205@v1.11.3`, `:223,226@v1.12.3`, `:294,297@v1.15.0` | all three, text format |
| | The word `SERVFAIL` appears only in the query log of the `log` plugin, whose `{rcode}` is the rcode name (`plugin/pkg/replacer/replacer.go:67`, `plugin/log/log.go:69@v1.11.3`, `:89@v1.15.0`). The `log` plugin is not in the k3s default Corefile (`manifests/coredns.yaml:55-77@v1.33.5+k3s1`). For EKS this is **unverified**: the add-on's default Corefile is not published in a source repository; a run records whether the cluster's Corefile has `log` in `coredns.summary` `config.log` (`lib/coredns.py:79`) | as listed | all three |
| `coredns.upstream-timeout` | The upstream error text reaches the `errors` line (rows above). A read deadline prints `i/o timeout` (`src/internal/poll/fd.go:60@go1.21.0`, `@go1.26.0`) inside `read udp <src>-><dst>: ` from `net.OpError` (`src/net/net.go:469@go1.21.0`, `:501@go1.26.0`); `unreachable` comes from errno 101 `network is unreachable` (`src/syscall/zerrors_linux_amd64.go:1458@go1.21.0`, `:1456@go1.26.0`) | as listed | all three |
| `coredns.loop` | The `loop` plugin logs `Loop (<src> -> <dst>) detected for zone "<zone>"` with `Fatalf`, so the process exits and the pod restarts; the line reads `[FATAL] plugin/loop: Loop (` | `plugin/loop/loop.go:53@v1.11.3`, `:53@v1.12.3`, `:52@v1.15.0` | all three, text format |
| `coredns.reload-failed` | The `reload` plugin logs `Corefile parse failed: <err>` (warning) when the new Corefile does not parse and `Corefile changed but reload failed: <err>` (error) when the restart fails; both lines start `plugin/reload: ` | `plugin/reload/reload.go:97,111@v1.11.3`, `:97,111@v1.12.3`, `:116,134@v1.15.0` | all three |
| | A directive error inside `<err>` reads `<file>:<line> - Error during parsing: <msg>`, from CoreDNS's fork of the Caddy parser | `coredns/caddy caddyfile/dispenser.go:229@v1.1.1` (`go.mod@v1.11.3`), `:229@8de985351a98` (`go.mod@v1.12.3`), `:238@v1.1.4` (`go.mod@v1.15.0`) | all three |

Notes that do not change a pattern:

- v1.15.0 can consolidate repeated errors (`errors { consolidate ... }`, `plugin/errors/errors.go:53-80@v1.15.0`), in which case the individual `plugin/errors: 2 ...` lines are replaced by a summary line. Neither default Corefile configures it.
- `Error during parsing` also appears when a pod starts with a broken Corefile; there the process exits instead of keeping the old configuration. `coredns.reload-failed` counts both.
- A timeout line also contains `plugin/errors: 2 `; `coredns.servfail` excludes `timeout` and `unreachable` lines so that one upstream outage is one finding (`coredns.upstream-timeout`), not two. `lib/logs.py` `scan` counts a line under every signature whose pattern matches it and suppresses duplicates only for `component: any` signatures.

Corrections found in source:

- `coredns.servfail` matched only ` SERVFAIL `, which only the `log` plugin prints. With the k3s default Corefile, or any Corefile without `log`, it could never match, so the signature stayed silent through upstream outages. The pattern now also matches `plugin/errors: 2 `, the line CoreDNS writes when it answers SERVFAIL itself. `tests/test_sources.py` holds lines built from the format strings above.

### kubelet (and the klog node journals)

Versions read: kubernetes v1.32.13 (the 1.32 line of the EKS cluster, which runs v1.32.9-eks), v1.33.5 (the k3s cluster, v1.33.5+k3s1, which pins `k8s.io/kubernetes v1.33.5` in `go.mod:173@v1.33.5+k3s1`) and v1.37.2 (newest release, 2026-10-28); where a phrase changed, v1.34.13, v1.35.10 and v1.36.6 were read to find the boundary. klog is v2.130.1 at 1.32/1.33 (`go.mod:109@v1.32.13`, `:106@v1.33.5`) and v2.140.0 at 1.37 (`go.mod:104@v1.37.2`), read in `vendor/k8s.io/klog/v2`; k3s replaces it with its fork `k3s-io/klog/v2 v2.120.1-k3s1` (`go.mod:58@v1.33.5+k3s1`) / `v2.140.0-k3s1` (`go.mod:57@v1.37.2+k3s1`), not read. cAdvisor is `google/cadvisor v0.51.0` (`go.mod:38@v1.32.13`), `v0.52.1` (`go.mod:35@v1.33.5`) and `google/cadvisor/lib v0.60.5` (`go.mod:32@v1.37.2`), read in `vendor/`. Go strings were read at go1.24.13 (`.go-version@v1.32.13`), go1.24.6 (`.go-version@v1.33.5`, `Dockerfile.dapper:1@v1.33.5+k3s1`) and go1.26.10 (`.go-version@v1.37.2`, `go.mod:3@v1.37.2+k3s1`).

How a kubelet line looks. klog text is the default (JSON only with `--logging-format=json`; neither cluster sets it). The header is `Lmmdd hh:mm:ss.uuuuuu <pid> <file>:<line>] ` with the severity as one character `I`/`W`/`E`/`F` and no year or zone (`vendor/k8s.io/klog/v2/internal/buffer/buffer.go:117-160@v2.130.1`). Structured calls (`klog.ErrorS`, `klog.InfoS`, `logger.Error`, `logger.Info`) write `"<msg>" key=value ...` and quote every string value with `strconv.Quote`, so a quote inside an `err=` value becomes `\"` (`vendor/k8s.io/klog/v2/internal/serialize/keyvalues.go:250-262@v2.130.1`, `:270-282@v2.140.0`); `klog.Errorf` lines are plain text after `] `. On EKS the journal collector keeps lines matching `' [EW][0-9]{4} [0-9:.]+ '` and `journalctl -o short-iso` puts `<iso> <host> kubelet[<pid>]: ` in front (`collectors/eks-platform/collect.yaml:61-66`); on k3s every embedded component (kubelet, kube-proxy, apiserver, controller-manager, scheduler, flannel) logs into the one journal, the collector strips `<host> k3s[<pid>]: ` and keeps `^[EW][0-9]{4}`, `"level":"(warn|error|fatal)"` and `level=(warning|error|fatal)` (`collectors/k3s-platform/collect.yaml:4-15`); since kubegap 0.1.78 both also keep the I lines `"Eviction manager: `, `"Container runtime network not ready"` and `"Probe failed"`. The EKS kubelet runs `--v=2` (observed in the node's bootstrap arguments, **unverified in source**: the AMI's kubelet unit is not in a repository); the k3s kubelet runs at klog `v=0` because k3s passes `-v` only when non-zero (`k3s-io/k3s pkg/daemons/agent/agent_linux.go:53-54@v1.33.5+k3s1`) and resets the flag to `cmds.LogConfig.VLevel` during the first two minutes (`pkg/daemons/executor/embed.go:55-64@v1.33.5+k3s1`). Error line: `E1104 13:20:26.123456 1234 pod_workers.go:1301] "Error syncing pod, skipping" err="failed to \"StartContainer\" for \"app\" with CrashLoopBackOff: \"back-off 5m0s restarting failed container=app pod=app_default(uid)\"" pod="default/app" podUID="uid"` — `ERROR_RE` matches it through the klog-header branch (`^\[?[^\]]*\]? ?\S* ?E\d{4} `, every optional group empty) and through the word `failed`; with the EKS prefix `<iso> <host> kubelet[1593]: ` in front the header branch still matches (`[^\]]*` runs to `kubelet[1593`, `\]?` takes the bracket, `\S*` the colon), so an EKS E line without an error word is an unclassified error unless a signature consumes it. Info line: `I1104 13:20:26.123456 1234 eviction_manager.go:387] "Eviction manager: must evict pod(s) to reclaim" resourceName="ephemeral-storage"` — `ERROR_RE` does not match (prefix `I`, no error word), `NOT_ERROR_RE` neither. `_ts_of` finds the EKS prefix with `ISO_RE` and the k3s header with `KLOG_TS_RE` (year assumed current). Events: the kubelet logs Events only through client-go's `loggerV.Info("Event occurred", ..., "reason", e.Reason, "message", e.Message)` at the verbosity passed to `StartStructuredLogging(3)` (`staging/src/k8s.io/client-go/tools/record/event.go:386,389@v1.32.13`, `:386,389@v1.33.5`, `:392,395@v1.37.2`; `cmd/kubelet/app/server.go:566@v1.32.13`, `:576@v1.33.5`, `:625@v1.37.2`), an I line at V(3) that neither cluster prints and neither collector would keep; every Event reason that was in the catalog is now read from the events summary instead.
