Notes that do not change a pattern:

- The kubelet relays containerd's CRI errors in its own E/W lines, which is how the k3s journal (no containerd lines in it: the k3s containerd log is a separate file) shows pull and sandbox failures: `"CreatePodSandbox for pod failed"` (ErrorS, `pkg/kubelet/kuberuntime/kuberuntime_manager.go:1237@v1.32.13`, `:1252@v1.33.5`, `:1747@v1.37.2`) and `<ref> Failed to pull image "<ref>": <err>` (`klog.Warning` through `logIt`, `pkg/kubelet/images/image_manager.go:127-128,181@v1.32.13`, `:292@v1.33.5`; `logger.Error`, `:139,147,369@v1.37.2`). The `FailedCreatePodSandBox` Event is `kuberuntime_manager.go:1242@v1.32.13`, `:1257@v1.33.5`, `:1752@v1.37.2`.
- `SystemOOM` and `System OOM encountered` are the reason and message of the OOM watcher's Event (`pkg/kubelet/oom/oom_watcher_linux.go:69,84,88@v1.32.13`, `:70,86,90@v1.33.5`, `:68,88,92@v1.37.2`); the only log line is `V(1) "Got sys oom event"` (`:83`, `:85`, `:87`). The signature `kubelet.system-oom` was removed: the events summary covers `SystemOOM` and `OOMKilling`.
- The node condition message `KubeletNotReady ... container runtime network not ready: NetworkReady=false ...` is an object field (`kubelet.go:3003@v1.32.13`, `:3344@v1.37.2`); 1.37's `"Node became not ready"` is `logger.Info` (`pkg/kubelet/nodestatus/setters.go:552@v1.37.2`).
- `klog.V(3)` lines that never print on either cluster: `"Evicting pod"`, `"Orphaned pod found, removing"` (`pkg/kubelet/kubelet_volumes.go:250@v1.32.13`, `:248@v1.37.2`), `"Event occurred"`.

Corrections found in source:

- `kubelet.eviction` had `eviction manager:` in lowercase while the source writes `Eviction manager:`, and its three phrases are I lines, which the collectors dropped; `Evicted pod` and `evicting pod` are not kubelet strings. The pattern is now `Eviction manager: (must evict|attempting to reclaim|pods ranked for eviction|pod failed to evict)` and the collectors keep the `"Eviction manager: ` I lines.
- `kubelet.image-gc-failed` had `failed to garbage collect required amount of images` in lowercase; the source says `Failed to ...` up to 1.34 and from 1.35 `Failed to free sufficient space by deleting unused images`; `ImageGCFailed` is an Event reason. The pattern now carries both spellings and no Event reason.
- `kubelet.runtime-network-not-ready` was an E line up to 1.35 and an I line from 1.36, so the collectors would have dropped it on 1.36+; the collectors keep that I line now and the pattern is unchanged.
- `kubelet.volume-mount-failed` had the Event reason `FailedMount`, which the kubelet never logs; dropped.
- `kubelet.probe-failed` matched `<Type> probe failed`, which is only the Event message, and `prober.go.*failed`, which only hit the V(1) `"Probe errored"` E line by accident; the signature is removed: the only log line is a V(1) I line that would flood the journal file, and the events summary already tracks the `Unhealthy` reason (`lib/events.py` TRACKED).
- `kubelet.pod-sync-error` had `failed to "StartContainer"` with plain quotes; klog writes `failed to \"StartContainer\"`. The pattern now expects the backslashes.
- `kubelet.cadvisor-partial` had `Failed to get cgroup stats` capitalised; the kubelet prints `failed to get cgroup stats for` and `cgroup stats not found for` inside the `err=` of `"Failed to get system container stats"`. The pattern now carries both lowercase phrases.
- `kubelet.system-oom` was removed (Event only, see the note above).
- `kube-proxy.iptables-restore-failed` had `Failed to sync IPVS`, which no version prints; it is now `Failed to sync (service|endpoint for service)` and `Failed to list IPVS destinations`, and the redundant `iptables-restore.*(failed|error)` (same line as the first alternative) was dropped.

### containerd

Versions read: v1.7.29 and v2.1.5 (the two EKS node images), k3s-io/containerd v2.1.4-k3s1 (the k3s node, `containerd://2.1.4-k3s1`; `go.mod:11@v1.33.5+k3s1`), v2.4.2 (newest release, 2026-10-13); for the shim message `core/runtime/v2/shim.go` was also read at v1.7.36, v2.0.0, v2.0.14, v2.1.0, v2.1.1, v2.1.2, v2.1.3, v2.1.4, v2.1.9, v2.2.0, v2.3.0 and v2.4.0. Libraries: sirupsen/logrus v1.9.3 (`go.mod` of 1.7.29, 2.1.5, 2.1.4-k3s1) and v1.10.2 (2.4.2); containerd/log v0.1.0 (1.7.29, 2.1.5; read in `vendor/`) and v0.2.0 (2.4.2, not read); containerd/ttrpc v1.2.7 (1.7.29, 2.1.x) and v1.2.9 (2.4.2); containerd/go-cni v1.1.9, v1.1.12, v1.1.14.

How a containerd line looks. logrus through `containerd/log`: `log.SetFormat(TextFormat)` installs `logrus.TextFormatter{TimestampFormat: RFC3339NanoFixed, FullTimestamp: true}`, `format = "json"` under `[debug]` in `config.toml` switches to JSON, default text (`cmd/containerd/command/main.go:361-366@v1.7.29`, `:373-378@v2.1.5`, `:365-370@v2.4.2`; `cmd/containerd/server/config/config.go:231-232@v2.1.5`; `vendor/github.com/containerd/log/context.go:143-161@v2.1.5`). The fixed keys come first, `time="<RFC3339Nano>" level=<level> msg="<msg>"`, then the fields in sorted order; values with spaces are quoted with `%q` (`sirupsen/logrus text_formatter.go:147-152,319-335@v1.9.3`, key names `formatter.go:8-10@v1.9.3`, `:21-23@v1.10.2`); levels are spelled `info`, `warning`, `error`, `fatal` (`logrus.go:67-68@v1.9.3`). On EKS `journalctl -o short-iso` adds `<iso> <host> containerd[<pid>]: ` and the collector keeps `level=(warn|error|fatal)` (`collectors/eks-platform/collect.yaml:68-73`); on k3s containerd writes `/var/lib/rancher/k3s/agent/containerd/containerd.log` directly (`k3s-io/k3s pkg/agent/config/config.go:665@v1.33.5+k3s1`, `pkg/agent/containerd/containerd.go:57-64`, level from `CONTAINERD_LOG_LEVEL` `:87-88`, default info) and the collector applies the same filter (`collectors/k3s-platform/collect.yaml:65-72`). Error line: `time="2026-11-04T10:05:50.123456789Z" level=error msg="PullImage \"docker.io/library/nginx:nope\" failed" error="rpc error: code = NotFound desc = failed to pull and unpack image \"docker.io/library/nginx:nope\": failed to resolve reference \"docker.io/library/nginx:nope\": docker.io/library/nginx:nope: not found"` — `ERROR_RE` matches on `level=error`; a `level=warning` line matches only through an error word (`error="failed to create inotify fd: too many open files"` has `failed`). Info line: `time="..." level=info msg="RunPodSandbox for &PodSandboxMetadata{...} returns sandbox id \"9f8e\""` — `NOT_ERROR_RE` matches `level=info`. `_ts_of` takes the `time="..."` value (EKS: the journal prefix first).

| Signature | What kubegap relies on | Source | Holds for |
|---|---|---|---|
| `containerd.no-space` | Go's ENOSPC text `no space left on device` inside the `error=` value of any containerd warning/error line | `golang/go src/syscall/zerrors_linux_amd64.go:1385@go1.24.13`, `@go1.24.6`, `@go1.26.10` | all |
| `containerd.image-pull-failed` | The CRI instrumented service logs `PullImage %q failed` at error level with the CRI error in `error=`; that error is `failed to pull and unpack image %q: %w` (or `failed to pull image %q: %w` for the sandbox image) wrapping the client's `failed to resolve reference %q: %w` (CRI calls `c.client.Pull`), which wraps the registry status error `unexpected status from %s request to %s: %s` (every HTTP method; 1.7 also `unexpected status code %v: %v`) | `pkg/cri/instrument/instrumented_service.go:986,1006@v1.7.29`, `pkg/cri/sbserver/image_pull.go:188,191@v1.7.29` (sbserver is the service wired at `pkg/cri/cri.go:111`), `pkg/cri/sbserver/helpers.go:265@v1.7.29`, `pull.go:183@v1.7.29`, `remotes/docker/fetcher.go:284,286@v1.7.29`, `remotes/errors/errors.go:36@v1.7.29`; `internal/cri/instrument/instrumented_service.go:354@v2.1.5` (`:356@v2.1.4-k3s1`, `:358@v2.4.2`), `internal/cri/server/images/image_pull.go:280,283,323@v2.1.5` (`:295,335@v2.1.4-k3s1`, `:290,293,345@v2.4.2`), `internal/cri/server/podsandbox/sandbox_run.go:328@v2.1.5`, `internal/cri/server/sandbox_run.go:434@v2.4.2`, `client/pull.go:188@v2.1.5`, `@v2.1.4-k3s1`, `:202@v2.4.2`, `core/remotes/errors/errors.go:43@v2.1.5`, `@v2.1.4-k3s1`, `@v2.4.2` | all, text format |
| `containerd.sandbox-failed` | `RunPodSandbox for %+v failed, error` (error level) with `failed to setup network for sandbox %q`, `failed to reserve sandbox name %q`, `failed to start sandbox %q` (1.7: `failed to start sandbox container task %q`), `failed to create containerd task` in `error=`; the kubelet's `"CreatePodSandbox for pod failed"` E line carries the same text into the k3s journal | `pkg/cri/instrument/instrumented_service.go:125,141@v1.7.29`, `pkg/cri/sbserver/sandbox_run.go:75,207,246@v1.7.29`, `pkg/cri/sbserver/container_start.go:130@v1.7.29`, `pkg/cri/sbserver/podsandbox/sandbox_run.go:223,259@v1.7.29`; `internal/cri/instrument/instrumented_service.go:73@v2.1.5` (`:75@v2.1.4-k3s1`, `:77@v2.4.2`), `internal/cri/server/sandbox_run.go:81,262,293@v2.1.5`, `@v2.1.4-k3s1`, `:86,267,319@v2.4.2`, `internal/cri/server/container_start.go:219@v2.1.5`, `@v2.1.4-k3s1`, `:148@v2.4.2`, `internal/cri/server/podsandbox/sandbox_run.go:244,279@v2.1.5`, `@v2.1.4-k3s1`, `:260,301@v2.4.2`; `kubernetes/kubernetes pkg/kubelet/kuberuntime/kuberuntime_manager.go:1237@v1.32.13`, `:1252@v1.33.5`, `:1747@v1.37.2` | all |
| `containerd.shim-error` | `cleaning up after shim disconnected` is `Warn` through v2.1.0 (and every 1.7.x, 2.0.x) and `Info` from v2.1.1; `failed to clean up after shim disconnected` is `Warn` when the cleanup errors; `failed to delete task` is `Debug` through v2.1.9 and `Error` from v2.2.0, and also a wrapped error (`failed to delete task: %w`) inside CRI error lines; `ttrpc: closed` is ttrpc's `ErrClosed`, which containerd checks with `errors.Is` and usually suppresses | `runtime/v2/shim.go:153,156,459@v1.7.29`, `:153,459@v1.7.36`, `runtime/v2/manager.go:512@v1.7.29`; `core/runtime/v2/shim.go:138,500@v2.0.0`, `:138,527@v2.0.14`, `:141,530@v2.1.0`, `:141@v2.1.1`, `:141@v2.1.2`, `:141@v2.1.3`, `:141@v2.1.4`, `:141,144,530@v2.1.5`, `@v2.1.4-k3s1`, `:141,529@v2.1.9`, `:141,530@v2.2.0`, `:143,546@v2.3.0`, `:150,649@v2.4.0`, `:150,153,649@v2.4.2`; `core/runtime/v2/task_manager.go:243@v2.1.5`, `:241@v2.1.4-k3s1`, `:329,340@v2.4.2`; `containerd/ttrpc errors.go:32@v1.2.7`, `@v1.2.9`; `shim.go:430,460,719@v1.7.29`, `:501,531,839@v2.1.5`, `:620@v2.4.2` (ErrClosed checks) | boundaries 2.1.0 to 2.1.1 (Warn to Info) and 2.1.9 to 2.2.0 (Debug to Error) |
| | `shim reaped` is the runtime-v1 linux shim at Info, removed in 2.x; the plain `shim disconnected` lines are Info at every version | `runtime/v1/linux/runtime.go:206@v1.7.29`; `runtime/v2/shim_load.go:138@v1.7.29`, `runtime/v2/manager.go:273@v1.7.29`, `core/runtime/v2/shim_load.go:161@v2.1.5`, `core/runtime/v2/shim_manager.go:291@v2.1.5`, `shim_load.go:178@v2.4.2`, `shim_manager.go:298,373@v2.4.2` | all |

Notes that do not change a pattern:

- A clean shim disconnect on containerd 2.1.1 and later (both EKS 2.1.5 node images, the k3s node, the newest release) is an Info line and is not collected; the signature then sees only `failed to clean up after shim disconnected` and `failed to delete task`. The `meaning` says so.
- The k3s journal carries no containerd lines; containerd's own text reaches it only inside kubelet `err=` values (notes under kubelet).

Corrections found in source:

- `containerd.image-pull-failed` had `unexpected status from HEAD request`, but the shared error type prints the request method (`HEAD`, `GET`, `POST`), and 1.7's `unexpected status code` form is gone in 2.x; the alternative is now `unexpected status (from \w+ request|code)[^"]*\b(401|403|404|429)\b`.
- `containerd.sandbox-failed` had the Event reason `FailedCreatePodSandBox`, which containerd never prints and the kubelet logs only as an Event; replaced by the kubelet's E line `CreatePodSandbox for pod failed`.
- `containerd.shim-error` had `shim reaped` (Info in 1.7, gone in 2.x) and `ttrpc: closed.*shim` (the words come in the other order: the `msg` names the shim before the `error=` field); both dropped, `shim.*ttrpc: closed` kept for the line where the message names the shim.

### metrics-server

Versions read: v0.8.0 (both clusters) and v0.9.1 (newest release, 2026-10-29). klog v2.130.1 / v2.140.0 through component-base logs (`cmd/metrics-server/app/start.go:26`); Go 1.24.4 (`go.mod@v0.8.0`, strings read at go1.24.6) and 1.26.10 (`go.mod@v0.9.1`).

How a metrics-server line looks. klog text (same header and quoting as the kubelet above), in the pod log behind the kubectl prefix. Error line: `[pod/metrics-server-7c8d9-abcde/metrics-server] 2026-11-04T10:05:50.123456789Z E1104 10:05:50.123456 1 scraper.go:149] "Failed to scrape node" err="Get \"https://10.0.1.5:10250/metrics/resource\": tls: failed to verify certificate: x509: certificate signed by unknown authority" node="ip-10-0-1-5"` — `ERROR_RE` matches (`Failed`, `failed`); `NOT_ERROR_RE` does not. Info lines are `klog.V(1).InfoS("Scraping metrics from nodes", ...)` and V(2)/V(4) (`pkg/scraper/scraper.go:121,143,182@v0.8.0`), so the default `--v=0` pod log is almost only E lines; a V(1) line would not match `ERROR_RE`. `_ts_of` takes the kubectl timestamp.

| Signature | What kubegap relies on | Source | Holds for |
|---|---|---|---|
| `metrics-server.scrape-failed` | `"Failed to scrape node"` and `"Failed to scrape node, timeout to access kubelet"` (ErrorS, V0) with the kubelet client error as `err=`; `x509` and `certificate` match Go's verification errors `x509: certificate signed by unknown authority` and `x509: certificate has expired or is not yet valid: ` inside it | `pkg/scraper/scraper.go:147,149@v0.8.0`, `:147,149@v0.9.1`; `golang/go src/crypto/x509/verify.go:79,152@go1.24.6`, `:79,159@go1.26.10` | both, `--v=0` |
| | v0.9.1 adds `"Failed to scrape node, node is not ready"` at V(4), not printed by default | `pkg/scraper/scraper.go:145@v0.9.1` | 0.9.1 |

Notes that do not change a pattern:

- `certificate` alone is loose; at default verbosity no info line of `pkg/scraper` contains the word (the flag descriptions in `cmd/metrics-server/app/options/kubelet_client.go:73-83` are help text, not log lines).

Corrections found in source:

- `metrics-server.scrape-failed` had `unable to fully scrape metrics` and `no metrics known for`; neither string exists at v0.8.0 or v0.9.1 (grepped `fully scrape` and `metrics known` case-insensitively in `pkg/` and `cmd/`); both dropped.

### flannel (standalone pod and the k3s built-in)

Versions read: v0.27.0 (pinned by k3s v1.33.5+k3s1, `go.mod:97`) and v0.28.10 (newest release, 2026-10-08, pinned by k3s v1.37.2+k3s1, `go.mod:92`). Neither cluster runs a flannel pod (EKS uses aws-node; k3s embeds flannel), so `logs/pods/flannel/*.log` is empty on both and only the k3s journal source is exercised.

How a flannel line looks. flannel logs with klog imported as `log` (`main.go:45@v0.27.0`, `:46@v0.28.10`, `pkg/subnet/kube/kube.go:44@v0.27.0`, `pkg/backend/vxlan/vxlan.go:67@v0.27.0`), mostly `log.Errorf`/`log.Error` (unstructured E lines: `E1104 10:05:50.123456 1234 vxlan_network.go:421] Error adding route to 10.42.1.0/24 via 192.168.0.12: network is unreachable`; `ERROR_RE` matches through `Error`); inside k3s those lines go to the k3s journal with the same header, and the k3s wrapper (`pkg/agent/flannel`) logs with logrus: `time="2026-11-04T10:05:50+09:00" level=error msg="flannel exited: failed to register flannel network: failed to acquire lease: ..."` (`ERROR_RE` matches `level=error`). Info line: `I1104 10:05:50.123456 1234 vxlan_network.go:65] watching for new subnet leases` (`log.V(0).Info`, `pkg/backend/vxlan/vxlan_network.go:65@v0.27.0`) — neither regex matches, and the collector drops it. k3s does not run flannel's `main.go`: it calls the subnet manager and backend itself and wraps their errors (`pkg/agent/flannel/flannel.go:67,85,110@v1.33.5+k3s1`, `:72,90,115@v1.37.2+k3s1`), logging the result with `logrus.Errorf("flannel exited: %v", err)` (`pkg/agent/flannel/setup.go:109@v1.33.5+k3s1`) or, from 1.37, `signals.RequestShutdown(errors.WithMessage(err, "flannel exited"))` → `logrus.Errorf("Shutdown request received: %q", e)` (`setup.go:120@v1.37.2+k3s1`, `pkg/signals/signals.go:32@v1.37.2+k3s1`).

| Signature | What kubegap relies on | Source | Holds for |
|---|---|---|---|
| `flannel.lease-failed` | Every backend returns `failed to acquire lease: %v`; standalone flannel logs it as `Error registering network: %s` and the subnet manager failure as `Failed to create SubnetManager: `; k3s wraps them as `failed to register flannel network` and `failed to create the SubnetManager` and logs `flannel exited: ...` | `pkg/backend/vxlan/vxlan.go:216@v0.27.0`, `:146@v0.28.10`, `pkg/backend/hostgw/hostgw.go:99@v0.27.0`, `@v0.28.10`; `main.go:228,359@v0.27.0`, `:251,384@v0.28.10`; `k3s-io/k3s pkg/agent/flannel/flannel.go:67,85@v1.33.5+k3s1`, `:72,90@v1.37.2+k3s1`, `pkg/agent/flannel/setup.go:109@v1.33.5+k3s1`, `:120@v1.37.2+k3s1`, `pkg/signals/signals.go:32@v1.37.2+k3s1` | both flannel versions, both k3s versions |
| | `failed to retrieve lease` and `lease expired` are printed by no version (grepped `retrieve lease`, `lease expired` case-insensitively in both trees) | — | both |
| `flannel.vxlan-error` | vxlan route failures are `Error adding route to %v via %v: %v` (file `vxlan_network.go` in the header), v0.28 adds `failed to recreate vxlan: %v`; route backends log `Error deleting route to %v: %v` and `Error adding route to %v: %s`; the iptables manager logs `Failed to setup IPTables. ...`, `Failed to setup IP6Tables. ...`, `Failed to bootstrap IPTables: %v`, `Failed to ensure iptables rules: %v`; masquerade setup fails as `Failed to setup masq rules, %v` (standalone) or k3s's `failed to setup masq rules` | `pkg/backend/vxlan/vxlan_network.go:181@v0.27.0`, `:107,421@v0.28.10`; `pkg/backend/route_network.go:120,132,154,168@v0.27.0`, `@v0.28.10`; `pkg/trafficmngr/iptables/iptables.go:236,242,251,257,366,372,379,391@v0.27.0`, `:237,243,252,258,367,373,380,393,417,430@v0.28.10`; `main.go:402@v0.27.0`, `:428@v0.28.10`; `k3s-io/k3s pkg/agent/flannel/flannel.go:110@v1.33.5+k3s1`, `:115@v1.37.2+k3s1` | both |
| | `failed to set up IPMasq` and `Failed to (add|remove) route` are printed by no version (grepped `ipmasq`, `add route`, `remove route`, `adding route`, `removing route` case-insensitively); the lowercase `failed to add route` exists only inside `fmt.Errorf` of the udp and wireguard backends | `pkg/backend/udp/udp_network_amd64.go:183@v0.27.0`, `:195@v0.28.10`; `pkg/backend/wireguard/device.go:224,238@v0.27.0`, `:232,246@v0.28.10` | both |

Notes that do not change a pattern:

- The whole `flannel.vxlan-error` pattern is case-insensitive (`(?i)` at the start) because flannel's messages begin with `Error`/`Failed` and the old lowercase alternatives only matched when the appended error text happened to contain a lowercase word.
- The iptables alternatives name flannel's own messages so that kube-proxy's `"Failed to execute iptables-restore"` E lines in the same k3s journal stay with `kube-proxy.iptables-restore-failed`.

Corrections found in source:

- `flannel.lease-failed` had only standalone flannel's wording (`Error registering network`, `Failed to create SubnetManager`), which the k3s built-in never prints; the k3s wrappers `failed to register flannel network` and `failed to create the SubnetManager` were added, and the two phrases no version prints (`failed to retrieve lease`, `lease expired`) were dropped.
- `flannel.vxlan-error` had `failed to set up IPMasq` and `Failed to (add|remove) route`, which no version prints; they are now `failed to setup masq rules` and `Error (adding|deleting) route`; `iptables.*(failed|error)` is now flannel's own `Failed to (setup|bootstrap) IP6?Tables|Failed to ensure iptables rules`; the pattern is case-insensitive.

### k3s (kine, remotedialer, embedded etcd, apimachinery proxy)

Versions read: k3s v1.33.5+k3s1 (the cluster) and v1.37.2+k3s1 (newest release, 2026-11-03). Libraries from `go.mod`: k3s-io/kine v0.14.0 (`go.mod:113@v1.33.5+k3s1`) and v0.17.1 (`:108@v1.37.2+k3s1`), plus newest kine v0.17.4 (2026-11-04); rancher/remotedialer at commits e6b68fd83a6b (`:131@v1.33.5+k3s1`) and f160aa32568d (`:129@v1.37.2+k3s1`), plus v0.6.1 (newest release); gorilla/websocket at e064f32e3674 (`:105@v1.33.5+k3s1`, `:100@v1.37.2+k3s1`); etcd server replaced by k3s-io/etcd v3.5.21-k3s1 (`go.mod:33@v1.33.5+k3s1`) and v3.7.1-k3s3 (`:31@v1.37.2+k3s1`), zap v1.17.0 (`etcd server/go.mod:40@v3.5.21-k3s1`) and v1.27.1 (`:36@v3.7.1-k3s3`); apimachinery replaced by the k3s-io/kubernetes fork `v1.33.5-k3s1` (`go.mod:42`) and `v1.37.2-k3s1` (`:40`), in which `upgradeaware.go` was read (the kubernetes/kubernetes copy at v1.33.5, v1.34.13, v1.35.10, v1.36.6, v1.37.2 for the boundary); sirupsen/logrus v1.9.3 (`:136@v1.33.5+k3s1`) and v1.10.2 (`:134@v1.37.2+k3s1`); Go go1.24.6 (`Dockerfile.dapper:1@v1.33.5+k3s1`) and go1.26.10 (`go.mod:3@v1.37.2+k3s1`).

How a k3s journal line looks. Three encoders share `logs/node/<node>/k3s-errors-24h.log`. (1) klog text from the embedded kubernetes components and flannel (see kubelet above). (2) logrus text from k3s itself, kine, remotedialer and the flannel wrapper: k3s never calls `SetFormatter` (grepped `SetFormatter` in `pkg/` and `main.go` at both tags: none), so logrus's default `TextFormatter` without a TTY writes `time="2026-11-04T10:05:50+09:00" level=warning msg="Slow SQL: ..." duration=5.21s name=list_latest` (`sirupsen/logrus text_formatter.go:117-129,147-152,200@v1.9.3`, timestamp `time.RFC3339` `formatter.go:7`); the collector keeps `level=(warning|error|fatal)`. Error line `time="..." level=error msg="Remotedialer proxy error; reconnecting..." error="websocket: close 1006 (abnormal closure): unexpected EOF" url="wss://192.168.0.10:6443/v1-k3s/connect"` — `ERROR_RE` matches `level=error`; info line `time="..." level=info msg="Database tables and indexes are up to date"` — `NOT_ERROR_RE` matches `level=info` (and the collector drops it). (3) zap JSON from the embedded etcd: k3s starts it with `Logger: "zap", LogOutputs: []string{"stderr"}` (`pkg/etcd/etcd.go:1044-1045,1111-1112@v1.33.5+k3s1`), default level `info` (`etcd server/embed/config.go:517@v3.5.21-k3s1`, `client/pkg/logutil/log_level.go:21`), JSON encoding with keys `level`, `ts`, `caller`, `msg` and lowercase levels, `ts` formatted `2006-01-02T15:04:05.000000Z0700` (`client/pkg/logutil/zap.go:46,50,51,54,57,60-62@v3.5.21-k3s1`, `@v3.7.1-k3s3`; `uber-go/zap zapcore/level.go:66@v1.17.0`, `:120@v1.27.1` spell it `warn`). Warn line `{"level":"warn","ts":"2026-11-04T10:05:50.123456+0900","caller":"etcdserver/util.go:171","msg":"apply request took too long",...}` — `ERROR_RE` does not match (`"level":"warn"` is not `error|fatal`, no error word) and `NOT_ERROR_RE` lists `"level":"(warn|warning)"`, so etcd warnings never become unclassified errors; signatures still count them because `scan` runs every pattern on every line. `_ts_of` finds `ISO_RE` inside `time="..."` and `"ts":"..."`.

| Signature | What kubegap relies on | Source | Holds for |
|---|---|---|---|
| `k3s.slow-sql` | kine logs `Slow SQL (started: %v) (total time: %v): %s` (0.14) / `Slow SQL: %s` with fields `name`, `duration`, `started` (0.17) at logrus Info when a query takes 1 s or more (`SlowSQLThreshold = time.Second`) and at Warn from 5 s (`SlowSQLWarningThreshold = 5 * time.Second`); the collector keeps only the Warn lines | `k3s-io/kine pkg/metrics/metrics.go:43-44,51,59,61@v0.14.0`, `:46,64,66@v0.17.1`, `:46,64,66@v0.17.4` | both k3s versions, queries of 5 s or more |
| | The lowercase `slow sql` exists only in a flag description | `pkg/app/app.go:100@v0.14.0`, `:131@v0.17.1` | both |
| `k3s.agent-tunnel` | remotedialer logs `Failed to connect to proxy. Empty dialer response` / `... Response status: %v - %v. Couldn't read response body (err: %v)` / `... Response body: %s` and `Remotedialer proxy error` at logrus Error with the dial error in `error=`; k3s's tunnel logs `Remotedialer proxy error; reconnecting...`; a rejected upgrade is gorilla's `websocket: bad handshake` | `rancher/remotedialer client.go:23,49,54,56@e6b68fd83a6b`, `@f160aa32568d`, `@v0.6.1`; `k3s-io/k3s pkg/agent/tunnel/tunnel.go:422@v1.33.5+k3s1`, `:403@v1.37.2+k3s1`; `gorilla/websocket client.go:24@e064f32e3674` | both |
| | `failed to dial` is printed neither by remotedialer nor by k3s (grepped both case-insensitively); in the same journal it comes from the kubelet's plugin socket dialer, the device plugin client and the etcd maintenance client | `kubernetes/kubernetes pkg/kubelet/pluginmanager/operationexecutor/operation_generator.go:193@v1.33.5`, `pkg/kubelet/cm/devicemanager/plugin/v1beta1/api.go:40@v1.33.5`; `k3s-io/etcd client/v3/maintenance.go:83@v3.5.21-k3s1` | both |
| `k3s.cert-expired` | Go's `CertificateInvalid{Reason: Expired}` prints `x509: certificate has expired or is not yet valid: ` + detail, relayed inside the `err=`/`error=` of whichever component failed the handshake (apiserver authenticator, kubelet client, k3s supervisor client) | `golang/go src/crypto/x509/verify.go:79@go1.24.13`, `@go1.24.6`, `@go1.26.10` | both |
| | k3s's own expiry monitor reports only Events (`CertificateExpirationWarning`, `CACertificateExpirationWarning`); the `k3s certificate check` command prints `certificate %s (%s) expired at %s` to its own stdout, not the journal | `k3s-io/k3s pkg/certmonitor/certmonitor.go:95,100,134@v1.33.5+k3s1`, `:95,100,133@v1.37.2+k3s1`; `pkg/cli/cert/cert.go:114@v1.33.5+k3s1`, `:112@v1.37.2+k3s1` | both |
| | `failed to get CA certs` is `getCACerts`'s wrapper for any failure to fetch `/cacerts` (server down, DNS); it reaches the journal through `Failed to validate connection to cluster at %s: %v` (error), `Failed to validate server token: %v` (warning) and `Waiting to retrieve agent configuration; server is not ready: %v` (info, dropped) | `pkg/clientaccess/token.go:380,413,428@v1.33.5+k3s1`, `:469@v1.37.2+k3s1`; `pkg/agent/run.go:357@v1.33.5+k3s1`, `:349@v1.37.2+k3s1`; `pkg/agent/config/config.go:72,112@v1.33.5+k3s1`, `:69,109@v1.37.2+k3s1` | both |
| `k3s.etcd-request-stats` | etcd logs `request stats` at Warn for expensive unary requests (Debug for all requests only at debug level), `apply request took too long` at Warn, `slow fdatasync` at Warn when the WAL sync exceeds `warnSyncDuration`; in zap JSON the message is `"msg":"request stats"` | `k3s-io/etcd server/etcdserver/api/v3rpc/interceptor.go:171-176,182,197@v3.5.21-k3s1`, `:187,203@v3.7.1-k3s3`; `server/etcdserver/util.go:171@v3.5.21-k3s1`, `server/etcdserver/txn/util.go:94@v3.7.1-k3s3`; `server/wal/wal.go:806@v3.5.21-k3s1`, `server/storage/wal/wal.go:849@v3.7.1-k3s3`; `client/pkg/logutil/zap.go:46,54,57@v3.5.21-k3s1`, `@v3.7.1-k3s3` | both, embedded etcd, JSON encoder |
| `k3s.proxy-websocket-closed` | apimachinery's upgrade-aware proxy logs `Error proxying data from client to backend: %v` / `... from backend to client: %v` with `klog.Errorf` up to 1.35 (`...: websocket: close 1006 ...`) and `logger.Error(err, "Error proxying data from client to backend")` from 1.36 (`"..." err="websocket: close 1006 ..."`) | `kubernetes/kubernetes staging/src/k8s.io/apimachinery/pkg/util/proxy/upgradeaware.go:397,427,441@v1.33.5`, `:427@v1.34.13`, `:427@v1.35.10`, `:428@v1.36.6`, `:398,428,442@v1.37.2`; `k3s-io/kubernetes staging/src/k8s.io/apimachinery/pkg/util/proxy/upgradeaware.go:397,427,441@v1.33.5-k3s1`, `:398,428,442@v1.37.2-k3s1` | both; boundary 1.35 to 1.36 |

Notes that do not change a pattern:

- kine's Info-level `Slow SQL` lines (1 s to 5 s) are not collected; `k3s.slow-sql` counts queries of 5 s or more, and its `meaning` says so.
- The etcd `request stats` Debug line (every request) would flood the journal only with `LogLevel: debug`, which k3s does not set for the embedded server (it passes `zapcore.DebugLevel` only to its own etcd *client* logger, `pkg/etcd/etcd.go:773@v1.33.5+k3s1`).

Corrections found in source:

- `k3s.slow-sql` had `slow sql` in lowercase, which only a flag description contains; the pattern is `Slow SQL`.
- `k3s.agent-tunnel` had `failed to dial`, printed by neither remotedialer nor k3s but by the kubelet's plugin dialer and the etcd client in the same journal; dropped.
- `k3s.cert-expired` had `failed to get CA certs`, a connectivity error, under a critical "certificate expired" finding; dropped. The two x509 alternatives matched the same text and were merged into `certificate has expired or is not yet valid`.
- `k3s.proxy-websocket-closed` required `: websocket` right after the message, which kubernetes 1.36 broke by making the line structured (`" err="websocket`); the pattern now accepts both forms.

### common.* (every pod log; `too many open files` and `no space left on device` also the node logs)

Versions read: Go go1.24.13 (`.go-version@v1.32.13`), go1.24.6 (`.go-version@v1.33.5`) and go1.26.10 (`.go-version@v1.37.2`), the toolchains of the kubernetes versions on the two clusters and the newest; the strings are Go runtime and standard-library constants that every Go component relays.

How a common.* line looks. These signatures scan `logs/pods/*/*.log` (every component, every encoder) and, for `common.too-many-open-files` and `common.no-space`, the three node journal files `kubelet-24h.log`, `containerd-24h.log` and `k3s-errors-24h.log` too (not every node file: on k3s the other `logs/node/<node>/*.log` files are grep subsets of the same journal and would count a line twice); a line that a component signature already consumed is not counted again under `common.*` (`lib/logs.py` `scan`, the duplicate rule). `common.panic` anchors the Go banners to the start of the container's own line after the kubectl prefix (`^(\S+ )?(\[[^\]]+\] )?\S* ?`): `[pod/app-6c9d/app] 2026-11-04T10:05:50.123456789Z panic: runtime error: invalid memory address or nil pointer dereference` matches; a logger line that mentions `panic` in prose after its own timestamp does not. `ERROR_RE` matches the panic line (`panic`) and the `fatal error:` line (`fatal`); the `goroutine 1 [running]:` line matches neither regex and is counted only by the signature.

| Signature | What kubegap relies on | Source | Holds for |
|---|---|---|---|
| `common.panic` | The runtime prints `panic: ` (then `runtime error: ` for runtime panics), `fatal error: `, the traceback header `goroutine <id> [running]` and, for fatal signals, `SIGSEGV: segmentation violation` at the start of the line | `golang/go src/runtime/panic.go:679,1096,1407@go1.24.13`, `@go1.24.6`, `:747,1224,1570@go1.26.10`; `src/runtime/error.go:82,93@go1.24.13`, `@go1.24.6`, `:92,103@go1.26.10`; `src/runtime/traceback.go:1188,1222,1231@go1.24.13`, `@go1.24.6`, `:1210,1246,1255@go1.26.10`; `src/runtime/sigtab_linux_generic.go:21` (all three); `src/runtime/signal_unix.go:841@go1.24.13`, `@go1.24.6`, `:847@go1.26.10` | all three |
| | Go never prints `segmentation fault`; glibc and bash print `Segmentation fault` for a non-Go process, which the pattern now matches with `[Ss]egmentation (fault|violation)`. **unverified in source**: the glibc/bash wording was not read in a repository for this group (the pattern accepts both capitalisations) | — | — |
| `common.too-many-open-files` | errno 24 `too many open files` | `src/syscall/zerrors_linux_amd64.go:1381` (all three) | all three |
| `common.no-space` | errno 28 `no space left on device` | `src/syscall/zerrors_linux_amd64.go:1385` (all three) | all three |
| `common.cert-expired` | `x509: certificate has expired or is not yet valid: ` + detail | `src/crypto/x509/verify.go:79` (all three) | all three |
| `common.connection-refused` | errno 111 `connection refused` | `src/syscall/zerrors_linux_amd64.go:1466` (all three) | all three |
| `common.context-deadline` | `context deadline exceeded` and the poller's `i/o timeout` | `src/context/context.go:175`, `src/internal/poll/fd.go:60` (all three) | all three |

Notes that do not change a pattern:

- The `[signal SIGSEGV: segmentation violation code=... addr=... pc=...]` line of a nil-pointer crash (`panic.go:1407@go1.24.13`) is not matched by the anchor (its first token is `[signal`); the crash is counted by its `panic: runtime error: ...` and `goroutine N [running]:` lines.
- `common.cert-expired`'s two former alternatives matched the same Go string; one remains.

Corrections found in source:

- `common.panic` had `segmentation fault`, which the Go runtime never prints (`SIGSEGV: segmentation violation`) and non-Go processes print with a capital S; the alternative is now `[Ss]egmentation (fault|violation)`.
- `common.too-many-open-files` and `common.no-space` scanned pod logs only, so the containerd warning observed on the k3s node (`failed to create inotify fd: too many open files`) could only become an unclassified error; both now also scan the three node journal files.

### AWS Load Balancer Controller

Versions read: v2.7.2 (the EKS cluster) and v3.6.2 (newest release, 2026-10-25). Libraries at the `go.mod` pins: controller-runtime v0.14.6 / v0.24.1, zap v1.24.0 / v1.28.0, go-logr/zapr v1.2.3 / v1.3.0, aws-sdk-go v1.50.8 (v2.7.2), aws-sdk-go-v2 v1.41.12 with smithy-go v1.27.1 (v3.6.2).

How an alb-controller line looks. controller-runtime's zap logger in production mode (`main.go:224@v2.7.2`, `:633@v3.6.2` pass `zap.UseDevMode(false)`), so the JSON encoder with `zap.NewProductionEncoderConfig()` (`pkg/log/zap/zap.go:82-86@v0.14.6`, `:82-86@v0.24.1`): keys `level` (lowercase, `uber-go/zap config.go:108@v1.24.0`, `:134@v1.28.0`; `zapcore/level.go:122` spells `error`), `ts`, `logger`, `msg`, written before the fields (`zapcore/json_encoder.go:365@v1.24.0`). controller-runtime overrides the time encoder to RFC3339 (`pkg/log/zap/zap.go:218-219@v0.14.6`, `:210-211@v0.24.1`), so `ts` is `2026-11-04T10:53:46Z`, not an epoch float. The error of a logr `Error` call is the `error` field (`go-logr/zapr zapr.go:263@v1.2.3`, `:254@v1.3.0`). `--log-level` takes `info` (default) or `debug` (`pkg/config/controller_config.go:16,30,110@v2.7.2`, `:20,45,176@v3.6.2`); no flag changes the encoder. An info line `{"level":"info","ts":"2026-11-04T10:53:46Z","logger":"controllers.ingress","msg":"successfully deployed model","ingressGroup":"shop/web"}` is cleared by NOT_ERROR_RE (`"level":"info"`); an error line `{"level":"error","ts":"2026-11-04T10:53:46Z","msg":"Reconciler error","controller":"ingress","error":"no certificate found for host: shop.example.com"}` is caught by ERROR_RE (`"level":"error"`). `_ts_of` takes the kubectl timestamp in front; the container's `ts` is ISO too.

| Signature | What kubegap relies on | Source | Holds for |
|---|---|---|---|
| `alb-controller.reconcile-error` | The controller passes a reconcile error through `runtime.HandleReconcileError` and controller-runtime logs it as `log.Error(err, "Reconciler error")` at ERROR; the controller's text is in the `error` field | `pkg/runtime/reconcile.go:10@v2.7.2`, `:12@v3.6.2`; `kubernetes-sigs/controller-runtime pkg/internal/controller/controller.go:329@v0.14.6`, `:494@v0.24.1` | both, default level |
| | `"level":"error"` is zap's lowercase level spelling in the production JSON encoder | `uber-go/zap config.go:108@v1.24.0`, `:134@v1.28.0`; `zapcore/level.go:122@v1.24.0`, `:122@v1.28.0` | both |
| | Requeue-type errors are logged at `V(1)` only (`requeue after`), below the default level, and build/deploy failures are also Events (`Failed build model due to %v`, `Failed deploy model due to %v`) that controller-runtime's recorder logs only at `V(1)`, so Event text is never in the pod log | `pkg/runtime/reconcile.go:17@v2.7.2`, `:20@v3.6.2`; `controllers/ingress/group_controller.go:162-173@v2.7.2`, `:236-279@v3.6.2`; `kubernetes-sigs/controller-runtime pkg/internal/recorder/recorder.go:103@v0.14.6`, `:116,128@v0.24.1` | both |
| `alb-controller.access-denied` | `AccessDenied`, `UnauthorizedOperation` and `is not authorized to perform` are AWS response text the controller never spells (only `AccessDeniedException` is compared for a metric); they reach the log inside the `error` field of `Reconciler error`, rendered `<code>: <message>` by aws-sdk-go v1 and `api error <code>: <message>` by aws-sdk-go-v2 | `pkg/metrics/aws/collector.go:78@v3.6.2`; `aws/aws-sdk-go aws/awserr/types.go:13@v1.50.8`; `aws/smithy-go errors.go:37,60@v1.27.1` | both; the message text `is not authorized to perform` is **unverified** (IAM server side) |
| `alb-controller.certificate` | Certificate discovery returns `no certificate found for host: %s`, which the model build error carries into `Reconciler error`; `CertificateNotFound` is the ELBv2 error code relayed the same way | `pkg/ingress/cert_discovery.go:89@v2.7.2`, `pkg/ingress/model_build_listener.go:181@v2.7.2`; `pkg/certs/cert_discovery.go:92@v3.6.2` | both; `CertificateNotFound` text is **unverified** (ELBv2 server side) |
| `alb-controller.throttled` | The SDK retries `Throttling` and `RequestLimitExceeded` silently (the controller sets no SDK log mode: `pkg/aws/cloud.go` has no `LogLevel`/`ClientLogMode` at either tag); only a request that fails after the retries reaches `Reconciler error` | `aws/aws-sdk-go aws/request/retryer.go:87,89@v1.50.8`; `aws/aws-sdk-go-v2 aws/retry/standard.go:61,68@v1.41.12` | both; `Rate exceeded` is **unverified** (EC2/ELB message text) |

Notes that do not change a pattern:

- The controller's own `r.logger.Error(...)` calls (16 under `controllers/`+`pkg/` at v2.7.2, 72 at v3.6.2) are also `"level":"error"` lines and may carry an AWS error in `error`; `alb-controller.reconcile-error` counts them by design.
- `warn_at: 5` of `alb-controller.throttled` is reached only when five requests failed after exhausting the SDK retries; a count of zero does not mean there was no throttling.

Corrections found in source:

- `alb-controller.reconcile-error` had `failed to reconcile`, which the controller never logs (0 hits at v2.7.2; v3.6.2 has only the Global Accelerator Event message `Failed to reconcile due to %v`, `controllers/aga/globalaccelerator_controller.go:298@v3.6.2`, and Events are not logged at the default level). Dropped.
- `alb-controller.certificate` had `unable to resolve.*certificate` and `certificate.*not found`, which no version prints (grep -i over both trees: 0 hits). The pattern is now `no certificate found for host|CertificateNotFound`.

### EBS CSI driver

Versions read: v1.37.0 (the EKS add-on; its manifest ships the sidecars external-attacher v4.7.0-eks-1-31-5 and csi-snapshotter v8.1.0-eks-1-31-5, `deploy/kubernetes/base/controller.yaml:168,197@v1.37.0`) and v1.67.2 (newest, 2026-10-26; csi-attacher v4.13.0, csi-snapshotter v8.7.0, `controller.yaml:178,209@v1.67.2`). Boundary: `pkg/cloud/handlers.go` at v1.40.0, v1.45.0, v1.50.0, v1.51.0, v1.52.0, v1.53.0, v1.54.0, v1.55.0, v1.60.0, v1.65.0. Libraries: klog v2.130.1 / v2.140.0, aws-sdk-go-v2 v1.32.4 / v1.47.3, smithy-go v1.22.0 / v1.28.5, grpc v1.68.0 / v1.84.0, external-attacher v4.7.0 / v4.13.0, external-snapshotter v8.1.0 (client-go v0.31.0, apimachinery v0.31.0) / v8.7.0 (client-go v0.37.0, apimachinery v0.37.0), kubernetes v1.32.13.

How an ebs-csi line looks. klog text in every container: the driver through the component-base logs API (`cmd/main.go:45,57,62,100@v1.37.0`, `:49,70,75,99@v1.67.2`; `--logging-format` default `text`, JSON registered as an option), the sidecars through plain klog (`kubernetes-csi/external-snapshotter cmd/csi-snapshotter/main.go:100-101@v8.1.0`). The manifest runs every container with `--v=2` (`controller.yaml:75,141,173,202,231@v1.37.0`, same at v1.67.2; chart `charts/aws-ebs-csi-driver/values.yaml:21,58@v1.37.0`). A line is `<I|W|E>mmdd hh:mm:ss.uuuuuu <pid> <file>:<line>] "<msg>" key="value"` (`kubernetes/klog internal/buffer/buffer.go:117-143@v2.130.1`, same at v2.140.0); values are `strconv.Quote`d (`internal/serialize/keyvalues.go:256@v2.130.1`, `:276@v2.140.0`) and `ErrorS` writes the error as `err=` (`klog.go:819@v2.130.1`). An info line `I1104 10:53:46.454132       1 csi_handler.go:261] "Attaching" VolumeAttachment="csi-0a1b"` has no error word, ERROR_RE does not match; an error line `E1104 10:53:46.454132       1 reflector.go:158] "Unhandled Error" err="…"` is caught by ERROR_RE (`\bError\b` and the `E\d{4} ` header after the kubectl prefix). The csi-attacher's `I… "Error processing" err="failed to attach: …"` is an I line that ERROR_RE still counts through `error`/`failed`. `_ts_of` takes the kubectl timestamp.

| Signature | What kubegap relies on | Source | Holds for |
|---|---|---|---|
| `ebs-csi.attach-failed` | The csi-attacher sidecar logs every failed attach as `logger.V(2).Info("Error processing", "err", err)` with `fmt.Errorf("failed to attach: %s", err)` around the driver's gRPC status; with `--v=2` it prints | `kubernetes-csi/external-attacher pkg/controller/csi_handler.go:243,270@v4.7.0`, `:246,273@v4.13.0`; `controller.yaml:173@v1.37.0`, `:183@v1.67.2` | both, `--v>=2` |
| | The driver's status is `status.Errorf(codes.Internal, "Could not attach volume %q to node %q: %v")` over `could not attach volume %q to node %q: %w` over the SDK error, whose text is `rpc error: code = <Code> desc = <msg>` in the sidecar; v1.67.2 maps `AttachmentLimitExceeded` to `codes.ResourceExhausted, "Attachment limit exceeded for volume %q on node %q: %v"` | `pkg/driver/controller.go:417@v1.37.0`, `:502,504@v1.67.2`; `pkg/cloud/cloud.go:957@v1.37.0`, `:1326,1328,2588@v1.67.2`; `grpc/grpc-go internal/status/status.go:202@v1.68.0`, `:202@v1.84.0` | both |
| | `VolumeInUse` and `AttachmentLimitExceeded` are EC2 error codes rendered `api error <code>: <message>`; the driver never spells `VolumeInUse` (0 hits at both tags) and checks `AttachmentLimitExceeded` only from v1.67 | `aws/smithy-go errors.go:37@v1.22.0`, `:37@v1.28.5`; `pkg/cloud/cloud.go:2588@v1.67.2` | both; the EC2 message texts are **unverified** |
| | The driver also logs every non-throttle API error itself as `klog.ErrorS(apiErr, "Error from AWS API")` up to v1.53.0; v1.54.0 demoted it to `klog.V(3).ErrorS`, below the default `--v=2` | `pkg/cloud/handlers.go:76@v1.37.0`, `:86@v1.53.0`, `:86@v1.54.0`, `:84@v1.67.2`; installed by `pkg/cloud/cloud.go:362@v1.37.0`, `:456@v1.67.2` | printed ≤ v1.53.x |
| | `timed out waiting for volume to create` is the driver's CreateVolume timeout (`fmt.Errorf("timed out waiting for volume to create: %w", err)`), returned as `Could not create volume %q: %v` and relayed by the csi-provisioner sidecar | `pkg/cloud/cloud.go:683@v1.37.0`, `:880@v1.67.2`; `pkg/driver/controller.go:342@v1.37.0`, `:419@v1.67.2` | both; **unverified**: the provisioner's surrounding wording was not read |
| | `AttachVolume.Attach failed` and `Multi-Attach error` are kube-controller-manager / kubelet strings and pod Events (`FailedAttachVolume`, in `lib/events.py` TRACKED); they never appear in an ebs-csi container | `kubernetes/kubernetes pkg/volume/util/operationexecutor/operation_generator.go:289@v1.32.13`, `pkg/volume/util/operationexecutor/operation_executor.go:377-380@v1.32.13`, `pkg/volume/util/nestedpendingoperations/nestedpendingoperations.go:329@v1.32.13`, `pkg/controller/volume/attachdetach/reconciler/reconciler.go:392,397,428,433@v1.32.13` | dropped |
| `ebs-csi.throttled` | Throttle codes are recognised through `retry.DefaultThrottleErrorCodes` and logged only at `klog.V(4).ErrorS(apiErr, "Throttle error from AWS API")`; at `--v=2` a throttle the SDK retries leaves no line, only a request that still fails carries `api error RequestLimitExceeded: …` into a sidecar error. The metric `cloudprovider_aws_api_throttled_requests_total` (`aws_ebs_csi_api_request_throttles_total` from v1.67) counts every throttle | `pkg/cloud/handlers.go:42,47,74@v1.37.0`, `:41,46,82@v1.67.2`; `pkg/metrics/constants.go:21@v1.67.2`; `aws/aws-sdk-go-v2 aws/retry/standard.go:61,68@v1.32.4`, `:70,77@v1.47.3` | both; `Rate exceeded` is **unverified** (EC2 message text) |
| `ebs-csi.snapshot-crd-missing` | client-go's reflector wraps the apiserver 404 (`the server could not find the requested resource`) as `failed to list %v: %w` and, on the watch side, `Failed to watch` → `utilruntime.HandleError` → `"Unhandled Error" err="…"`; client-go v0.31.0 also prints a `W` line `failed to list %v: %v` per retry, v0.37.0 does not | `kubernetes/client-go tools/cache/reflector.go:158,561,562@v0.31.0`, `:227,747@v0.37.0`; `kubernetes/apimachinery pkg/util/runtime/runtime.go:156@v0.31.0`, `:203,228@v0.37.0`; `pkg/api/errors/errors.go:448@v0.31.0`, `:450@v0.37.0` | both sidecars; two lines per retry on v8.1.0, one on v8.7.0 |
| | The csi-snapshotter sidecar watches `VolumeSnapshotContents` and `VolumeSnapshotClasses`, so both type names occur; plain `*v1.VolumeSnapshot` is the snapshot-controller's informer (not in this pod) | `kubernetes-csi/external-snapshotter cmd/csi-snapshotter/main.go:254,255@v8.1.0`, `:298,299@v8.7.0` | both |

Notes that do not change a pattern:

- The observed EKS line `E1104 10:53:46.454132 1 reflector.go:158] "Unhandled Error" err="k8s.io/client-go@v0.31.0/tools/cache/reflector.go:243: Failed to watch *v1.VolumeSnapshotClass: failed to list *v1.VolumeSnapshotClass: the server could not find the requested resource"` is `reflector.go:158,562@v0.31.0` with `runtime.go:156@v0.31.0`; with client-go v0.37.0 the same failure reads `E… reflector.go:227] "Failed to watch" err="failed to list *v1.VolumeSnapshotClass: …" reflector="…" type="*v1.VolumeSnapshotClass"` and the pattern still matches inside `err=`.
- The csi-attacher records no Event for a failed attach (`grep Event( pkg/controller` at v4.7.0: 0); it saves the error into `VolumeAttachment.status.attachError` (`csi_handler.go:264@v4.7.0`).

Corrections found in source:

- `ebs-csi.attach-failed` matched `AttachVolume.Attach failed` and `Multi-Attach error`, which only kube-controller-manager / kubelet print (`operation_generator.go:289`, `reconciler.go:392-433@v1.32.13`) and which are pod Events; in the ebs-csi pod log they were silent. The pattern now matches the csi-attacher's `failed to attach: rpc error` line, the EC2 codes `VolumeInUse` and `AttachmentLimitExceeded` inside it, and the driver's `timed out waiting for volume to create`.
- `ebs-csi.attach-failed` also relied on the driver's own `Error from AWS API` line for the EC2 codes; that line is unconditional only up to v1.53.0 and `V(3)` from v1.54.0 (`pkg/cloud/handlers.go:86@v1.53.0`, `:86@v1.54.0`), below the manifest's `--v=2`, so from v1.54 only the csi-attacher line carries the code.
- `ebs-csi.throttled` could never fire on a retried throttle: the driver logs throttle codes at `V(4)` only (`handlers.go:74@v1.37.0`, `:82@v1.67.2`). The pattern stays, the meaning now says it fires only after exhausted retries and that the metric is the signal.
- `ebs-csi.attach-failed`'s `timed out waiting for volume` is a CreateVolume timeout (`cloud.go:683@v1.37.0`); the alternative is now the full text and the meaning says what it is.

### VPC CNI (aws-node)

Versions read: v1.19.0 (the EKS add-on) and v1.23.2 (newest, 2026-09-30). Libraries: zap v1.26.0 / v1.28.0, logrus v1.9.3 / v1.9.4, aws-sdk-go v1.55.5 (v1.19.0), aws-sdk-go-v2 v1.47.0 with smithy-go v1.28.1 (v1.23.2).

How an aws-node line looks. The container runs the Go entrypoint `cmd/aws-vpc-cni/main.go`, which logs with logrus (`main.go:46@v1.19.0`, `:27@v1.23.2`, no `SetFormatter`): `time="2026-11-04T10:53:46Z" level=info msg="Starting IPAM daemon... "` (`sirupsen/logrus text_formatter.go:150@v1.9.3`, `:150@v1.9.4`; `formatter.go:7@v1.9.3` RFC3339; level words `logrus.go:69@v1.9.3`, `:71@v1.9.4`). It starts ipamd as a child with stdout/stderr wired to the container (`main.go:435-437@v1.19.0`, `:437-439@v1.23.2`), but ipamd's logger writes zap JSON to the file named by `AWS_VPC_K8S_CNI_LOG_FILE`, default `/host/var/log/aws-routed-eni/ipamd.log` (`pkg/utils/logger/config.go:21@v1.19.0`, `:9@v1.23.2`; lumberjack writer `zaplogger.go:131-153@v1.19.0`, `:140-162@v1.23.2`), and to stdout only when the env is literally `stdout` (`zaplogger.go:136-137@v1.19.0`, `:145-146@v1.23.2`); the chart and manifest set the file path (`charts/aws-vpc-cni/values.yaml:72@v1.19.0`, `:80@v1.23.2`; `config/master/aws-k8s-cni.yaml:478@v1.19.0`). The CNI plugin binary logs to `AWS_VPC_K8S_PLUGIN_LOG_FILE`, default `/var/log/aws-routed-eni/plugin.log` (`cmd/aws-vpc-cni/main.go:72@v1.19.0`, `:72@v1.23.2`; `cmd/routed-eni-cni-plugin/cni.go:115-119@v1.19.0`). ipamd's JSON uses `zap.NewProductionEncoderConfig()` with ISO8601 time (`zaplogger.go:101-103@v1.19.0`, `:103-105@v1.23.2`; keys `level`/`ts`/`caller`/`msg`, `uber-go/zap config.go:126-134@v1.26.0`, `:126-134@v1.28.0`, lowercase level `zapcore/level.go:122`), caller enabled (`zaplogger.go:118@v1.19.0`, `:121@v1.23.2`), default level `Debug` (`config.go:10`; chart `DEBUG`). An entrypoint info line is cleared by NOT_ERROR_RE (`level=info`); `time="…" level=error msg="Timed out waiting for IPAM daemon to start"` is caught by ERROR_RE. An ipamd line `{"level":"error","ts":"2026-11-04T10:53:46.454Z","caller":"ipamd/ipamd.go:867","msg":"Failed to increase pool size due to not able to allocate ENI …"}` is caught by ERROR_RE; a debug line with `failed` in `msg` is cleared by NOT_ERROR_RE (`"level":"debug"`). `_ts_of` takes the kubectl timestamp.

| Signature | What kubegap relies on | Source | Holds for |
|---|---|---|---|
| `vpc-cni.ip-assign-failed` | ipamd logs `Failed to allocate %d IP addresses on an ENI: %v` (warn) and `Failed to increase pool size due to not able to allocate ENI %v` (error) when the ENI/IP allocation fails, then `Unable to attach IPs/Prefixes for the ENI, subnet doesn't seem to have enough IPs/Prefixes. …` when the EC2 code is `InsufficientFreeAddressesInSubnet` or `InsufficientCidrBlocks` | `pkg/ipamd/ipamd.go:182,314,867,869,872@v1.19.0`, `:185,376,1079,1081,1084@v1.23.2` | both, only in ipamd.log unless `AWS_VPC_K8S_CNI_LOG_FILE=stdout` |
| | The datastore logs `DataStore has no available IP/Prefix addresses` (error) and returns `AssignPodIPv4Address: no available IP/Prefix addresses` (v1.19.0), `AssignPodIPv6Address: no available IP addresses` (v1.19.0 IPv6), `AssignPodIPAddress: no available IP/Prefix addresses` (v1.23.2, logged as `Failed to assign IPs from network card %d: %v`, warn) | `pkg/ipamd/datastore/data_store.go:664,734,735@v1.19.0`, `:83,858@v1.23.2`; `pkg/ipamd/rpc_handler.go:233@v1.23.2` | both |
| | `Failed to allocate IPv6 Prefixes on ENI %v: %v` exists at v1.19.0 only (v1.23.2 wraps it lowercase inside an error) | `pkg/awsutils/awsutils.go:1726@v1.19.0`, `:2211@v1.23.2` | v1.19.0 |
| | The CNI plugin logs `Failed to assign an IP address to container %s` (matched by the first alternative when plugin.log is read) and returns `add cmd: failed to assign an IP address to container` to the kubelet, where it appears in `FailedCreatePodSandBox` events | `cmd/routed-eni-cni-plugin/cni.go:183,185@v1.19.0`, `:219,221@v1.23.2` | both, plugin.log / kubelet |
| `vpc-cni.ec2-throttled` | ipamd never spells the throttle codes (grep -i `throttl|ratelimit|LimitExceeded` in `pkg/awsutils`, `pkg/ec2wrapper`: 0 hits); the SDK retries them and a request that still fails is relayed in the `%v` of an ipamd `log.Errorf` line such as `Failed to CreateNetworkInterface %v for subnet %s` | `aws/aws-sdk-go aws/request/retryer.go:87,89@v1.55.5`, `aws/awserr/types.go:13@v1.55.5`; `aws/aws-sdk-go-v2 aws/retry/standard.go:70,77@v1.47.0`, `aws/smithy-go errors.go:37@v1.28.1`; `pkg/awsutils/awsutils.go:1025@v1.19.0`, `:1452@v1.23.2` | both, ipamd.log only; `Rate exceeded` is **unverified** |
| `vpc-cni.ipamd-error` | `level=error` is the logrus spelling of the entrypoint's `log.Errorf` lines, the only lines in the pod log by default | `cmd/aws-vpc-cni/main.go:27,447@v1.19.0`, `:27,449@v1.23.2`; `sirupsen/logrus text_formatter.go:150@v1.9.3`, `:150@v1.9.4`; `logrus.go:69@v1.9.3`, `:71@v1.9.4` | both |
| | ipamd's error lines are `"level":"error"` JSON with `"caller":"ipamd/<file>:<line>"`; the alternative is anchored on both because the default level is Debug and debug/info messages also contain `failed` | `pkg/utils/logger/zaplogger.go:101,118@v1.19.0`, `:103,121@v1.23.2`; `uber-go/zap config.go:127,129,134@v1.26.0`, `:127,129,134@v1.28.0`; `zapcore/level.go:122@v1.26.0`, `:122@v1.28.0` | both, ipamd.log |
| | ENI failures: `Failed to attach ENI %s: %v` (error), `IP pool reconcile: Failed to delete ENI during reconcile: %v` (error), `Failed to CreateNetworkInterface %v for subnet %s` (error); the create path's own text is the lowercase wrapped error `AllocENI: failed to create ENI` | `pkg/awsutils/awsutils.go:797,860,1025@v1.19.0`, `:1046,1114,1452@v1.23.2`; `pkg/ipamd/ipamd.go:1442@v1.19.0`, `:1728@v1.23.2` | both, ipamd.log |

Notes that do not change a pattern:

- The three signatures keep `logs/pods/vpc-cni/*.log` as source and their meanings say that ipamd lines reach the pod log only with `AWS_VPC_K8S_CNI_LOG_FILE=stdout`; on the default add-on configuration a count of zero is not evidence of health. Reading `/var/log/aws-routed-eni/ipamd.log` from the node is a collector change, not a signature change.
- `InsufficientFreeAddressesInSubnet` is compared as an EC2 error code (`containsInsufficientCIDRsOrSubnetIPs`, `ipamd.go:314@v1.19.0`, `:376@v1.23.2`) and reaches a line only inside the relayed error text.

Corrections found in source:

- All three vpc-cni signatures were read against the aws-node pod log, which holds only the entrypoint's logrus lines; every ipamd and plugin alternative was permanently silent (`pkg/utils/logger/config.go:21@v1.19.0`, `zaplogger.go:136@v1.19.0`, `cmd/aws-vpc-cni/main.go:72@v1.19.0`). The meanings now state the condition.
- `datastore has no available IP` never matched: the source is `DataStore has no available IP/Prefix addresses` (`data_store.go:734@v1.19.0`, `:858@v1.23.2`). Now `DataStore has no available IP`.
- `no available IP addresses` matched only the v1.19.0 IPv6 path; the IPv4 and v1.23.2 texts have `/Prefix` (`data_store.go:735@v1.19.0`, `:83@v1.23.2`). Now `no available IP(/Prefix)? addresses`.
- `Failed to (assign|allocate) (an )?IP` missed `Failed to allocate %d IP addresses on an ENI` (`ipamd.go:869@v1.19.0`, `:1081@v1.23.2`); now `[Ff]ailed to (assign|allocate) (an |\d+ )?IP`, which also covers the plugin's `Failed to assign an IP address to container %s` (`cni.go:183@v1.19.0`) and the kubelet-side lowercase form, so the separate `failed to assign an IP address to container` alternative is gone. `Unable to attach IPs/Prefixes for the ENI` (`ipamd.go:872@v1.19.0`, `:1084@v1.23.2`) is added as ipamd's own exhaustion line.
- `Failed to (create|attach|delete) ENI` missed the create path, whose text is lowercase inside a wrapped error (`awsutils.go:797@v1.19.0`, `:1046@v1.23.2`) and whose log line is `Failed to CreateNetworkInterface %v for subnet %s` (`:1025@v1.19.0`, `:1452@v1.23.2`). Now `[Ff]ailed to (create|attach|delete) ENI|Failed to CreateNetworkInterface`.
- `ipamd.*(error|failed)` matched any ipamd JSON line with `failed` in `msg`, including debug and info lines (default level Debug, `config.go:10@v1.19.0`). Now `"level":"error".*"caller":"ipamd/`.

### Karpenter

Versions read: kubernetes-sigs/karpenter and aws/karpenter-provider-aws at v0.37.0 (the EKS cluster runs `public.ecr.aws/karpenter/controller:0.37.0`), v1.0.0 and v1.15.1 (newest, 2026-10-23); the provider pins the core at the same tag (`go.mod` of each provider tag). Boundaries read: provider `pkg/providers/version/version.go` at every minor v1.1.0 … v1.14.0; core `pkg/controllers/disruption/controller.go` and `queue.go` at v1.1.0 … v1.14.0; core `pkg/controllers/node/termination/terminator/eviction.go` at v1.1.0 … v1.14.0. Libraries: zap v1.27.0 / v1.28.0, go-logr/zapr v1.3.0, controller-runtime v0.18.2 (0.37.0) / v0.18.4 (1.0.0) / v0.24.1 (1.15.1), klog v2.120.1 / v2.130.1 / v2.140.0, awslabs/operatorpkg 5e8ed3699dea (1.15.1), aws-sdk-go v1.53.6 (0.37.0) / v1.55.5 (1.0.0), aws-sdk-go-v2 v1.47.1 with smithy-go v1.28.2 (1.15.1).

How a karpenter line looks. zap JSON with Karpenter's own encoder config at all three versions: `MessageKey "message"`, `LevelKey "level"` with `CapitalLevelEncoder` (`INFO`, `ERROR`), `TimeKey "time"` with `ISO8601TimeEncoder`, `NameKey "logger"`, no caller unless `--log-level=debug`, sampling 100/100 (`pkg/operator/logging/logging.go:54-80@v0.37.0`, `:46-73@v1.0.0`, `:49-76@v1.15.1`; `uber-go/zap zapcore/level.go:146@v1.27.0` spells `ERROR`); the logger is named `controller` (`logging.go:103@v0.37.0`, `pkg/operator/operator.go:137@v0.37.0`, `:137@v1.0.0`, `:155@v1.15.1`; `logging.go:80@v1.15.1`) and carries `commit` (`logging.go:99@v0.37.0`, `:90@v1.15.1`). It is wrapped by zapr (`error` field, `go-logr/zapr zapr.go:207,254@v1.3.0`) and set as the controller-runtime and klog logger (`operator.go:138-139@v0.37.0`). `level`, `time`, `logger`, `message` are written before the fields (`uber-go/zap zapcore/json_encoder.go:361@v1.27.0`, `:361@v1.28.0`). `--log-level` default `info` (`charts/karpenter/values.yaml:149@v0.37.0`, `:157@v1.0.0`, `:172@v1.15.1`); 0.37.0 could also take a mounted zap config file (`logging.go:106-125@v0.37.0`, gone at v1.15.1). The controller-runtime builder adds `<Kind>: {"name": …}` to every reconcile line (`kubernetes-sigs/controller-runtime pkg/builder/controller.go:408@v0.18.2`, `:408@v0.18.4`, `:449@v0.24.1`; `kubernetes/klog k8s_references.go:29-31,62@v2.120.1`, same at v2.140.0). An info line `{"level":"INFO","time":"2026-11-04T00:30:10.620Z","logger":"controller","message":"launched nodeclaim","commit":"490ef94",…}` is cleared by NOT_ERROR_RE (`"level":"info"`, case-insensitive); an error line `{"level":"ERROR",…,"message":"failed launching nodeclaim",…,"error":"insufficient capacity, with fleet error(s), InsufficientInstanceCapacity: …"}` is caught by ERROR_RE (`"level":"(error|fatal)"` under its `(?i)`). `_ts_of` takes the kubectl timestamp; `time` is ISO too.

| Signature | What kubegap relies on | Source | Holds for |
|---|---|---|---|
| `karpenter.insufficient-capacity` | When every CreateFleet error is an unfulfillable-capacity code (`InsufficientInstanceCapacity`, `MaxSpotInstanceCountExceeded`, `VcpuLimitExceeded`, `UnfulfillableCapacity`, …), the provider renders each as `<Code>: <Message>`, wraps `with fleet error(s), %w` into `NewInsufficientCapacityError` (`insufficient capacity, %s`), and the lifecycle controller logs `failed launching nodeclaim` at ERROR with that text in `error` | provider `pkg/errors/errors.go:45@v0.37.0`, `:45@v1.0.0`, `:59@v1.15.1`; `pkg/providers/instance/instance.go:499,507@v0.37.0`, `:499,507@v1.0.0`, `:871,881@v1.15.1`; core `pkg/cloudprovider/types.go:309@v0.37.0`, `:341@v1.0.0`, `:780@v1.15.1`; `pkg/controllers/nodeclaim/lifecycle/launch.go:86@v0.37.0`, `:79@v1.0.0`, `:99@v1.15.1` | all three, default level |
| | The scheduler logs `.Error(err, "could not schedule pod")` at ERROR; the reason (`incompatible with nodepool %q, …` and `no instance type satisfied resources …`, reworded `no instance type met/satisfies/has …` by v1.15.1, `incompatible with nodepool` gone there) is in its `error` field, so the message alone counts the line | `pkg/controllers/provisioning/scheduling/scheduler.go:108,284@v0.37.0`, `:110,286@v1.0.0`, `:331@v1.15.1`; `scheduling/nodeclaim.go:108@v0.37.0`, `:109@v1.0.0`, `:251,551,567,570@v1.15.1` | all three |
| `karpenter.version-unsupported` | ≤ v1.0.x: the provider logs `failed validating kubernetes version` (ERROR) with `karpenter version is not compatible with K8s version %s` when the cluster minor is outside `MinK8sVersion..MaxK8sVersion` (1.30 at both tags, so a 1.32 cluster trips it); from v1.1.0 the validation error is returned as `validating kubernetes version, %w`, wrapped `updating version, %w` by the version controller and logged by controller-runtime as `Reconciler error`; v1.15.1 words it `karpenter is not compatible with kubernetes version (version=X)` through operatorpkg's structured error | `pkg/providers/version/version.go:36,72,85@v0.37.0`, `:38,74,100@v1.0.0`, `:78,116@v1.1.0`, `:44,79,99,125@v1.15.1`; `pkg/controllers/providers/version/controller.go:45@v1.1.0`, `:49@v1.15.1`; `awslabs/operatorpkg serrors/serrors.go:33@5e8ed3699dea`; `kubernetes-sigs/controller-runtime pkg/internal/controller/controller.go:494@v0.24.1` | all three; boundary v1.1.0 |
| `karpenter.launch-failed` | `failed launching nodeclaim` is logged only for insufficient-capacity (and from v1.0.0 NodeClass-not-ready) errors; any other launch error is returned as `launching nodeclaim, %w` over the provider's `creating instance, %w` (v1.15.1: `creating nodeclaim, %s`) and logged by controller-runtime as `Reconciler error`, so the `error` field starts `launching nodeclaim, creating …` and carries the EC2 code | core `launch.go:86,100,103@v0.37.0`, `:79,91,103@v1.0.0`, `:99,113,132@v1.15.1`; `pkg/cloudprovider/types.go:838@v1.15.1`; provider `pkg/cloudprovider/cloudprovider.go:101@v0.37.0`, `:120@v1.0.0`, `:170,176@v1.15.1`; `instance.go:499,507,509@v0.37.0`, `:499,507,509@v1.0.0`, `:871,881@v1.15.1`; controller-runtime `controller.go:324@v0.18.2`, `:324@v0.18.4`, `:494@v0.24.1` | all three |
| | `VcpuLimitExceeded` is in the unfulfillable-capacity set at every version (so it takes the `failed launching nodeclaim` path); `UnauthorizedOperation`, `AccessDenied`, `InvalidParameter` are EC2 codes the provider checks only at v1.15.1 (condition reasons) and otherwise relays in the SDK error text | `pkg/errors/errors.go:47@v0.37.0`, `:47@v1.0.0`, `:28,30,61,243,255@v1.15.1`; `aws/aws-sdk-go aws/awserr/types.go:13@v1.53.6`, `:13@v1.55.5`; `aws/smithy-go errors.go:37@v1.28.2` | all three; the EC2 message texts are **unverified** |
| `karpenter.drain-failed` | Up to v1.5.x the eviction queue logs `failed evicting pod` (ERROR) for an eviction API error that is not 404/409 and not a PDB 429; from v1.6.0 the error is returned instead (reaches `Reconciler error` with the API text). A non-drain error of the termination reconcile is returned as `draining node, %w` at every version (`Reconciler error`, `error` field) | `pkg/controllers/node/termination/terminator/eviction.go:182@v0.37.0`, `:195@v1.0.0`, `:209@v1.5.0`, `:303,317@v1.15.1`; `pkg/controllers/node/termination/controller.go:89@v0.37.0`, `:108@v1.0.0`, `:209@v1.15.1` | `failed evicting pod` ≤ v1.5.x; `draining node,` all three |
| | A PDB block (apiserver 429 `Cannot evict pod as it would violate the pod's disruption budget.`) is never logged: Karpenter catches `IsTooManyRequests` and publishes the `FailedDraining` Event `Failed to drain node, evicting pod … violates a PDB` | `kubernetes/kubernetes pkg/registry/core/pod/storage/eviction.go:418,436@v1.32.13`; `eviction.go:176-180@v0.37.0`, `:189-193@v1.0.0`, `:303-314@v1.15.1`; `terminator/events/events.go:41-42@v0.37.0`, `:57-58@v1.15.1` | Event only |
| `karpenter.spot-interruption` | The interruption controller sets `messageKind` to the queue message kind and logs `initiating delete from interruption message` (INFO) when it deletes the NodeClaim; the kind is `SpotInterruptionKind` at 0.37.x and `spot_interrupted` from 1.0.0 (`capacity_reservation_interrupted` added at 1.15.1); a rebalance recommendation is `NoAction` at every version (Event only), state changes take the same delete line under `StateChangeKind` / `instance_terminated` / `instance_stopped` | provider `pkg/controllers/interruption/controller.go:148,219,297@v0.37.0`, `:151,216@v1.0.0`; `pkg/controllers/interruption/utils.go:63,164,219@v1.15.1`; `messages/types.go:40@v0.37.0`, `:40@v1.0.0`, `:40,43@v1.15.1`; message before fields `uber-go/zap zapcore/json_encoder.go:365@v1.24.0`, `:361@v1.27.0`, `:361@v1.28.0` | all three, default level |
| | `Spot interruption warning was triggered` and `Spot rebalance recommendation was triggered` are Event messages | `pkg/controllers/interruption/events/events.go:29,37,49,57@v0.37.0`, `:29,37,69,77@v1.15.1` | Event only |
| | Karpenter never logs Events: its recorder calls `r.rec.Event` only, and the controller-runtime event logger (`V(1)`) is wrapped by `IgnoreDebugEvents`, so `DisruptionBlocked`, `Cannot disrupt …`, `pdb prevents pod evictions`, the `do-not-disrupt` texts, `Evicted`, `FailedDraining` are Events/conditions only | core `pkg/events/recorder.go:86@v0.37.0`, `:86@v1.0.0`, `:86@v1.15.1`; `pkg/operator/logging/logging.go:156-158@v0.37.0`; `operator.go:145@v0.37.0`, `:153@v1.0.0`, `:188@v1.15.1`; `pkg/controllers/disruption/events/events.go:110-128@v0.37.0`, `:100-132@v1.0.0`, `:130-162@v1.15.1`; `pkg/controllers/state/statenode.go:344@v1.15.1`; `kubernetes-sigs/controller-runtime pkg/internal/recorder/recorder.go:104@v0.18.2`, `:128@v0.24.1` | all three |

Notes that do not change a pattern:

- `disruption budget` appears in a log line only as `detected disruption budget errors` (ERROR, an invalid budget spec in a NodePool: `pkg/controllers/disruption/controller.go:294-298@v0.37.0`, `:279-283@v1.0.0`, `:279-285@v1.15.1`) and, at v1.15.1, `disruption budget already exhausted; clamping to 0` at `V(1)` (`helpers.go:356-361@v1.15.1`).
- The observed `karpenter.version-unsupported` hit on the EKS cluster (0.37.0 on Kubernetes 1.32) is `version.go:72@v0.37.0` with `MaxK8sVersion = "1.30"` (`:36`).
- At v1.15.1 operatorpkg's `serrors.NewLogger` lifts the structured values of a wrapped error into log fields (`serrors/logger.go:29-31@5e8ed3699dea`); the `error` text itself keeps its `(key=value)` suffix (`serrors/serrors.go:25-33`).

Corrections found in source:

- `karpenter.insufficient-capacity` had `no instance type satisfied` and `incompatible with nodepool`: both occur only inside the `error` field of the same `could not schedule pod` line (`scheduler.go:108,284@v0.37.0`, `nodeclaim.go:108@v0.37.0`), the first was reworded by v1.15.1 (`nodeclaim.go:251,551-570@v1.15.1`) and the second is gone there (0 hits). Both dropped; `could not schedule pod` counts the line once at every version.
- `karpenter.version-unsupported` had `unsupported kubernetes version` and `kubernetes version .* is not supported`, which no version prints, and `failed validating kubernetes version`, which stopped at v1.1.0 (`version.go:78@v1.1.0` returns the error instead). The pattern now also matches `validating kubernetes version, karpenter( version)? is not compatible with (K8s|kubernetes) version` (`version.go:116@v1.1.0`, `:125@v1.15.1`).
- `karpenter.launch-failed` had `creating instance.*(error|failed)` and `launching.*error`, which needed a trailing `error`/`failed` word that EC2 messages do not contain; replaced by `"error":"launching nodeclaim, ` (`launch.go:100,103@v0.37.0`, `:132@v1.15.1`), the prefix of every non-capacity launch error in `Reconciler error`.
- `karpenter.disruption-blocked` is removed: all five alternatives were Event messages or condition errors (`disruption/events/events.go:110-128@v0.37.0`, `statenode.go:344@v1.15.1`) that Karpenter never logs; `lib/events.py` TRACKED covers `DisruptionBlocked`.
- `karpenter.drain-failed` had `failed to drain` (the `FailedDraining` Event message `Failed to drain node, %s`), `cannot evict pod` (nowhere), `eviction.*(failed|blocked)` (nothing in that order) and the apiserver's PDB text, which Karpenter turns into an Event (`eviction.go:176-180@v0.37.0`). The pattern is now `failed evicting pod|"error":"draining node, ` (`eviction.go:182@v0.37.0`, `:209@v1.5.0`; `termination/controller.go:89@v0.37.0`, `:209@v1.15.1`).
- `karpenter.spot-interruption` had lowercase `spot interruption`, `interruption (warning|notice)`, `rebalance recommendation` (Event messages with capitals only) and `instance terminating` (nowhere). The pattern is now the `initiating delete from interruption message` line with `messageKind` `SpotInterruptionKind` / `spot_interrupted` / `capacity_reservation_interrupted` (`controller.go:148,219@v0.37.0`, `messages/types.go:40@v0.37.0`, `:40@v1.0.0`, `utils.go:63,164@v1.15.1`, `types.go:43@v1.15.1`); rebalance recommendations never produce the line (`controller.go:297@v0.37.0`, `utils.go:219@v1.15.1`).

### lib/scaling.py Karpenter log facts

`lib/scaling.py` (`disruption_line`, `launch_failure_key`) reads the Karpenter pod log with three regexes after requiring `"logger":"controller"` in the line (true at every version: `logging.go:103@v0.37.0`, `operator.go:137@v0.37.0`, `:137@v1.0.0`, `:155@v1.15.1`, `logging.go:80@v1.15.1`).

| Regex | What it relies on | Source | Holds for |
|---|---|---|---|
| `"message":"(launched|registered|initialized) nodeclaim".*?"NodeClaim":\{"name":"([^"]+)"` | The lifecycle controller logs `launched nodeclaim`, `registered nodeclaim`, `initialized nodeclaim` at INFO; the `NodeClaim` key is the Kind that controller-runtime's default LogConstructor adds (`log.WithValues(gvk.Kind, klog.KRef(req.Namespace, req.Name))`), its value a `klog.ObjectRef` with `json:"name"` and `namespace,omitempty`, so it renders `"NodeClaim":{"name":"…"}`; `message` is written before the context fields | `pkg/controllers/nodeclaim/lifecycle/launch.go:111@v0.37.0`, `:111@v1.0.0`, `:140@v1.15.1`; `registration.go:68@v0.37.0`, `:77@v1.0.0`, `:119@v1.15.1`; `initialization.go:87@v0.37.0`, `:81@v1.0.0`, `:106@v1.15.1`; `kubernetes-sigs/controller-runtime pkg/builder/controller.go:408@v0.18.2`, `:408@v0.18.4`, `:449@v0.24.1`; `kubernetes/klog k8s_references.go:29-31,62@v2.120.1`, `:29-31,62@v2.140.0`; `go-logr/zapr zapr.go:171-178@v1.3.0`; `uber-go/zap zapcore/json_encoder.go:361@v1.27.0`, `:361@v1.28.0` | all three (v0.37.0, v1.0.0, v1.15.1) |
| `"message":"disrupting via (\w+) (\w+), terminating (\d+) nodes \((\d+) pods\) ([^"]+)"` | v0.37.0: `Info(fmt.Sprintf("disrupting via %s %s", m.Type(), cmd))` with `Command.String()` = `"%s, terminating %d nodes (%d pods) "` + `<name>/<instanceType>/<capacityType>` list, i.e. `disrupting via consolidation delete, terminating 1 nodes (3 pods) ip-…/m5.large/spot`. v1.0.0–v1.2.x: `Info(fmt.Sprintf("disrupting nodeclaim(s) via %s", cmd))` with the reason moved to a `reason` field and `c.Decision()` in the string, i.e. `disrupting nodeclaim(s) via delete, terminating 1 nodes (2 pods) …`. From v1.3.0: `Info("disrupting node(s)")` with the command rendered as structured values (`cmd.LogValues()`), moved to `queue.go` at v1.6.0 | `pkg/controllers/disruption/controller.go:188@v0.37.0`, `types.go:184-195@v0.37.0`; `controller.go:188@v1.0.0`, `types.go:141-152@v1.0.0`; `controller.go:198@v1.1.0`, `:205@v1.2.0`; `controller.go:205@v1.3.0`; `queue.go:295@v1.6.0`, `:390@v1.15.1` | v0.37.x only; boundaries v1.0.0 and v1.3.0 |
| `"level":"ERROR".*(launching|creating) nodeclaim|InsufficientInstanceCapacity|insufficient capacity|MaxSpotInstanceCountExceeded|UnfulfillableCapacity` | ICE launches: ERROR `failed launching nodeclaim` with `error` `insufficient capacity, with fleet error(s), <Code>: <msg>`; other launch errors: controller-runtime `Reconciler error` (ERROR) with `error` `launching nodeclaim, creating instance, …` (v1.15.1 `launching nodeclaim, creating nodeclaim, …`); `level` is the first key and spelled `ERROR` at every version; v1.15.1 additionally attaches `AWSErrorCode=UnfulfillableCapacity` as a structured value on the fleet error | `launch.go:86,100,103@v0.37.0`, `:79,103@v1.0.0`, `:99,132@v1.15.1`; `pkg/cloudprovider/types.go:309@v0.37.0`, `:780,838@v1.15.1`; provider `pkg/errors/errors.go:45-48@v0.37.0`, `:45-48@v1.0.0`, `:59-62@v1.15.1`; `instance.go:499,507@v0.37.0`, `:455-462,871,881@v1.15.1`; `cloudprovider.go:101@v0.37.0`, `:170@v1.15.1`; controller-runtime `controller.go:324@v0.18.2`, `:324@v0.18.4`, `:494@v0.24.1`; `logging.go:73@v0.37.0`, `:65@v1.0.0`, `:68@v1.15.1` (`CapitalLevelEncoder`) | all three |

The structured form from 1.3.0 carries, after the message `disrupting node(s)`, the keys `decision`, `disrupted-node-count`, `replacement-node-count`, `pod-count`, `disrupted-nodes` (a list of `{Node, NodeClaim, instance-type, capacity-type}`, the two objects as klog `ObjectRef`s, so `"Node":{"name":"…"}`) and `replacement-nodes` (`kubernetes-sigs/karpenter pkg/controllers/disruption/types.go:143-176@v1.3.0`, `:167-200@v1.6.0`, `:449-487@v1.15.1`); the reason is a `reason` field through at least 1.6.0 (`controller.go:205@v1.3.0`, `queue.go:295@v1.6.0`) and at 1.15.1 the first token of the `command` field, `<reason>/<id>: <decision>: nodepools=[…]: [nodes] (savings: $…)` (`queue.go:388-390@v1.15.1`, `types.go:350-380@v1.15.1`); the tag at which `reason` moved into `command` between 1.6.0 and 1.15.1 was not bisected. `disruption_line` reads all three shapes and `tests/test_scaling_karpenter_logs.py` holds one line of each.

### external-dns

Versions read: v0.14.0 (the EKS cluster) and v0.24.0 (newest, 2026-10-23). Libraries: logrus v1.9.3 / v1.10.2, aws-sdk-go v1.44.311 (0.14.0), aws-sdk-go-v2 v1.47.3 with `service/route53` v1.70.4 and smithy-go v1.28.5 (0.24.0).

How an external-dns line looks. logrus text by default: `--log-format` is `text` unless `json` (`pkg/apis/externaldns/types.go:317,609@v0.14.0`, `:44-45,320@v0.24.0`; `main.go:93-94@v0.14.0`, `controller/execute.go:219@v0.24.0` set the JSON formatter only then), `--log-level` default `info` (`types.go:319,611@v0.14.0`, `:321@v0.24.0`). A line is `time="<RFC3339>" level=<level> msg="<msg>"` (`sirupsen/logrus text_formatter.go:150@v1.9.3`, `:182@v1.10.2`; `formatter.go:7@v1.9.3`; level words `logrus.go:69@v1.9.3`, `:29@v1.10.2`); `msg` is `%q`-quoted when it needs quoting (`text_formatter.go:337@v1.9.3`), so an SDK v1 error's `\n\t` stays escaped on one line. `time="2026-11-04T10:53:46Z" level=info msg="Desired change: UPSERT a.example.com A [Id: /hostedzone/Z…]"` is cleared by NOT_ERROR_RE; `time="…" level=error msg="Failure in zone example.com. [Id: /hostedzone/Z…] when submitting change batch: InvalidChangeBatch: […]"` is caught by ERROR_RE. `_ts_of` takes the kubectl timestamp.

| Signature | What kubegap relies on | Source | Holds for |
|---|---|---|---|
| `external-dns.change-failed` | The AWS provider logs `Failure in zone %s [Id: %s] when submitting change batch: %v` (v0.24.0: `Failure in zone %s when submitting change batch: %v`) at error and returns `failed to submit all changes for the following zones: %v` (v0.24.0 as a soft error) | `provider/aws/aws.go:587,633@v0.14.0`, `:778,824@v0.24.0` | both, text format |
| | `InvalidChangeBatch`, `AccessDenied`, `Throttling` are Route 53 response codes inside that `%v`: aws-sdk-go v1 renders `<code>: <message>\n\tstatus code: …`, aws-sdk-go-v2 `operation error Route 53: ChangeResourceRecordSets, https response error …, InvalidChangeBatch: <message>` (the typed exception's `Error()` is `"%s: %s"` of code and message) | `aws/aws-sdk-go aws/awserr/types.go:13-15@v1.44.311`; `aws/aws-sdk-go-v2 service/route53/types/errors.go:710-711@v1.70.4` (module tag `service/route53/v1.70.4`); `aws/smithy-go errors.go:60@v1.28.5` | both; the Route 53 message texts are **unverified** |
| | v0.14.0 never spells `Throttling` (the SDK retries it: `aws/request/retryer.go:87@v1.44.311`, `WithMaxRetries(awsConfig.APIRetries)` `provider/aws/session.go:41@v0.14.0`); v0.24.0 catches `ThrottlingException` on `ListHostedZones` and logs `Skipping AWS profile %q due to provider side throttling: %v` at **info** with lowercase `throttling`, which the pattern does not count | `provider/aws/aws.go:414-415@v0.24.0` | both |
| | `level=error` is logrus's text spelling of every `log.Errorf` | `sirupsen/logrus text_formatter.go:150@v1.9.3`, `:182@v1.10.2`; `logrus.go:69@v1.9.3`, `:29@v1.10.2` | both, text format |

Notes that do not change a pattern:

- **unverified**: the controller loop that prints the returned `failed to submit all changes…` error (the `controller/` package) was not read; the provider's origin of the text is cited.

Corrections found in source:

- None: every alternative of `external-dns.change-failed` prints at both versions. The meaning now names the two lines.

### Argo CD

Versions read: v2.13.0 (the EKS cluster), v3.2.0 (the k3s cluster), v3.6.1 (newest release, 2026-11-04); boundary tags v2.14.0 and v3.0.0 (`util/log/logrus.go` and the application-controller command). Libraries at the `go.mod` pins: logrus v1.9.3 (v2.13.0, v3.2.0) and v1.10.2 (v3.6.1); gitops-engine `v0.7.1-0.20240905010810-bd7681ae3f8b` (v2.13.0) and `v0.7.1-0.20251006172252-b89b0871b414` (v3.2.0), in-tree `./gitops-engine` at v3.6.1 (`go.mod:17,368@v3.6.1`); go-grpc-middleware v1.4.0 (v2.13.0) and v2 v2.3.2 (v3.2.0; v3.6.1 pins v2.3.4, not re-read); grpc-go v1.66.2 / v1.75.1 / v1.84.0; go-git v5.12.0 / v5.14.0 / v5.19.2; go-github v63.0.0 (v2.13.0) and v69.2.0 (v3.2.0, v3.6.1); argoproj/pkg `d56162821bd1` (v2.13.0, the `CmdError` text); git v2.43.0 (the git of the ubuntu:24.04 base image, `Dockerfile:1@v2.13.0`; the git inside the ubuntu:25.04 and ubuntu:26.04 images of v3.2.0 and v3.6.1 is **unverified**, the repository does not pin it). The Helm chart was read at argo-helm `argo-cd-9.1.0` (appVersion v3.2.0).

How an Argo CD line looks. logrus. `util/log/logrus.go` `CreateFormatter` falls back to `TextFormatter` up to v2.14.x (`:45-48@v2.13.0`, `:45@v2.14.0`) and to `JSONFormatter` from v3.0.0 (`:48-51@v3.0.0`, `:48@v3.2.0`, `:48@v3.6.1`); every binary's `--logformat` default moved from `"text"` to `"json"` at the same release (`cmd/argocd-application-controller/commands/argocd_application_controller.go:237@v2.13.0` vs `:266@v3.0.0`; all eleven `cmd/*` defaults say `"json"` at v3.2.0), the flag writes `ARGOCD_LOG_FORMAT` (`util/cli/cli.go:185-196@v3.2.0`, `common/common.go:259@v2.13.0`). The level is lowercase (`sirupsen/logrus logrus.go:70@v1.9.3`, `:29@v1.10.2`), the keys are `time`, `level`, `msg` (`formatter.go:8-10@v1.9.3`, `:21-23@v1.10.2`). Text: `time="2026-11-04T15:24:35Z" level=info msg="Reconciliation completed" app-namespace=argocd application=web`; JSON (keys sorted): `{"error":"…","level":"error","msg":"Failed to sync cluster","time":"2026-11-04T14:52:05Z"}`. The argo-helm chart sets `global.logging.format: text` (`charts/argo-cd/values.yaml:72@argo-cd-9.1.0`) and writes it as `<component>.log.format` into `argocd-cmd-params-cm` (`charts/argo-cd/templates/_helpers.tpl:244-247@argo-cd-9.1.0`), which the controller reads as `ARGOCD_APPLICATION_CONTROLLER_LOGFORMAT` (`manifests/base/application-controller/argocd-application-controller-statefulset.yaml:79-83@v3.2.0`), so a chart install with default values prints text even on v3. The k3s cluster's v3.2.0 prints JSON: that install is not a default-values chart install (observed, not read: **unverified** which; `manifests/install.yaml` or an explicit `global.logging.format: json` both give it). `ERROR_RE` / `NOT_ERROR_RE` (`lib/logs.py:34-37`): the info lines above match `level=info` / `"level":"info"` in `NOT_ERROR_RE`; the error lines match `level=error` / `"level":"error"` in `ERROR_RE`. The audit line `level=info msg="Sync operation to 3f1c2ab failed: one or more objects failed to apply"` matches both (`\bfailed\b` and `level=info`). `_ts_of` finds the ISO time in `time="…"` / `"time":"…"`.

|---|---|---|---|
| `argocd.sync-failed` | gitops-engine's `setOperationFailed` (`executeSyncFailPhase` at v3.6.1) builds `one or more objects failed to apply[, reason: …]` and `setOperationPhase` logs `Updating operation state. phase: %s -> %s, message: '%s' -> '%s'` at Info through the logrus adapter | `argoproj/gitops-engine pkg/sync/sync_context.go:411,541,592,921@bd7681ae3f8b`, `:456,595,732,1068@b89b0871b414`; `argoproj/argo-cd gitops-engine/pkg/sync/sync_context.go:553,709,716,938,953,964,1293@v3.6.1`; adapter `controller/sync.go:309@v3.2.0` | all three, info level, both formats |
