Notes that do not change a pattern:

- A 429 line in promtail counts under `loki.rate-limited` and `promtail.send-failed`; `lib/logs.py` suppresses duplicates only for `component: any`.
- inotify watch exhaustion (`fs.inotify.max_user_watches`) returns ENOSPC (`no space left on device`), not EMFILE; `promtail.too-many-open-files` sees only the instance limit.

Corrections found in source:

- `loki.rate-limited` and `loki.stream-limit` were case-sensitive while Loki v3.6.0 lower-cased `Ingestion rate limit exceeded` and `Maximum active stream limit exceeded` (`pkg/validation/validate.go:30,37@v3.6.0`); both patterns now start with `(?i)`. `per-user streams limit`, `streams limit exceeded` (never leave the ingester, `pkg/ingester/instance.go:238@v2.6.1`) and `too many outstanding requests` (query path, answered to Grafana) were removed; `dropping batch due to rate limiting` was added.
- `loki.out-of-order` matched `too far in the future`, which Loki never prints; the text is `has timestamp too new` (`validate.go:41@v2.6.1`, `:57@v3.7.9`).
- `loki.flush-failed` lost `flush failed` and `error flushing`: printed by no version.
- `promtail.tail-failed`'s bare `failed to tail file` also matched the Info `file is a directory` line (`filetarget.go:389@v3.7.9`) for every directory under `/var/log/pods`; it is now `failed to tail file, stat failed|failed to start tailer`.

### fluent-bit

Versions read: v3.0.4 (k3s) and v5.1.5 (newest). The errno texts come from glibc's `strerror_r` in the image base: `debian:bullseye-slim` (`dockerfiles/Dockerfile:20@v3.0.4`, glibc 2.31, `bminor/glibc sysdeps/gnu/errlist.c@glibc-2.31`) and `debian:trixie-slim` (`dockerfiles/Dockerfile:22@v5.1.5`, glibc 2.41, `sysdeps/gnu/errlist.h@glibc-2.41`).

How a fluent-bit line looks. Its own logger: `[%Y/%m/%d %H:%M:%S] [%5s] ` (`src/flb_log.c:565-581@v3.0.4`; v5.1.5 adds milliseconds, `:221-233`) with the level title right-aligned to five columns — `[ info]`, `[ warn]`, `[error]`, `[debug]` (`src/flb_log.c:519-532@v3.0.4`, `:809-822@v5.1.5`) — then `[<subsystem>]` or `[output:es:es.0]`, then the message: `[2026/11/05 10:05:50] [ warn] [engine] failed to flush chunk '1-....flb', retry in 11 seconds: task_id=3, input=tail.0 > output=es.0 (out_id=0)`. The default level is info (`include/fluent-bit/flb_log.h:42@v3.0.4`). Errno is printed by `flb_errno()` as `[<file>:<line> errno=<n>] <strerror>` (`src/flb_log.c:673-678@v3.0.4`, `:1318-1326@v5.1.5`; macro `include/fluent-bit/flb_log.h:232@v3.0.4`). There is no `level=` key, so `NOT_ERROR_RE` never applies: `[ info] [input:tail:tail.0] inotify_fs_add(): inode=1 ...` is not an error (no error word), `[ warn] [engine] failed to flush chunk ...` and `[error] [output:es:es.0] HTTP status=429 ...` are errors by the words `failed`/`error`. The timestamp uses slashes, so only the kubectl prefix gives `_ts_of` a time.

| Signature | What kubegap relies on | Source | Holds for |
|---|---|---|---|
| `fluent-bit.flush-failed` | The engine logs `[engine] failed to flush chunk '<id>', retry in N seconds: ...` at Warn per retry; HTTP outputs log `HTTP status=<code> URI=<uri>[, response:\n<body>]` (out_es, out_opensearch) or `<host>:<port>, HTTP status=<code>` (out_loki) and `could not flush records to <host>:<port> (http_do=N)` at Error | `src/flb_engine.c:423@v3.0.4`, `:822@v5.1.5`; `plugins/out_es/es.c:907,911@v3.0.4`, `:1564,1568,1761,1765@v5.1.5`; `plugins/out_opensearch/opensearch.c:974,978@v3.0.4`, `:1210,1214@v5.1.5`; `plugins/out_loki/loki.c:1665,1672,1677,1696@v3.0.4`, `:2026,2056,2061,2080@v5.1.5` | both, default level |
| | The success line `[engine] flush chunk '<id>' succeeded at retry N: ...` is Info | `src/flb_engine.c:310@v3.0.4`, `:679@v5.1.5` | both |
| | The response body printed after `response:` is on its own lines (one kubectl line each); an ES bulk answered 200 with `errors:true` prints the item errors only with `trace_error on` (`error: Output\n<body>`) | `plugins/out_es/es.c:905-911,922-936,1203@v3.0.4`, `:1562-1568,1651,2032@v5.1.5` | both |
| `fluent-bit.upstream-down` | `no upstream connections available` (out_loki, out_forward and others; not in out_es), `upstream connection initialization error` (in_kubernetes_events, in_nginx_exporter_metrics), glibc's `Connection refused` / `Connection timed out` through `flb_errno()`, `[upstream] connection #N to <host> timed out after N seconds (...)` and `[http_client] broken connection to <host>:<port> ?`, all at Error | `plugins/out_loki/loki.c:1577@v3.0.4`, `:1934@v5.1.5`; `plugins/out_forward/forward.c:1575@v3.0.4`; `plugins/in_kubernetes_events/kubernetes_events.c:672@v3.0.4`, `:878@v5.1.5`; `errlist.c:686,696@glibc-2.31`, `errlist.h:416,422@glibc-2.41`; `src/flb_upstream.c:920@v3.0.4`, `:1086@v5.1.5`; `src/flb_http_client.c:1348@v3.0.4`, `:2100@v5.1.5` | both, default level |
| | `[net] could not connect to <host>:<port>` and `[net] TCP connection timed out` are Debug | `src/flb_network.c:534,1419,1437@v3.0.4`, `:630,1517,1535@v5.1.5` | both |
| `fluent-bit.backpressure` | `[input] <name> paused (mem buf overlimit...)`, `... paused (storage buf overlimit N/M)` and, from v5, `... paused (rate gate limit exceeded)` at Warn; the matching `[input] <name> resume (...)` is Info; the storage backlog plugin logs `[storage backlog] could not retrieve chunk tag ...` (Error) and, at v5, `[storage backlog] chunk '<id>' evicted ...` (Warn) | `src/flb_input_chunk.c:1259,1289,1316@v3.0.4`, `:3067,3092,3125@v5.1.5`; `src/flb_input.c:2740@v5.1.5`; `plugins/in_storage_backlog/sb.c:299@v3.0.4`, `:328,557@v5.1.5` | both |
| | `storage.backlog` (with a dot) appears only as the configuration key, `input chunk ... full`, `exceeds Mem_Buf_Limit` and `backpressure` (one v5 metrics-init error apart) are printed by no version | `include/fluent-bit/flb_config.h:361@v3.0.4`, `:477@v5.1.5`; `src/flb_output.c:1744@v5.1.5` | both |
| `fluent-bit.too-many-open-files` | A failed `inotify_add_watch` prints `[plugins/in_tail/tail_fs_inotify.c:<line> errno=24] Too many open files` (the pattern takes errno 24 and 28 only: `flb_errno()` runs for every failure of that call, and a file rotated away between discovery and the watch gives errno 2, which is routine) (instance limit) or `errno=28] No space left on device` (watch limit) through `flb_errno()`; in_tail logs `cannot open <path>` at Error | `plugins/in_tail/tail_fs_inotify.c:143,147@v3.0.4`, `:144,148@v5.1.5`; `errlist.c:265,318@glibc-2.31`, `errlist.h:157,190@glibc-2.41`; `plugins/in_tail/tail_file.c:953@v3.0.4`, `:1445@v5.1.5` | both |

Notes that do not change a pattern:

- out_es prints nothing of its own when the upstream has no connection (no `no upstream connections available` in `es.c`); the engine's `failed to flush chunk` line is the evidence.

Corrections found in source:

- `fluent-bit.flush-failed`'s `flush chunk .* (failed|retry)` matched the Info success line `flush chunk '...' succeeded at retry N` (`src/flb_engine.c:310@v3.0.4`), so every recovered retry counted as a failure; the alternative was removed.
- `fluent-bit.upstream-down` and `fluent-bit.too-many-open-files` used lower-case `connection refused`, `connection timed out` and `too many open files`, while glibc capitalises them (`errlist.c:696,686,265@glibc-2.31`); the patterns now accept both cases. `could not connect` (Debug) was removed; `connection #N to <host> timed out` (`src/flb_upstream.c:920@v3.0.4`) and the `tail_fs_inotify.c:<line> errno=` line were added; `inotify_add_watch` is never printed.
- `fluent-bit.backpressure` matched the Info `resume (mem buf overlimit)` line (`src/flb_input_chunk.c:1259@v3.0.4`) and four texts no version prints (`storage.backlog`, `input chunk.*full`, `exceeds Mem_Buf_Limit`, `backpressure`); it is now anchored to `[input] <name> paused (` and the `[storage backlog]` error lines.

### Elasticsearch and OpenSearch

Versions read: Elasticsearch v7.10.2 (k3s), v8.19.24 (last 8.x) and v9.5.6 (newest); OpenSearch 3.9.0 (newest), because the collector is `elasticsearch-opensearch`. The ECS layout strings come from elastic/ecs-logging-java v1.7.0 (`build-tools-internal/version.properties:17@v8.19.24`, `@v9.5.6`; `server/build.gradle:77-78@v8.19.24`).

How an Elasticsearch line looks. 7.10.2's Docker config sends `ESJsonLayout` to the console at `rootLogger.level = info` (`distribution/docker/src/docker/config/log4j2.properties:3-9@v7.10.2`): `{"type": "server", "timestamp": "2026-11-05T10:05:50,123Z", "level": "WARN", "component": "o.e.c.r.a.DiskThresholdMonitor", "cluster.name": "logs", "node.name": "es-0", "message": "...", "cluster.uuid": "x", "node.id": "y" }` — the key separator is `": "` with a space (`server/.../common/logging/ESJsonLayout.java:90-96,121-125,141@v7.10.2`) and stack traces are a `"stacktrace": [...]` array, one element per line. 8.x and 9.x keep the generic `distribution/src/config/log4j2.properties:3-12` whose rolling `ECSJsonLayout` appender the Docker build turns into a console appender (`build-tools-internal/.../TransformLog4jConfigFilter.java:78-100@v8.19.24`, `value = "Console"` `:93`); `ECSJsonLayout` wraps `co.elastic.logging.log4j2.EcsLayout` (`server/.../common/logging/ECSJsonLayout.java:12,25-29@v8.19.24`): `{"@timestamp":"2026-11-05T10:05:50.123Z", "log.level": "WARN", "message":"...", "ecs.version": "1.2.0", ..., "log.logger":"...", "error.stack_trace":"..."}` with the level key `log.level` padded to five columns (`ecs-logging-core/.../EcsJsonSerializer.java:45-49,131-136@v1.7.0`). OpenSearch 3.9.0's Docker config is `OpenSearchJsonLayout` to the console (`distribution/docker/src/docker/config/log4j2.properties:14-20@3.9.0`; `OpenSearchJsonLayout.java:127-129,176-177@3.9.0`), the same shape as ES 7. None of the three level spellings (`"level": "INFO"` with a space, `"log.level": "INFO"`) matches `NOT_ERROR_RE`'s `"level":"info"` or `ERROR_RE`'s `"level":"(error|fatal)"`, so the classifier sees only words: `{"type": "server", ..., "level": "INFO", ..., "message": "[logs-2026.11.05] creating index, cause [auto(bulk api)], ..."}` is not an error, `{"type": "server", ..., "level": "ERROR", ..., "message": "fatal error in thread [...], exiting"}` is an error by the words `ERROR`/`error`, and an Info line whose message contains `failed` is an unclassified error. Both timestamp forms satisfy `_ts_of`'s ISO regex.

| Signature | What kubegap relies on | Source | Holds for |
|---|---|---|---|
| `elasticsearch.read-only-flood` | `DiskThresholdMonitor` logs `flood stage disk watermark [<t>] exceeded on <node>, all indices on this node will be marked read-only` at Warn (8.x/9.x also a shorter `flood stage disk watermark [<t>] exceeded on <node>`) | `server/.../cluster/routing/allocation/DiskThresholdMonitor.java:171@v7.10.2`, `:194,218@v8.19.24`, `:204,227@v9.5.6`; OpenSearch `server/.../cluster/routing/allocation/DiskThresholdMonitor.java:229@3.9.0` | all four, default level |
| | `cluster_block_exception` is the REST `type` (`getExceptionName` = the class name in underscore case) and `blocked by: [TOO_MANY_REQUESTS/12/disk usage exceeded flood-stage watermark, index has read-only-allow-delete block]` is `ClusterBlockException`'s message (`status/id/description` of block 12, status TOO_MANY_REQUESTS since 7.4); the server logs the block only at Debug, so both texts are seen in fluent-bit's log after `HTTP status=429 ... response:` or in `error: Output` with `trace_error` | `server/.../ElasticsearchException.java:659-665,829@v7.10.2`, `:803,1403@v8.19.24`, `:882,1479@v9.5.6`; OpenSearch `libs/core/.../OpenSearchException.java:724-730@3.9.0`; `server/.../cluster/block/ClusterBlockException.java:79,82@v7.10.2`, `:75@v8.19.24`, `:75@v9.5.6`, OpenSearch `:99,102@3.9.0`; `server/.../cluster/metadata/IndexMetadata.java:98-100@v7.10.2`, `:132-139@v8.19.24`, `:135-142@v9.5.6`, OpenSearch `:151-157@3.9.0`; `server/.../action/support/master/TransportMasterNodeAction.java:143@v7.10.2`, `:193@v8.19.24`, `:276@v9.5.6`; `fluent/fluent-bit plugins/out_es/es.c:907,936@v3.0.4`, `:1564,1651,1761@v5.1.5` | all four |
| | `FORBIDDEN/12/index read-only / allow delete (api)` is the pre-7.4 wording of block 12 and appears in no version read | `IndexMetadata.java:98-100@v7.10.2` | — |
| `elasticsearch.disk-watermark` | `low disk watermark [<t>] exceeded on <node>, replicas will not be assigned to this node` (Info) and `high disk watermark [<t>] exceeded on <node>, shards will be relocated away from this node; ...` (Warn, Info when a reroute just happened); `<t>` is `90%` for a relative setting (`format1Decimals` strips `.0`; `RatioValue.formatNoTrailingZerosPercent`), the byte string (`10gb`) for an absolute one, and `max_headroom=150gb` when the 8.x headroom applies | `DiskThresholdMonitor.java:219,275,280@v7.10.2`, `:270,345@v8.19.24`, `:278,353@v9.5.6`, OpenSearch `:279,351,360@3.9.0`; `server/.../cluster/routing/allocation/DiskThresholdSettings.java:325-342@v7.10.2`, `:560-576,580,591,602@v8.19.24`, `:548,568,579,590@v9.5.6`, OpenSearch `:488,494,500@3.9.0`; `server/.../common/Strings.java:680-690@v7.10.2`; `server/.../common/unit/RelativeByteSizeValue.java:106-109@v8.19.24`, `@v9.5.6`; `server/.../common/unit/RatioValue.java:76-85@v8.19.24`, `@v9.5.6` | all four, default level |
| | The recovery lines `high disk watermark [<t>] no longer exceeded on ..., but low disk watermark [<t>] is still exceeded` and `low disk watermark [<t>] no longer exceeded on ...` do not match the pattern (word order) | `DiskThresholdMonitor.java:222,239@v7.10.2`, `:276,296@v8.19.24`, `:284,304@v9.5.6`, OpenSearch `:285,304@3.9.0` | all four |
| `elasticsearch.circuit-breaker` | `[parent] Data too large, data for [...]` and `[<breaker>] Data too large, data for [<field>]` are the `CircuitBreakingException` messages, logged by the server at Debug only and returned as 429 `circuit_breaking_exception`; at the default level the text is in fluent-bit's log (response body) or in a server stack trace | `server/.../indices/breaker/HierarchyCircuitBreakerService.java:318,345@v7.10.2`, `:432,447@v8.19.24`, `:433,448@v9.5.6`, OpenSearch `:432@3.9.0`; `server/.../common/breaker/ChildMemoryCircuitBreaker.java:71,75@v7.10.2`, `:78,91@v8.19.24`, `:79,93@v9.5.6`, OpenSearch `:90,103@3.9.0`; `ElasticsearchException.java:975@v7.10.2`, `:1794@v8.19.24`, `:1875@v9.5.6`, OpenSearch `OpenSearchException.java:148@3.9.0` | all four |
| `elasticsearch.oom` | The uncaught-exception handler special-cases `OutOfMemoryError` and logs `fatal error in thread [<name>], exiting` with the throwable, whose `java.lang.OutOfMemoryError: ...` line is in the stack trace field | `server/.../bootstrap/ElasticsearchUncaughtExceptionHandler.java:44@v7.10.2`, `:31@v8.19.24`, `:31@v9.5.6` (the ES message line itself is **unverified**: only the `instanceof` branch was read); OpenSearch `server/.../bootstrap/OpenSearchUncaughtExceptionHandler.java:61,83@3.9.0` | all four |
| `elasticsearch.master-not-discovered` | `ClusterFormationFailureHelper` logs its description at Warn every 10 s while the cluster is unformed: `master not discovered yet: ...`, `master not discovered yet, this node has not previously joined a bootstrapped cluster, ...`, `master not discovered or elected yet, an election requires ...`; OpenSearch says `cluster-manager not discovered ...` | `server/.../cluster/coordination/ClusterFormationFailureHelper.java:103,159,177,185,207@v7.10.2`, `:130,300,319,330,349@v8.19.24`, `:135,367,386,397,411@v9.5.6`; OpenSearch `:132,210,228,239,268@3.9.0` | all four, default level |
| | `not enough master nodes discovered during pinging (found [...], but needed [N]), pinging again` is Zen discovery (Warn), removed with Zen in 8.0; `MasterNotDiscoveredException` (OpenSearch `ClusterManagerNotDiscoveredException`) is thrown to the client, not logged | `server/.../discovery/zen/ZenDiscovery.java:851@v7.10.2`; `TransportMasterNodeAction.java:208,230@v7.10.2`, `:288,316@v8.19.24`, `:371@v9.5.6`; OpenSearch `server/.../action/admin/cluster/health/TransportClusterHealthAction.java:284@3.9.0` | 7.x only / all four |
| `elasticsearch.bulk-rejected` | `rejected execution of <task> on EsThreadPoolExecutor[...]` and `rejected execution of coordinating operation [...]` are `EsRejectedExecutionException` messages (OpenSearch `OpenSearchRejectedExecutionException`), returned as 429 / bulk item errors named `es_rejected_execution_exception` (`open_search_rejected_execution_exception` on OpenSearch, derived from the class name through `getExceptionName`; the registered enum entry was not located and the name is **unverified** beyond that derivation); the server logs a failed bulk item at Debug/Trace only, so the text is seen in fluent-bit's log when `trace_error` is on | `server/.../common/util/concurrent/EsAbortPolicy.java:48@v7.10.2`; `server/.../common/util/concurrent/EsRejectedExecutionHandler.java:48@v8.19.24`, `@v9.5.6`; OpenSearch `server/.../common/util/concurrent/OpenSearchAbortPolicy.java:67@3.9.0`; `server/.../index/IndexingPressure.java:67,98@v7.10.2`, `:261@v8.19.24`, `:276@v9.5.6`, OpenSearch `:108,152@3.9.0`; `ElasticsearchException.java:659@v7.10.2`, `:803@v8.19.24`, `:882@v9.5.6`, OpenSearch `OpenSearchException.java:724@3.9.0`; `server/.../action/bulk/TransportShardBulkAction.java:360@v7.10.2`, `:521@v8.19.24`, `:651@v9.5.6`, OpenSearch `:766@3.9.0`; `fluent/fluent-bit plugins/out_es/es.c:936@v3.0.4`, `:1651@v5.1.5` | all four |

Notes that do not change a pattern:

- fluent-bit prints a non-2xx response body on its own lines after `HTTP status=<code> URI=<uri>, response:`; kubectl gives each line its own timestamp, so the ES JSON error lands in `logs/pods/fluent-bit/*.log`, which is why the three ES signatures with client-facing texts also read that file.
- Whether `CircuitBreakingException`, `MasterNotDiscoveredException` or `EsRejectedExecutionException` ever appear in a server stack trace at Warn or Error is **unverified**: no `logger.warn` carrying them was found in the trees read.

Corrections found in source:

- `elasticsearch.read-only-flood`, `elasticsearch.circuit-breaker` and `elasticsearch.bulk-rejected` relied on REST-body texts that the server logs at Debug only (`ChildMemoryCircuitBreaker.java:75@v7.10.2`, `TransportShardBulkAction.java:360@v7.10.2`, `TransportMasterNodeAction.java:143@v7.10.2`); their `sources` now also read `logs/pods/fluent-bit/*.log`, where fluent-bit prints the response, and the meanings say so. `FORBIDDEN/12/index read-only` (pre-7.4) and `failed to execute bulk` (Debug) were removed.
- `elasticsearch.disk-watermark` required `[<digits>%]`; an absolute watermark prints `[10gb]` and the 8.x headroom prints `[max_headroom=150gb]` (`DiskThresholdSettings.java:560-576@v8.19.24`), so the bracket is now `\[[^\]]+\]`.
- `elasticsearch.master-not-discovered` and `elasticsearch.bulk-rejected` were silent on OpenSearch, which prints `cluster-manager not discovered` (`ClusterFormationFailureHelper.java:210@3.9.0`) and names its exception `OpenSearchRejectedExecutionException`; both spellings were added, as was `ClusterManagerNotDiscoveredException`.

### ingress-nginx

Versions read: controller-v1.14.0 (the k3s cluster image `registry.k8s.io/ingress-nginx/controller:v1.14.0`, released 2025-11-04) and controller-v1.15.1 (2026-03-19, the last release: the repository was archived 2026-03-24). Every controller string is identical at both tags. The nginx core strings were read in nginx/nginx at release-1.27.1: `images/nginx/rootfs/build.sh:21` pins `NGINX_VERSION=1.27.1` at both controller tags, and the build.sh history has no NGINX bump after "Bump to OpenResty v1.27.1.2" (2025-06-18), so the base images `nginx:v2.2.4` (`NGINX_BASE@controller-v1.14.0`) and `nginx:v2.2.8` (`@controller-v1.15.1`) both carry 1.27.1. The image is Alpine 3.22.2 (`images/nginx/rootfs/Dockerfile:14,24@controller-v1.14.0`) and 3.23.3 (`@controller-v1.15.1`), whose musl is 1.2.5 (`alpinelinux/aports main/musl/APKBUILD:5` on branches 3.22-stable and 3.23-stable); musl was read at tag v1.2.5 from git.musl-libc.org (cited as `musl-libc/musl`). klog is `k8s.io/klog/v2 v2.130.1` (`go.mod:44@controller-v1.14.0`) and `v2.140.0` (`go.mod:47@controller-v1.15.1`); Go strings at go1.25.3 and go1.26.1 (`go.mod:3`). glibc's errno text was read at glibc-2.41 (`bminor/glibc`) for the non-Alpine case only.

How an ingress-nginx line looks. The controller pod log is one stream with three layouts. (1) The controller: klog text with default flags (`cmd/nginx/main.go:54` `klog.InitFlags(nil)`, verbosity 0), header `Lmmdd hh:mm:ss.uuuuuu threadid file:line] ` with `L` from `IWEF` (`kubernetes/klog internal/buffer/buffer.go:117-152@v2.130.1`, `:117-152@v2.140.0`; `internal/severity/severity.go:39@v2.130.1`). (2) The nginx access log on stdout: `log-format-upstream` (`internal/ingress/controller/config/config.go:54`) with `[$time_local]` as `[04/Nov/2026:12:00:00 +0000]`, written to `/var/log/nginx/access.log` -> `/dev/stdout` (`rootfs/Dockerfile:84`). (3) The nginx error log on stderr: `YYYY/MM/DD HH:MM:SS [level] pid#tid: *cid message[ (errno: text)][ while action], client: ..., request: "...", upstream: "...", host: "..."` (`nginx/nginx src/core/ngx_log.c:112-130,288-313@release-1.27.1`, `src/core/ngx_times.c:152`, `src/http/ngx_http_request.c:3857-3858`), levels `emerg alert crit error warn notice info debug` (`ngx_log.c:75-85`), default threshold `notice` (`config.go:42` `errorLevel = "notice"`), file `/var/log/nginx/error.log` -> `/dev/stderr` (`rootfs/Dockerfile:85`); the controller runs nginx with its own stdout/stderr (`internal/ingress/controller/nginx.go:434-435`). Switches: the ConfigMap keys `error-log-level` (`config.go:224`), `log-format-upstream` (`:289`), `access-log-path`/`error-log-path` (`:770,775`) and klog's `--v`. ERROR_RE / NOT_ERROR_RE (`lib/logs.py:34-37`): the info line `I1104 12:00:00.123456       7 store.go:406] "Ignoring ingress because of error while validating ingress class" ingress="default/foo" error="..."` is classified as an error (`\berror\b` matches, no `level=info` to exempt it); the error-log line `2026/11/04 12:00:00 [error] 32#32: *123 connect() failed (111: Connection refused) while connecting to upstream, ...` is classified as an error by `[error]`/`failed`; an access-log line `10.0.0.1 - - [04/Nov/2026:12:00:00 +0000] "GET /api HTTP/1.1" 502 150 ...` matches neither regex, so a 5xx is invisible to the scanner unless the path contains a whole word `error`/`failed`. `_ts_of` reads neither `2026/11/04` nor `[04/Nov/2026:...]`; it uses the kubectl prefix (and the klog header for controller lines).

| Signature | What kubegap relies on | Source | Holds for |
|---|---|---|---|
| `ingress-nginx.emerg` | nginx writes `[emerg]` as the level token of an error-log line (`err_levels[1] = "emerg"`); the master logs a rejected reload there. The same text with the prefix `nginx: [emerg] ` is the `nginx -t` form (written while `ngx_use_stderr` is 1, which the master clears after start-up), which the controller runs as `nginx -c <tmp> -t` and prints inside its `Unexpected failure reloading the backend:` error, one line per output line | `nginx/nginx src/core/ngx_log.c:77,112-121,198-208@release-1.27.1`, `src/core/nginx.c:378`; `internal/ingress/controller/util.go:146@controller-v1.14.0`, `:146@controller-v1.15.1`; `internal/ingress/controller/nginx.go:657-670` (both); `internal/ingress/controller/controller.go:223@controller-v1.14.0`, `:226@controller-v1.15.1`; `rootfs/Dockerfile:85`, `rootfs/etc/nginx/template/nginx.tmpl:374` (both) | both tags, any `error-log-level` (emerg is the lowest level) |
| `ingress-nginx.no-live-upstreams` | `no live upstreams` at NGX_LOG_ERR from the http upstream module and the stream proxy | `nginx/nginx src/http/ngx_http_upstream.c:1569@release-1.27.1`, `src/stream/ngx_stream_proxy_module.c:746` | both tags, `error-log-level` error or lower (default notice) |
| `ingress-nginx.upstream-errors` | `upstream prematurely closed connection` (NGX_LOG_ERR); `connect() failed` from `ngx_connection_error`, which uses NGX_LOG_ERR for ECONNREFUSED/ETIMEDOUT/EHOSTUNREACH, followed by ` (111: Connection refused)` (`ngx_log_errno`, strerror text copied at start-up from musl) and ` while connecting to upstream` from the http log handler with the action set before `connect()`; `upstream timed out` (NGX_LOG_ERR) | `ngx_http_upstream.c:2456,3733,4224`; `:2803-2804`, `src/core/ngx_connection.c:1565-1627`, `src/core/ngx_log.c:304`, `src/os/unix/ngx_errno.c:77,95`, `musl-libc/musl src/errno/__strerror.h:34@v1.2.5`, `src/http/ngx_http_request.c:3857-3858`; `ngx_http_upstream.c:3471,3676,4108,4363` | both tags, default error-log-level; the errno text is musl's on the Alpine image (ETIMEDOUT is `Operation timed out`, `__strerror.h:33`), the pattern does not depend on it |
| `ingress-nginx.worker-connections` | `%ui worker_connections are not enough` at NGX_LOG_ALERT when `ngx_get_connection` finds no free connection, `..., reusing connections` at NGX_LOG_WARN, and the config-time EMERG form; file-descriptor exhaustion is `accept4() failed (24: <strerror>)` at NGX_LOG_CRIT, where strerror(EMFILE) is `No file descriptors available` and strerror(ENFILE) `Too many open files in system` on musl, `Too many open files` on glibc; the Go controller's own syscall text is lowercase `too many open files` | `nginx/nginx src/core/ngx_connection.c:1196-1198,1388-1391@release-1.27.1`, `src/event/ngx_event.c:454-457`, `src/event/ngx_event_accept.c:85-91`, `src/os/unix/ngx_errno.c:77,95`; `musl-libc/musl src/errno/__strerror.h:54,55@v1.2.5`; `bminor/glibc sysdeps/gnu/errlist.h:157,164@glibc-2.41`; `golang/go src/syscall/zerrors_linux_amd64.go:1381@go1.25.3`, `:1381@go1.26.1` | both tags; the musl texts hold for the project's Alpine images (`images/nginx/rootfs/Dockerfile:24`), the glibc text for a rebuilt image on a glibc base |
| `ingress-nginx.invalid-ingress` | `Ignoring ingress because of error while validating ingress class` (`klog.InfoS`, no `V(n)`, printed at verbosity 0); `Error obtaining Endpoints for Service %q: %v`, `Error obtaining Service %q: %v`, `Error getting SSL certificate %q: %v[. Using default certificate]` (`klog.Warningf`) | `internal/ingress/controller/store/store.go:406,439` (both tags); `internal/ingress/controller/controller.go:1126@controller-v1.14.0`, `:1135@controller-v1.15.1`, `internal/ingress/controller/endpointslices.go:86` (both); `controller.go:1068,1142@controller-v1.14.0`, `:1077,1151@controller-v1.15.1`; `controller.go:1455,1734@controller-v1.14.0`, `:1468,1747@controller-v1.15.1`; `cmd/nginx/main.go:54` | both tags, klog text format (the controller sets no `--logging-format`) |
| | The lowercase `ignoring ingress %v in %v based on annotation %v: %v` and `ignoring ingress %v in namespace %v different from the namespace watched %s` are different warnings (an Ingress of another class or namespace) and are not counted | `controller.go:349,354@controller-v1.14.0`, `:352,357@controller-v1.15.1` | both tags |

Notes that do not change a pattern:

- The `nginx -t` output is a multi-line klog E message; only its first line carries the klog header. The container runtime stores each output line separately, so `nginx: [emerg] ...` is its own line in the pod log.
- A `[warn] ... worker_connections are not enough, reusing connections` line prints at most once per second (`ngx_connection.c:1385-1391@release-1.27.1`); it is counted like the alert, both mean the limit was reached.
- `Ignoring ingress because of error while validating ingress class` is an info line that repeats at every resync for an Ingress with a missing or foreign IngressClass; with `warn_at: 1` a cluster running two ingress controllers reports it every run.

Corrections found in source:

- `ingress-nginx.emerg` had two alternatives, `nginx: \[emerg\]` and `\[emerg\]`; the first is contained in the second (`ngx_log.c:207` adds `nginx: ` in front of the same ` [emerg] ` token). The pattern is `\[emerg\]`.
- `ingress-nginx.worker-connections` matched `too many open files`, a text nginx never prints: nginx prints `strerror(errno)`, which on the Alpine image is musl's `No file descriptors available` (EMFILE, `__strerror.h:55@v1.2.5`) or `Too many open files in system` (ENFILE, `:54`), and on glibc `Too many open files` (`errlist.h:157@glibc-2.41`), all starting with a capital letter. The lowercase form is Go's `syscall.Errno(24)` text (`zerrors_linux_amd64.go:1381@go1.26.1`), which only an incidental controller error would carry; nothing in `internal/`, `pkg/` or `cmd/` spells it at either tag. The pattern now lists the three spellings beside `worker_connections are not enough`.
- `ingress-nginx.invalid-ingress` matched `ignoring ingress because of error while validating` and `error obtaining service` in lowercase; the controller prints `Ignoring ingress because of error while validating ingress class` (`store.go:406,439`) and `Error obtaining Service %q: %v` (`controller.go:1068,1142@controller-v1.14.0`), so both alternatives were silent at every version. The pattern now carries the source's case.

### Traefik

Versions read: no Traefik runs on either cluster; the supported versions are what k3s packages: `manifests/traefik.yaml@v1.33.5+k3s1` installs chart `traefik-34.2.1+up34.2.0` with image tag `3.3.6` (`:16,30`) and `manifests/traefik.yaml@v1.37.2+k3s1` chart `traefik-41.6.101+up41.6.1` with image tag `3.7.15` (`:20,35`). Read in full: v3.3.2 (2025-01-14, read for #123), v3.3.6 (2025-04-18), v3.7.17 (2026-10-30, newest); `pkg/provider/acme/provider.go` and the Kubernetes provider files also at v3.7.15 (2026-10-15) and, for the two boundaries, at v3.4.0, v3.5.0, v3.6.0, v3.6.6 (2025-12-29), v3.6.7 (2026-01-14), v3.6.8 to v3.6.12, v3.6.18, v3.6.25 and v3.7.0. Libraries: `github.com/rs/zerolog v1.33.0` (`go.mod:53@v3.3.2`, `:59@v3.3.6`) and `v1.34.0` (`go.mod:63@v3.7.17`); `github.com/go-acme/lego/v4 v4.21.0` (`go.mod:20@v3.3.2`) and `lego/v5 v5.6.0` (`go.mod:26@v3.7.17`); the chart defaults from `traefik/traefik-helm-chart` v34.2.0 (`traefik/Chart.yaml:5,7`: appVersion v3.3.2) and v41.6.1 (`:5,7`: appVersion v3.7.13).

How a Traefik line looks. Traefik v3 logs with zerolog: `zerolog.New(w).With().Timestamp()` (`cmd/traefik/logger.go:42@v3.3.2`, `:43@v3.7.17`), level from `--log.level` with default `ERROR` (`pkg/types/logs.go:53-56@v3.3.2`, `pkg/observability/types/logs.go:54-57@v3.7.17`; `logger.go:97-112@v3.3.2`, `:94-109@v3.7.17`), format from `--log.format` with default `common` (same `SetDefaults`). The k3s chart passes `--log.level=INFO` (`traefik/values.yaml:361@v34.2.0`, `:543@v41.6.1`; `traefik/templates/_podtemplate.tpl:723@v34.2.0`, `:789@v41.6.1`), leaves the format common and `noColor: false` (`values.yaml:358,365@v34.2.0`, `:540@v41.6.1`; the `--log.noColor` flag is emitted only when true, `_podtemplate.tpl:719-720@v34.2.0`, `:785-786@v41.6.1`). Common format is `zerolog.ConsoleWriter{TimeFormat: time.RFC3339, NoColor: <noColor or filePath>}` (`logger.go:86-91@v3.3.2`, `:83-88@v3.7.17`): `<RFC3339 time> <LVL> <message> <fields sorted, error= first>` with the level as `TRC DBG INF WRN ERR FTL PNC` (`rs/zerolog globals.go:141-149@v1.33.0`, `:146@v1.34.0`; `console.go:388-395,203,211-214,451-462@v1.33.0`, `:403-410,210,218-221,466-470@v1.34.0`) and, with `NoColor` false and no `NO_COLOR` environment variable, ANSI colour codes around the timestamp, level and error-level message (`console.go:374-384,437,455-457,493@v1.33.0`), which `lib/logs.py` strips with `lib/mask.strip_control` before matching. JSON format (`--log.format=json`): `{"level":"error","<context fields>","error":"...","<event fields>","time":"<RFC3339>","message":"..."}` (`globals.go:31,34,45,57,60,105@v1.33.0`). ERROR_RE / NOT_ERROR_RE (`lib/logs.py:34-37`): the common-format error line `2026-11-04T12:00:00Z ERR Skipping service: no endpoints found ingress=foo namespace=default providerName=kubernetes serviceName=foo servicePort=80` is classified as an error only through the ` ERR ` alternative added for #129 (no `error`/`failed` word, no `level=`); the info line `2026-11-04T12:00:00Z INF Traefik version 3.3.6 built on 2025-04-18T09:09:38Z version=3.3.6` matches neither regex; in JSON, `"level":"error"` and `"level":"info"` classify both correctly. `_ts_of` finds the RFC3339 time in both formats (the regex is unanchored, so the colour codes around it do not matter).

| Signature | What kubegap relies on | Source | Holds for |
|---|---|---|---|
| `traefik.acme-failed` | `Unable to obtain ACME certificate for domains` (three sites) and `... for domain` (one), zerolog error level with `error=`, `domains=`, `providerName=<resolver>.acme`, `ACME CA=` fields | `pkg/provider/acme/provider.go:482,517,553,612@v3.3.2`, same `@v3.3.6`, `:499,534,570,629@v3.7.15`, `:499,534,570,629@v3.7.17`; `:38,495@v3.3.2`, `:42,512@v3.7.17` (resolverSuffix `.acme`, context fields) | all read versions, both formats |
| | `Error renewing certificate from LE: %v` (error level, renewal failed) and `Error renewing certificate from LE : %+v` (info level with `error=`, client creation failed) through v3.6.6; `Error renewing ACME certificate: %v` / `: %+v` from v3.6.7 | `provider.go:921,902@v3.3.2`, `@v3.3.6`; `:949,927@v3.6.6`; `:949,927@v3.6.7`; `:949,926@v3.7.15`; `:947,924@v3.7.17` | boundary v3.6.6 -> v3.6.7 (2026-01-14); v3.4.x and v3.5.x patch branches not bisected (no k3s version ships them) |
| | `acme.*error`: lego's problem text is `acme: error: <status> :: <method> :: <url> :: ...`, the `error=` value of the lines above. In common format `error=` is moved in front of the other fields, so `providerName=le.acme` follows it and the alternative matches only through the lego text; in JSON the context field `"providerName":"le.acme"` precedes `"error"` and every ACME line with an error field matches, including the info-level renewal line | `go-acme/lego acme/errors.go:36-40@v4.21.0`, `:134-140@v5.6.0`; `rs/zerolog console.go:211-214@v1.33.0`, `:218-221@v1.34.0` | all read versions |
| | lego's own lines (`[INFO] [example.com] acme: ...`) are emitted at DEBUG in v3.3.x (`legolog.Logger` wraps a `NoLevel(..., DebugLevel)` logger) and are not in the log at INFO; in v3.7.x lego v5 logs through slog at its own level with `lib=lego`, and its messages no longer start with `acme:` | `pkg/config/static/static_config.go:358-359@v3.3.2`, `pkg/logs/log.go:9-31@v3.3.2`, `go-acme/lego log/logger.go:52-58@v4.21.0`; `static_config.go:634-640@v3.7.17`, `go-acme/lego challenge/resolver/prober.go:240@v5.6.0` | as listed |
| `traefik.config-error` | `"level":"error"` (JSON) and ` ERR ` (common) are every zerolog error-level line | `rs/zerolog globals.go:34,45,146@v1.33.0`, `@v1.34.0`; `console.go:451-462@v1.33.0`, `:466-470@v1.34.0`; `cmd/traefik/logger.go:87@v3.3.2`, `:87@v3.3.6`, `:84@v3.7.15`, `:84@v3.7.17` | all read versions; ` ERR ` after `strip_control` |
| | Ingress provider: `Skipping service: no endpoints found` (only when `AllowEmptyServices` is false), `Cannot create service` with `error="service not found"` / `"service port not found"`, `Error configuring TLS` with `error="secret <ns>/<name> does not exist"`, `Cannot retrieve data`; all `logger.Error()` with `ingress=`/`namespace=` context | `pkg/provider/kubernetes/ingress/kubernetes.go:208,243,258,273,281,323,331,456,476,686@v3.3.2`, same `@v3.3.6`; `:160,251,283,309,317,382,390,565,585,833@v3.7.15`, same `@v3.7.17` | all read versions |
| | CRD provider: `error="kubernetes service not found: <ns>/<name>"`, `"middleware <ns>/<name> is not in the IngressRoute namespace <ns>"` (through v3.6), `"invalid reference to middleware <name>: ..."` (v3.7.0 on; the serversTransport form exists at every version), `"secret '<ns>/<name>' does not exist"`, all through `logger.Error().Err(err).Send()` (a line without message); `Error configuring TLS`, `Failed to create middleware keys`, `Cannot retrieve data`, `Secret %s/%s does not exist` (capital S, `Msgf`) | `pkg/provider/kubernetes/crd/kubernetes_http.go:47,83,105,148,176,193,411,443@v3.3.2`, same `@v3.3.6`, `:200@v3.6.0`, `:193@v3.7.0`, `:50,88,127,220,573,592,751@v3.7.15`, same `@v3.7.17`; `crd/kubernetes.go:201,866,1128@v3.3.2`, `:201,867,1129@v3.3.6`, `:186,732,1195,1464@v3.7.15`, `:186,735,1205,1479@v3.7.17` | boundary for the middleware wording between v3.6.0 and v3.7.0 (read at v3.6.0 and v3.7.0 only) |
| | `level=error` is the logrus text format of Traefik v2; no file under `pkg/` or `cmd/` prints it at v3.3.2 or v3.7.17. `unable to parse flushInterval: %w` is the only `unable to (parse|create|find)` text in the Kubernetes providers (plus `unable to parse number %s` in the ingress-nginx compatibility provider at v3.7.17); `invalid configuration` exists only in plugin setup | `crd/kubernetes_http.go:373@v3.3.2`, `:513@v3.7.17`; `pkg/provider/kubernetes/ingress-nginx/middleware.go:573@v3.7.17`; `pkg/plugins/plugins.go:19`, `pkg/server/middleware/plugins.go:22@v3.3.2`, `:21@v3.7.17` | dropped |

Notes that do not change a pattern:

- The ` ERR ` catch-all makes `traefik.config-error` count every error-level common-format line, so an ACME failure is counted by both Traefik signatures; the test lines record both ids.
- `Skipping service: no endpoints found` is guarded by `!p.AllowEmptyServices` (`ingress/kubernetes.go:278@v3.3.2`); with `--providers.kubernetesingress.allowEmptyServices=true` the service is created empty and the line is not printed.
- Traefik's access log is a separate option (`--accesslog`, off in the chart) and is not read by any signature.

Corrections found in source:

- `traefik.acme-failed` matched `Error renewing certificate`, which Traefik stopped printing at v3.6.7 (`provider.go:949@v3.6.7` reads `Error renewing ACME certificate: %v`; v3.6.6 still has `from LE`), so renewals failed silently on the k3s v1.37 image (3.7.15). The pattern is `Error renewing (ACME )?certificate`.
- `traefik.config-error` matched `level=error`, the Traefik v2 logrus spelling that v3 never prints (zerolog prints ` ERR ` in common format and `"level":"error"` in JSON); the catch-all is now `"level":"error"| ERR `, and the scanner strips zerolog's colour codes first.
- `traefik.config-error` matched `unable to (parse|create|find)` and `invalid configuration`, which in the Kubernetes providers only cover a malformed `flushInterval` (`crd/kubernetes_http.go:373@v3.3.2`) and plugin setup; a missing Service, Secret or Middleware prints `Cannot create service`, `service not found`, `Error configuring TLS`, `secret <ns>/<name> does not exist`, `kubernetes service not found`, `is not in the IngressRoute namespace` / `invalid reference to`, `Failed to create middleware keys`, `Cannot retrieve data`. Those phrases replaced the two.

### MetalLB

Versions read: v0.14.5 (the k3s cluster, released 2024-04-19), v0.15.3 (2025-12-04) and v0.16.2 (2026-11-04, newest). Libraries: `github.com/go-kit/log v0.2.1` (`go.mod:7@v0.14.5`, `:9@v0.15.3`, `:9@v0.16.2`); `github.com/hashicorp/memberlist v0.5.1` (`go.mod:9@v0.14.5`, `:11@v0.15.3`) and `v0.6.0` (`:11@v0.16.2`); `github.com/hashicorp/go-multierror v1.1.0` (`go.mod:70@v0.14.5`) and `v1.1.1` (`:92@v0.16.2`); `sigs.k8s.io/controller-runtime v0.17.3` / `v0.22.3` / `v0.25.1` (`go.mod:30@v0.14.5`, `:31@v0.15.3`, `:31@v0.16.2`); `go.uber.org/zap v1.27.0` (`go.mod:21@v0.14.5`) and `v1.28.0` (`:22@v0.16.2`); `github.com/go-logr/zapr v1.3.0` (`go.mod:53@v0.14.5`, `:63@v0.16.2`).

How a MetalLB line looks. Both the controller and the speaker build one go-kit JSON logger on stdout (`internal/logging/logging.go:58` at all three tags) with `ts` in RFC3339 (`:73-74`), a `level.NewFilter` from `--log-level` (`:75`; flag default `info`, `speaker/main.go:84@v0.14.5`, `:100@v0.16.2`; the manifests pass `--log-level=info`, `config/manifests/metallb-native.yaml:1645,1727@v0.14.5`, `:2213,2298@v0.16.2`) and `caller` (`:79`). The level value is `"error"`, `"warn"`, `"info"`, `"debug"` (`go-kit/log level/level.go:235@v0.2.1`). go-kit's JSON logger builds a `map[string]interface{}` and marshals it (`go-kit/log json_logger.go:23-32@v0.2.1`), so the keys are sorted alphabetically: `{"caller":"...","error":"...","level":"error","msg":"...","op":"...","ts":"..."}`. There is no format flag. klog output from client-go is captured through a pipe and re-emitted as the same JSON (`logging.go:62-66,123-124,170-178`). controller-runtime (webhooks, manager, event watcher) logs through the zap logger set at `logging.go:86-91` with the production JSON encoder (`kubernetes-sigs/controller-runtime pkg/log/zap/zap.go:81-86,171-200@v0.17.3`, `@v0.25.1`): keys `level`, `ts`, `logger`, `caller`, `msg`, lowercase level (`go.uber.org/zap config.go:126-135@v1.27.0`, `@v1.28.0`), RFC3339 `ts` (`zap.go:211-216@v0.17.3`, `:211-215@v0.25.1`). ERROR_RE / NOT_ERROR_RE (`lib/logs.py:34-37`): the error line `{"caller":"speakerlist.go:259","error":"1 error occurred:\n\t* Failed to join 192.168.0.101:7946: dial tcp 192.168.0.101:7946: i/o timeout\n\n","expected":1,"joined":0,"level":"error","msg":"partial join","op":"memberDiscovery","ts":"2026-11-04T12:42:50Z"}` is classified as an error by `"level":"error"`; the info line `{"caller":"speakerlist.go:261","level":"info","msg":"memberlist join successfully","number of other nodes":1,"op":"Member detection","ts":"2026-11-04T12:00:00Z"}` is exempted by `"level":"info"` even when `msg` says `failed`. `_ts_of` finds the `ts` value; newlines inside `error` are JSON-escaped so the line stays one line.

| Signature | What kubegap relies on | Source | Holds for |
|---|---|---|---|
| `metallb.pool-exhausted` | When no IP can be allocated the controller logs `level.Error` with `"msg":"IP allocation failed"` and `"error"` = the allocator's text, `no available IPs` (the fall-through of `Allocate`) or `no available IPs in pool <name>[ for <family> IPFamily]` | `controller/service.go:143@v0.14.5`, `:179@v0.15.3`, `:179@v0.16.2`; `internal/allocator/allocator.go:296,328@v0.14.5`, `:390,412@v0.15.3` and `allocation.go:89@v0.15.3`, `allocator.go:291,397,419@v0.16.2` and `allocation.go:89@v0.16.2` | all three, default `--log-level=info` (error passes the filter); the line is in the controller pod, which the glob `logs/pods/metallb/*.log` includes |
| | `AllocationFailed` is an apiserver Event: `c.client.Errorf(svc, "AllocationFailed", ...)` calls `c.events.Eventf(..., corev1.EventTypeWarning, ...)` on controller-runtime's recorder, whose watcher logs events at `V(1)`; MetalLB's zap level is `InfoLevel` at `--log-level=info` and zapr maps `V(1)` to zap level -1 (debug), so the Event is not in the log at the default level. The events summary (`lib/events.py`) carries the reason instead | `controller/service.go:144@v0.14.5`, `:180@v0.15.3`, `:180@v0.16.2`; `internal/k8s/k8s.go:438-440@v0.14.5`, `:506-508@v0.15.3`, `:500-502@v0.16.2`, `:169@v0.14.5`, `:187@v0.16.2` (`GetEventRecorderFor`); `kubernetes-sigs/controller-runtime pkg/internal/recorder/recorder.go:102-104@v0.17.3`, `:102-104@v0.22.3`, `:115-117,129@v0.25.1`; `internal/logging/logging.go:86-91,212-213` (all three); `go-logr/zapr zapr.go:187-193@v1.3.0` | all three |
| | `IP pool exhausted`, `assignment failed`, `address pool is full`, `no matching pool` appear in no file at any of the three tags (case-insensitive grep over `internal/`, `controller/`, `speaker/`); the nearest texts are `internal error: allocated IP has no matching address pool` (a speaker consistency check) and `no suitable pool for %s IPFamily` (swallowed inside `Allocate`, the caller sees `no available IPs`) | `speaker/main.go:354@v0.14.5`, `:389@v0.15.3`, `:451@v0.16.2`; `internal/allocator/allocator.go:345@v0.15.3`, `:352,380-397@v0.16.2` | dropped |
| `metallb.speaker-error` | `"level":"error"` is every go-kit error-level line of the speaker and the controller, and every controller-runtime zap error line in the controller pod; `partial join` is excluded by a negative lookahead, which is safe because the sorted keys put `level` before `msg` on every go-kit line | `internal/logging/logging.go:58` (all three); `go-kit/log level/level.go:235@v0.2.1`, `json_logger.go:23-32@v0.2.1`; `logging.go:91` (zap logger), `go.uber.org/zap config.go:127,134@v1.27.0`, `@v1.28.0`, `controller-runtime pkg/log/zap/zap.go:82@v0.17.3`, `@v0.25.1` | all three |
| | `failed to announce service` (speaker, `op=setBalancer`), `failed to send ARP reply` (ARP responder and, with the same text, the NDP responder), `failed to send BGP update`, `failed to send BGP withdraw`, `failed to send keepalive` (native BGP) | `speaker/main.go:392@v0.14.5`, `:427@v0.15.3`, `:489@v0.16.2`; `internal/layer2/arp.go:113`, `internal/layer2/ndp.go:151` (all three); `internal/bgp/native/native.go:142,175,191,392@v0.14.5`, `:154,187,203,404@v0.15.3`, `:156,189,205,406@v0.16.2` | all three; the BGP lines only with the native BGP implementation (the FRR mode logs through FRR) |
| | `level=error` never prints (no logfmt mode); `failed to bind` appears at no tag; `arp.*(error|failed)` matched the `arp.go` line only because the sorted `"caller":"arp.go:113"` key precedes `"error"`, while the ARP texts themselves are capitalised (`failed to send ARP reply`, `failed to make gratuitous ARP announcement`) | `logging.go:58`; `internal/layer2/announcer.go:221@v0.14.5`, `:221@v0.15.3`, `:299@v0.16.2` | dropped |
| `metallb.speaker-peer-unreachable` | Every minute (and on each speaker-pod change) the speaker lists the speaker pods' IPs from the API server, subtracts the current memberlist members and calls `memberlist.Join` on the rest; when a join fails or joins fewer than expected it logs `level.Error` `"msg":"partial join"` with `joined`, `expected` and the join error. memberlist formats each failed address as `Failed to join %s: %v` (lowercase `failed to join` from v0.6.0) and go-multierror wraps them as `1 error occurred:\n\t* ...\n\n` | `internal/speakerlist/speakerlist.go:195,208,236-262@v0.14.5`, `:205,218,269@v0.15.3`, `:222,235,286@v0.16.2`; `hashicorp/memberlist memberlist.go:270,286@v0.5.1`, `:286@v0.6.0`; `hashicorp/go-multierror format.go:14-17@v1.1.0`, `:16@v1.1.1` | all three, default log level |

Notes that do not change a pattern:

- `partial join` is a real failure to reach a peer, but it describes the cluster (a node down, or 7946 blocked), not an announcement failure: a speaker pod on a NotReady node stays in the API server's list until it is evicted, so the join to its IP times out once a minute. The k3s cluster logged 486 such lines in 24 hours while its second node was offline. The separate signature keeps the count visible without inflating `metallb.speaker-error`.
- The `logs/pods/metallb/*.log` glob covers both workloads of the namespace, so `metallb.speaker-error`'s catch-all also counts controller-pod error lines (go-kit and controller-runtime zap). The test lines record this.
- With `--log-level=debug` the Event watcher line would appear as a zap JSON line with `"reason":"AllocationFailed"`; at the default level it does not.

Corrections found in source:

- `metallb.pool-exhausted` had six alternatives of which four (`IP pool exhausted`, `assignment failed`, `address pool is full`, `no matching pool`) are printed by no version and one (`AllocationFailed`) is an apiserver Event that controller-runtime logs only at `V(1)`, below the default level. Only `no available IPs` ever matched. The pattern is now `IP allocation failed|no available IPs`, the controller's error line (`controller/service.go:143@v0.14.5`, `:179@v0.15.3`, `:179@v0.16.2`), and `AllocationFailed` moves to the events summary.
- `metallb.speaker-error` matched `level=error` (never printed: `logging.go:58` always builds a JSON logger), `failed to bind` (no tag prints it) and `arp.*(error|failed)` (matched one line by the accident of sorted keys); these are dropped. Its catch-all counted `partial join` (`speakerlist.go:259@v0.14.5`), one line per minute while a peer speaker is unreachable, which on the k3s cluster made a node outage look like 486 announcement errors; the catch-all now excludes it and the new `metallb.speaker-peer-unreachable` (severity low, warn at 60) counts it.

## PromQL queries, part 1: node-exporter, cAdvisor, kubelet, kube-state-metrics (#121)

Every query of `collectors/*/metrics.yaml` that reads a `node_*`, `container_*`, `kubelet_*` or `kube_*` series carries a `source:` list next to it: one entry per metric name, `<name>: <owner>/<repo> <file>:<line>@<tag>, :<line>@<tag>, ... (labels, default state)`, and one entry per label that the scrape configuration sets rather than the exporter. 82 queries (69 in `collectors/core/metrics.yaml`, 5 in `collectors/flannel/metrics.yaml`, 3 in `collectors/local-path-provisioner/metrics.yaml`, 5 in `collectors/prometheus/metrics.yaml`) read 56 names. `tests/test_sources.py` requires the list on every such query, an entry for each name the query reads, none of the two names `GONE` lists (removed or off by default), and that every version tag the entries cite appears in this section. The apiserver, CoreDNS, kube-proxy, Flannel and EBS CSI names are #122; the Prometheus, Alertmanager, Loki, Promtail, Fluent Bit and Grafana names are #123.

Versions read:

| Exporter | EKS cluster | k3s cluster | Newest release | Read for a boundary |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| node-exporter (`prometheus/node_exporter`) | v1.7.0 | v1.10.2 | v1.12.1 | — |
| kube-state-metrics (`kubernetes/kube-state-metrics`) | v2.10.1 | v2.17.0 | v2.20.0 | v2.11.0, v2.12.0, v2.13.0, v2.14.0, v2.15.0, v2.16.0 (`internal/store/endpoint.go`) |
| kubelet (`kubernetes/kubernetes`) | v1.32.13 | v1.33.5 | v1.37.2 | — |
| cAdvisor (`google/cadvisor`, the version each kubelet's `go.mod` pins) | v0.51.0 (`go.mod:38@v1.32.13`) | v0.52.1 (`go.mod:35@v1.33.5`) | lib v0.60.5 (`go.mod:32@v1.37.2`) | v0.53.0 |
| Prometheus (`prometheus/prometheus`, for the `instance` label) | v2.49.1 | v3.7.3 | — | — |
| prometheus-operator (`prometheus-operator/prometheus-operator`, for the `node` label on kubelet series) | v0.71.0 (kube-prometheus-stack 56.0.0, `charts/kube-prometheus-stack/Chart.yaml:27`) | v0.86.2 (kube-prometheus-stack 79.5.0, `Chart.yaml:36`) | — | — |

The rows below are the facts that are not per name; the per-name lines are in the `source:` entries.

| Fact | kubegap | Source | Holds for |
|---|---|---|---|
| node-exporter names are `node_<subsystem>_<name>`: `BuildFQName` with the package constant `namespace = "node"` | every `node_*` entry | `prometheus/node_exporter collector/collector.go:30@v1.7.0`, `:29@v1.10.2`, `:29@v1.12.1` | v1.7.0, v1.10.2, v1.12.1 |
| The nine collectors kubegap reads (stat, conntrack, timex, filesystem, netdev, netclass, uname, meminfo, sockstat) are registered `defaultEnabled`; `processes` is registered `defaultDisabled`, so `node_processes_state` is absent on a default install and an empty result reads as "no zombies" | the register line in each entry; GONE in `tests/test_sources.py` | `collector/processes_linux.go:46@v1.7.0`, `:45@v1.10.2`, `:44@v1.12.1` against, for example, `collector/stat_linux.go:43@v1.7.0` | v1.7.0, v1.10.2, v1.12.1 |
| `node_scrape_collector_success` is written once per enabled collector from the `Collect` loop, so a disabled collector has no success series either and cannot be told apart from a collector that never ran. Observed: `node_scrape_collector_success{collector="processes"}` has no series on either cluster | nothing reads it; the zombie query was removed instead | `collector/collector.go:39-40,147-150@v1.7.0`, `:39,146-149,158-176@v1.12.1` | v1.7.0, v1.12.1 |
| The netdev counters are named from the reader's map keys, `node_network_<key>_total`; the keys `receive_errors`, `receive_dropped`, `transmit_errors`, `transmit_dropped` are renamed to `receive_errs`, `receive_drop`, `transmit_errs`, `transmit_drop` by `legacy()` unless `--collector.netdev.enable-detailed-metrics` is set. With the flag on, the four queries reading `*_errs_total` and `*_drop_total` are empty. Observed: the flag is off on both clusters (`node_network_receive_errors_total` has no series) | queries node_network_errors_1d, node_network_errors_5m in `collectors/core/metrics.yaml`; flannel_if_errors_1d, flannel_if_drops_1d in `collectors/flannel/metrics.yaml` | `collector/netdev_common.go:39,102,118-120,193-213@v1.7.0`, `:38,101,123-125,208-228@v1.10.2`, `:36,99,121-123,210-230@v1.12.1`; keys `collector/netdev_linux.go:112-115,168-177@v1.7.0` | v1.7.0, v1.10.2, v1.12.1 with the flag off |
| `node_memory_<field>_bytes`: v1.7.0 parses `/proc/meminfo` itself and appends `_bytes` to every field with a kB unit; v1.10.2 and v1.12.1 read procfs's `Meminfo` struct and spell `MemAvailable_bytes`, `MemTotal_bytes`, `SwapTotal_bytes` as literals. The names are the same either way | queries node_memory_available_ratio, node_swap_total_bytes | `collector/meminfo_linux.go:60-67@v1.7.0`; `:122,128,167@v1.10.2`; `:121,127,166@v1.12.1`; `collector/meminfo.go:63@v1.7.0`, `:52@v1.10.2`, `:50@v1.12.1` | v1.7.0, v1.10.2, v1.12.1 |
| `node_filesystem_*` carry `device`, `mountpoint`, `fstype`; v1.10.2 adds `device_error`. kubegap filters on `fstype` and `mountpoint` only, so the extra label changes nothing | queries disk_usage_ratio, inode_usage_ratio; the three in `collectors/local-path-provisioner/metrics.yaml` | `collector/filesystem_common.go:63@v1.7.0` against `:70@v1.10.2`, `:68@v1.12.1` | v1.7.0, v1.10.2, v1.12.1 |
| `node_sockstat_TCP_tw` is `<protocol>_<pair>` of the sockstat collector: protocol `TCP`, pair `tw` | query node_tcp_timewait | `collector/sockstat_linux.go:136,171-174@v1.7.0`, `:135,170-173@v1.10.2`, `:134,169-172@v1.12.1` | v1.7.0, v1.10.2, v1.12.1 |
| kube-state-metrics series carry the resource's default labels: `namespace, pod, uid` on `kube_pod_*`, `node` on `kube_node_*`, `namespace, endpoint` on `kube_endpoint_*`, `namespace, horizontalpodautoscaler`, `namespace, secret`, `namespace, poddisruptionbudget` on theirs; the per-series labels kubegap filters on (`container`, `reason`, `phase`, `condition`, `qos_class`, `resource`, `unit`, `ready`, `type`, `node` on `kube_pod_info` and the request and limit series) are at the lines in each entry | every `kube_*` entry | `kubernetes/kube-state-metrics internal/store/pod.go:39@v2.10.1`, `:39@v2.17.0`, `:41@v2.20.0`; `internal/store/node.go:42@v2.10.1`; `internal/store/endpoint.go:40@v2.10.1`; `internal/store/horizontalpodautoscaler.go:51@v2.10.1`; `internal/store/secret.go:39@v2.10.1`, `:40@v2.17.0`; `internal/store/poddisruptionbudget.go:35@v2.10.1` (the same lines at v2.17.0 and v2.20.0 unless given) | v2.10.1, v2.17.0, v2.20.0 |
| `kube_endpoint_address_not_ready` exists up to 2.13.0, as the sum over subsets of not-ready addresses times ports, and is gone since 2.14.0; at every version the not-ready addresses are the `ready="false"` series of `kube_endpoint_address` (none for a subset without ports since 2.14.0) | query endpoint_not_ready_addresses; GONE in `tests/test_sources.py` | `internal/store/endpoint.go:148,155-157@v2.10.1`, `:148,155-157@v2.13.0`, absent at `@v2.14.0`, `@v2.17.0`, `@v2.20.0`; `:176-188@v2.10.1`, `:134-153@v2.14.0` | v2.10.1 to v2.20.0 |
| Since 2.14.0 `kube_endpoint_address` has one series per address and port, with `port_protocol`, `port_number`, `port_name`; before, one per address. A count of addresses has to count distinct `ip`, or a Service with two ports counts every address twice | queries endpoint_ready_addresses, endpoint_not_ready_addresses | `internal/store/endpoint.go:136-153@v2.14.0` against `:176-188@v2.13.0` | v2.10.1 to v2.20.0 |
| `kube_pod_container_status_waiting_reason` has a series only while a container is waiting, and `kube_pod_container_status_last_terminated_reason` only while it has a last terminated state; an empty result means no such container, not a missing metric. Observed: the waiting series was absent from the EKS Prometheus at the time of reading and present on k3s | queries pods_waiting_reason, oomkilled_last_terminated | `internal/store/pod.go:535-539@v2.10.1`, `:564-570@v2.17.0`; `:334-338@v2.10.1` | v2.10.1, v2.17.0 |
| `kube_pod_status_ready{condition="true"}` is one of the three series (`true`, `false`, `unknown`) `addConditionMetrics` writes per pod | query pod_ready_ratio_24h | `internal/store/pod.go:1483-1487@v2.10.1`, `:1517-1521@v2.17.0`, `:1682-1686@v2.20.0` | v2.10.1, v2.17.0, v2.20.0 |
| `kube_node_status_allocatable` carries `resource` and `unit`: cpu in cores (`core`), memory and ephemeral storage in bytes (`byte`), pods as an integer; the container request and limit series carry the same two plus `container` and `node` | queries node_cpu_request_ratio, node_memory_request_ratio, kubelet_running_pods, large_request_containers and the request and limit ratios | `internal/store/node.go:318-347,381@v2.17.0`; labels `:356@v2.10.1`, `:410@v2.20.0`; `internal/store/pod.go:282@v2.10.1`, `:285@v2.17.0`, `:294@v2.20.0` | v2.10.1, v2.17.0, v2.20.0 |
| kubelet names are `kubelet_<key>` (subsystem `kubelet`); `kubelet_pleg_relist_duration_seconds` is a histogram, so the query reads `_bucket` with `le`; the volume stats collector labels `namespace` and `persistentvolumeclaim` | queries kubelet_pleg_p99_1h, kubelet_running_pods, pvc_usage_ratio, pvc_inode_ratio, pvc_capacity_bytes, pvc_available_bytes, pvc_growth_bytes_per_day_7d; prom_pvc_used_ratio in `collectors/prometheus/metrics.yaml` | `kubernetes/kubernetes pkg/kubelet/metrics/metrics.go:34,50,322@v1.32.13`, `:34,50,330@v1.33.5`, `:36,52,414@v1.37.2`; `pkg/kubelet/metrics/collectors/volume_stats.go:30-63@v1.32.13`, `:30-63@v1.33.5`, `:30-63@v1.37.2` | v1.32.13, v1.33.5, v1.37.2 |
| The `container_*` series come from the cAdvisor version each kubelet pins, served by the kubelet at `/metrics/cadvisor`; the `container`, `pod` and `namespace` labels are set by the kubelet's label function, `id` by cAdvisor. The kubelet includes the CPU, memory, network and OOM metric sets, and cAdvisor emits each of the six names inside the matching `includedMetrics.Has` block. 0.60.x moved the file to `lib/metrics/prometheus.go`; the six names kubegap reads are unchanged from v0.51.0 to v0.60.5 | every `container_*` entry | `kubernetes/kubernetes go.mod:38@v1.32.13`, `:35@v1.33.5`, `:32@v1.37.2`; `pkg/kubelet/server/server.go:98,1162,1189-1194@v1.32.13`, `:102,1199,1226-1231@v1.33.5`, `:112,1462,1489-1494@v1.37.2`; `pkg/kubelet/cadvisor/cadvisor_linux.go:85-93@v1.32.13`, `:87-95@v1.33.5`, `:94-100@v1.37.2`; `google/cadvisor metrics/prometheus.go:137,164,191,203,371,434,772,775,1738,1740,1789@v0.51.0`, `:142,169,196,208,376,439,777,780,1743,1745,1842@v0.52.1`, `:142,169,196,208,376,439,777,780,1743,1745@v0.53.0`, `lib/metrics/prometheus.go:147,180,210,225,444,531,1063,1066,2086,2088,2205@v0.60.5` | kubelet v1.32.13 to v1.37.2 |
| `instance` is set by Prometheus to the target address when the target has no `instance` label of its own, so on node-exporter series it is `<node IP>:9100`, which the summaries map to a node by IP. A scrape configuration that relabels `instance` changes what the node queries group by | `lib/evaluate.py`, `lib/flannelctl.py`, `lib/localpath.py`, `lib/resilience.py`, `lib/kubeproxy.py` | `prometheus/prometheus scrape/target.go:507-510@v2.49.1`, `:519-522@v3.7.3` | v2.49.1, v3.7.3; observed `<IP>:9100` on both clusters |
| `node` on `kubelet_*` series comes from the prometheus-operator: every ServiceMonitor target gets `node` from an Endpoints address whose `targetRef.kind` is `Node`, and the kubelet Endpoints the operator writes carry that reference. The meta labels the relabeling reads are set by Prometheus's endpoints discovery from the address's `targetRef`. A scrape configuration that does not set `node` leaves the `on (node)` match of kubelet_running_pods and kubelet_pleg_p99_1h with nothing to match, so those queries are empty | queries kubelet_running_pods, kubelet_pleg_p99_1h | `prometheus-operator/prometheus-operator pkg/prometheus/promcfg.go:1310-1314@v0.71.0`, `:2000-2004@v0.86.2`; `prometheus/prometheus discovery/kubernetes/endpoints.go:254,285-287@v2.49.1`, `:331,366-368@v3.7.3`; `pkg/kubelet/controller.go:155@v0.71.0`, `:290,303@v0.86.2`; `prometheus-community/helm-charts charts/kube-prometheus-stack/Chart.yaml:27@kube-prometheus-stack-56.0.0`, `:36@kube-prometheus-stack-79.5.0` | the two operator versions; observed on both clusters |

Observed on the two clusters (read-only instant queries through kubegap's port-forward, 2026-11-04, and the latest full snapshots):

- 56 of the 58 names the queries read before this change had series on each cluster. k3s (kube-state-metrics v2.17.0, node-exporter v1.10.2): absent `kube_endpoint_address_not_ready` (removed in 2.14.0) and `node_processes_state` (collector off). EKS (v2.10.1, v1.7.0): absent `kube_pod_container_status_waiting_reason` (no container was waiting) and `node_processes_state`.
- `kube_endpoint_address{ready="false"}` had no series on either cluster at the time (no address was out of readiness), `ready="true"` had 111 (k3s) and 138 (EKS). The corrected query's non-empty path is read from the label code in source, not seen on a cluster. `kubelet_running_pods` carries `node` on both; `node_uname_info` carries `instance` as `<IP>:9100` on both.
- In the latest full snapshots (k3s 2026-10-27, EKS 2026-11-03) the queries with no series were, k3s: oomkilled_last_terminated, oom_events_7d, hpa_at_max, pods_pending, zombie_processes, node_network_errors_1d, node_network_errors_5m, large_request_containers, pod_pending_seconds, endpoint_not_ready_addresses, prom_oomkilled, prom_pvc_used_ratio (local-path volumes report no stats), monitoring_oomkilled; EKS: hpa_at_max, pods_waiting_reason, pods_pending, zombie_processes, node_network_errors_5m, pod_pending_seconds, pod_ready_ratio_24h, endpoint_not_ready_addresses, monitoring_restarts_7d. Apart from the two defects, each has a filter (`> 0`, `== 1`, `< 0.99`, a phase or reason) and its name exists on the cluster, so the empty result is the situation not occurring. cpu_throttle_ratio_24h, oom_pods_7d, pod_info_7d and nodes_seen_1d were added after those snapshots were taken.

Corrections found in source:

- `endpoint_not_ready_addresses` read `kube_endpoint_address_not_ready`, gone since kube-state-metrics 2.14.0. On the k3s cluster (2.17.0) it was empty for ever and the traffic summary's `endpoints_not_ready` list (report material; no check reads it) stayed empty whatever the endpoints did. It reads `kube_endpoint_address{ready="false"}` now. The old gauge was not-ready addresses times ports, so on 2.10.1 to 2.13.0 the list's number changes meaning from that product to the address count.
- `endpoint_ready_addresses` counted series; since 2.14.0 that is addresses times ports. Both endpoint queries count distinct `ip` now. No check reads `endpoint_ready_addresses` (`lib/traffic.py` counts ready addresses from the Endpoints objects); its `why` said otherwise and is corrected.

What stays open: kubegap does not ask Prometheus whether a name exists, so an empty result from a name a later release removes is still told apart only by reading that release's source; `GONE` lists the two names found so far, it is a regression guard, not a mechanism.
- `zombie_processes` read `node_processes_state`, which the default node-exporter never emits; nothing read the result. The query is removed; the zombie count `node.zombie-processes` judges comes from `collectors/core/node-os.sh`.

## PromQL queries, part 2: apiserver, CoreDNS, kube-proxy, EBS CSI, the kubelet volume metrics, `up` (#122)

Every query of `collectors/*/metrics.yaml` that reads an `apiserver_*`, `coredns_*`, `kubeproxy_*`, `aws_ebs_csi_*`, `cloudprovider_aws_*`, `csi_operations_seconds_*`, `storage_operation_duration_seconds_*`, `rest_client_requests_total` or `up` series carries a `source:` list in the format of #121: one entry per metric name, one entry per label the scrape configuration sets (`job`, `instance`). 49 queries (13 apiserver and 2 CoreDNS in `collectors/core/metrics.yaml`, 14 in `collectors/coredns/metrics.yaml`, 13 in `collectors/kube-proxy/metrics.yaml`, 7 in `collectors/ebs-csi/metrics.yaml`). The five Flannel queries read node-exporter's `node_network_*` and were covered by #121; Flannel emits no metrics of its own.

Versions read:

| Project | EKS cluster | k3s cluster | Newest release | Read for a boundary |

| kube-apiserver, kube-proxy, kubelet volume metrics, client-go restclient, component-base (`kubernetes/kubernetes`) | v1.32.13 (apiserver, kubelet), v1.32.0 (the kube-proxy image) | v1.33.5 | v1.37.2 | v1.30.14, v1.31.14, v1.33.0, v1.33.13, v1.34.13, v1.35.10, v1.36.6 (the newest patch of every minor from 1.30 to 1.37) |
| CoreDNS (`coredns/coredns`) | v1.11.3 | v1.12.3 | v1.15.0 | v1.10.1, v1.11.0, v1.11.1 (the forward-to-proxy rename), v1.14.6 (what kubeadm 1.37 ships) |
| client-go (`kubernetes/client-go`, the version each CoreDNS `go.mod` pins) | v0.29.3 (`go.mod@v1.11.3`) | v0.32.5 (`go.mod@v1.12.3`) | v0.37.1 (`go.mod@v1.15.0`) | — |
| EBS CSI driver (`kubernetes-sigs/aws-ebs-csi-driver`) | v1.37.0 (installed from the chart, not as an EKS add-on) | — | v1.67.2 | v1.38.0, v1.38.1 (the `aws_ebs_csi_` names; v1.38.1 is the release that announces them), v1.40.0, v1.41.0 (old names off by default; request histogram initialized at start), v1.42.0 (that initialization controller-only), v1.53.0 (unchanged) |
| kube-prometheus-stack (`prometheus-community/helm-charts`, for the `job` labels and the apiserver bucket drop) | kube-prometheus-stack-56.0.0 | kube-prometheus-stack-79.5.0 | — | — |
| prometheus-operator (`prometheus-operator/prometheus-operator`, `pkg/prometheus/promcfg.go`) | v0.71.0 | v0.86.2 | — | — |
| Prometheus (`prometheus/prometheus`, for `up` and `instance`) | v2.49.1 | v3.7.3 | — | — |
| k3s (`k3s-io/k3s`, the embedded apiserver, kubelet and kube-proxy) | — | v1.33.5+k3s1 | — | — |

The prometheus-operator turns a ServiceMonitor `jobLabel` into the `job` label through a `__meta_kubernetes_service_label_<label>` relabeling. On k3s the apiserver, kubelet and kube-proxy run in one process with one metrics registry, so the kubelet scrape (`job="kubelet"`) serves every apiserver series a second time and a `sum()` without a job filter doubled: observed 1274 open watches for one server node, 637 under each job. Every apiserver query filters `job="apiserver"` now; a scrape configuration that names the job differently leaves them empty. | kube-prometheus-stack's default apiserver ServiceMonitor drops the `apiserver_request_duration_seconds_bucket` series whose `le` is in a fixed list (0.15, 0.2, 0.3, ... 50), so under that default the verb p99 is interpolated over the remaining buckets. kubegap reads what is there | query apiserver_p99_latency_by_verb; check `controlplane.apiserver-latency` | `charts/kube-prometheus-stack/values.yaml:1172-1178@kube-prometheus-stack-56.0.0`, `:1570-1576@kube-prometheus-stack-79.5.0` | kube-prometheus-stack 56.0.0 and 79.5.0 |
