| Since 1.33 the two restore-failure counters are `CounterVec`s with `ip_family`, and a `CounterVec` child series exists only after its first `.Inc()`; up to 1.32 they are plain `Counter`s that exist from start at 0. The partial counter is registered in iptables mode only, the full one also in ipvs. A `sum by (instance)(A) + sum by (instance)(B)` is empty whenever one side has no series: on every healthy 1.33+ node, and after one full-restore failure on 1.33+ as long as no partial failure happened, and in ipvs mode always. Observed: both names absent from the k3s 1.33.5 node's `/metrics` and from Prometheus; both present on every EKS 1.32.0 node at 0. Two queries read the counters and `lib/kubeproxy.py` adds them per node (a rate over a `__name__` selector matching both is refused by Prometheus: `increase()` drops `__name__` and the label sets collide) | queries kubeproxy_restore_failures_24h, kubeproxy_partial_restore_failures_24h; `lib/kubeproxy.py`; check `kubeproxy.restore-failures` | `pkg/proxy/metrics/metrics.go:154,165@v1.32.13`, `:159-181@v1.33.5`, `:161-180@v1.37.2`, `:310-311,316@v1.32.13`, `:343-344,351@v1.33.5`; `pkg/proxy/iptables/proxier.go:456,483,823,1555@v1.33.5`; `pkg/proxy/ipvs/proxier.go:1435@v1.33.5` | v1.30.14 to v1.32.13 (plain counters), v1.33.0 to v1.37.2 (vectors) |
| `kubeproxy_sync_proxy_rules_no_local_endpoints_total` is a gauge of the number of Services with a Local traffic policy and no endpoint on the node, labels `traffic_policy` (and `ip_family` since 1.33), at every version read. `increase()` over it measured nothing, and the `why` and the evidence called it dropped traffic. The query reads its current value per node and policy now and the summary key is `no_local_endpoint_services`. Observed on k3s: one node with 2 external-policy Services without a local endpoint | query kubeproxy_no_local_endpoint_services (max over the IP families: a lower bound of distinct Services, since a dual-stack Service appears in both); check `kubeproxy.conntrack-invalid-drops`; `lib/kubeproxy.py` | `pkg/proxy/metrics/metrics.go:226-233@v1.30.14`, `:269-276@v1.33.5`, `:271-278@v1.37.2` | v1.30.14 to v1.37.2 |
| `kubeproxy_iptables_ct_state_invalid_dropped_packets_total` does not exist at 1.30; since 1.31 it is a custom collector over the nfacct counter `ct_state_invalid_dropped_pkts`, registered in iptables mode only and only when nfacct is available, and the packets are counted by an iptables rule with `-m nfacct`. An empty result is 1.30, another proxy mode or a kernel without nfacct as well as zero drops; since #140 the summary reports null with `ct_invalid_reason` and the check skips (the row in the Store HTTP APIs section) | query kubeproxy_ct_invalid_dropped_1h; check `kubeproxy.conntrack-invalid-drops` | `pkg/proxy/metrics/metrics.go:147,303@v1.31.14`, `:152,335@v1.33.5`, `:154,417@v1.37.2`; `pkg/proxy/iptables/proxier.go:1489-1491@v1.33.5`; absent from `pkg/proxy/metrics/metrics.go@v1.30.14` | v1.31.14 to v1.37.2 |
| `kubeproxy_sync_proxy_rules_iptables_total` carries `table` up to 1.32 and `table, ip_family` since 1.33, so on a dual-stack 1.33+ node there are two series per table and a `max` picked the larger family; the query sums them now (identical on single stack and before 1.33). Observed on k3s: IPv4 and IPv6 series, 12 in all for 3 scrape instances | query kubeproxy_iptables_rules; check `kubeproxy.iptables-scale` | `pkg/proxy/metrics/metrics.go:179-183@v1.32.13`, `:186-190@v1.33.5`, `:188-192@v1.37.2` | v1.30.14 to v1.37.2 |
| k3s runs kube-proxy inside its own process next to the kubelet and the apiserver, with one metrics registry, so the kube-proxy series arrive through the kubelet (`:10250`) and apiserver (`:6443`) scrapes with those `instance` values, twice for a server node. `lib/kubeproxy.py` maps each instance to a node by IP and keeps the max over the ports, so the per-node numbers are right; the cluster-wide p99 sums the duplicated buckets, which does not change a quantile | `lib/kubeproxy.py`; every kube-proxy query grouped by instance | `k3s-io/k3s pkg/daemons/executor/embed.go:77,99-101,126@v1.33.5+k3s1`; `prometheus/prometheus scrape/target.go:507-510@v2.49.1`, `:519-522@v3.7.3`; the one registry is component-base's package-level default, `staging/src/k8s.io/component-base/metrics/legacyregistry/registry.go:31@v1.30.14`, `:31@v1.33.5`, `:31@v1.37.2`, which kube-proxy registers into (`pkg/proxy/metrics/metrics.go:303@v1.31.14`, `:335@v1.33.5`, `:417@v1.37.2`) | k3s v1.33.5+k3s1 |
| `rest_client_requests_total{code,method,host}` is the client-go request counter that component-base registers in every Kubernetes component, so the kube-proxy query filters on the kube-proxy job and is empty where no such job exists (k3s) | query kubeproxy_rest_client_errors_1h | `staging/src/k8s.io/component-base/metrics/prometheus/restclient/metrics.go:90-94@v1.30.14`, `:90-94@v1.37.2` | v1.30.14 to v1.37.2 |
| The kubelet's `csi_operations_seconds` (ALPHA histogram, subsystem `csi`) carries `driver_name, method_name, grpc_status_code, migrated`, with `method_name` the full gRPC method (`/csi.v1.Node/NodeStageVolume`) and `grpc_status_code` `OK` or the gRPC code of the error; `storage_operation_duration_seconds` carries `volume_plugin, operation_name, status, migrated`, and for a CSI volume `volume_plugin` is `kubernetes.io/csi:<driver>`. The former `volume_plugin=~"kubernetes.io/csi.*"` matched every CSI driver, so with EFS or another driver installed its mount failures counted as EBS; the two queries match `kubernetes.io/csi:ebs.csi.aws.com` exactly now | queries ebscsi_node_ops_by_status_24h, ebscsi_node_ops_p99_24h, ebscsi_storage_ops_by_status_24h, ebscsi_storage_ops_p99_24h; checks `ebscsi.node-ops-errors`, `ebscsi.stage-latency` | `pkg/volume/util/metrics.go:48-53,68-74,120-129,140-145@v1.30.14`, `:49-54,69-75,127-136,148-154@v1.37.2`; `pkg/volume/csi/csi_plugin.go:52@v1.30.14`, `:54@v1.37.2` | v1.30.14 to v1.37.2 |
| The EBS CSI controller's EC2 request metrics are `cloudprovider_aws_api_request_errors{request}`, `cloudprovider_aws_api_throttled_requests_total{operation_name}` and `cloudprovider_aws_api_request_duration_seconds{request}` up to v1.37.0, and `aws_ebs_csi_api_request_errors_total` (with `code` too since v1.41.0), `aws_ebs_csi_api_request_throttles_total`, `aws_ebs_csi_api_request_duration_seconds` from the v1.38.0 tag (`pkg/cloud/handlers.go` identical at v1.38.0 and v1.38.1; v1.38.1 is the release that announces them); the old names stay behind `--deprecated-metrics`, default true at v1.38.1 and false since v1.41.0. The metrics server runs only with `--http-endpoint`, which the chart sets from `controller.enableMetrics`, false by default. The controller registry is a fresh `prometheus.NewRegistry`, so the endpoint has no `go_*` or `process_*` series; since v1.38.1 the node plugin's NVMe metrics carry the `aws_ebs_csi_` prefix too, so a count over `aws_ebs_csi_.*` is not a controller signal; since v1.41.0 the request histogram is initialized at start for the 16 known request values (from `InitializeRecorder`, in every mode; v1.42.0 moved the call under the controller mode), before that a series appears after the first EC2 call. The three controller queries read each counter under either name and the presence query counts the request histogram | queries ebscsi_controller_metrics_series, ebscsi_api_errors_24h, ebscsi_api_throttles_24h; check `ebscsi.controller-metrics-disabled`; `lib/ebscsi.py` | `kubernetes-sigs/aws-ebs-csi-driver pkg/cloud/handlers.go:45-54,93@v1.37.0`, `:45-62,102@v1.38.1`, `:45-63@v1.41.0`, `:44-62,101@v1.67.2`; `pkg/metrics/constants.go:19-30@v1.67.2`; `pkg/driver/options.go:101@v1.37.0`, `:103,118@v1.38.1`, `:103,118@v1.41.0`, `:112,132@v1.67.2`; `cmd/main.go:157@v1.37.0`, `:139,243@v1.67.2`; `pkg/metrics/metrics.go:47@v1.37.0`, `:28,51@v1.38.1`, `:66-75,213-229@v1.41.0`, `:213@v1.42.0`, `:270@v1.53.0`, `:29,74,287@v1.67.2`; `cmd/main.go:161-163@v1.42.0`; `pkg/cloud/handlers.go:52@v1.41.0`, `:51@v1.67.2` (the `code` label); `pkg/metrics/nvme.go:41-60@v1.38.1`, `:41-60@v1.67.2`; `charts/aws-ebs-csi-driver/values.yaml:231@v1.37.0`, `:254@v1.67.2`; `charts/aws-ebs-csi-driver/templates/controller.yaml:86@v1.37.0`, `:91@v1.67.2`; `CHANGELOG.md` sections v1.38.1 and v1.41.0 at `@v1.67.2` | v1.37.0, v1.38.0, v1.38.1, v1.40.0, v1.41.0, v1.42.0, v1.53.0, v1.67.2 |
| Flannel emits no metrics; the five queries of `collectors/flannel/metrics.yaml` read node-exporter's `node_network_*` for the `flannel.1` and `cni0` interfaces and carry the #121 citations | `collectors/flannel/metrics.yaml`; `lib/flannelctl.py` | `prometheus/node_exporter collector/netdev_common.go:102@v1.7.0`, `:99@v1.12.1` (see part 1) | node-exporter v1.7.0, v1.10.2, v1.12.1 |

Observed on the two clusters (read-only instant queries through kubegap's port-forward, 2026-11-04, and the latest full snapshots):

- Every name the 49 queries read had series on the cluster that has the component, with these exceptions: on k3s (Kubernetes 1.33.5) `kubeproxy_sync_proxy_rules_iptables_restore_failures_total` and `_partial_restore_failures_total` (no failure yet, see the row), `csi_operations_seconds` (no CSI driver), and no `up{job=~".*kube-proxy.*"}` (no kube-proxy Service); on EKS no `aws_ebs_csi_*` and no `cloudprovider_aws_*` series (driver v1.37.0, metrics off). `coredns_forward_request_duration_seconds` (the pre-1.11.0 name) had no series on either cluster.
- Label values seen: `apiserver_request_total{code}` 0, 200, 201, 304, 404, 409, 429, 500, 503 (and 400, 403, 422 on k3s); `apiserver_storage_size_bytes{storage_cluster_id="etcd-0"}` with `job="apiserver"` on EKS and `job` apiserver and kubelet on k3s; `coredns_proxy_request_duration_seconds_count{proxy_name="forward"}` on both; `coredns_dns_responses_total{plugin="loadbalance"}` on both; `coredns_kubernetes_rest_client_requests_total{code}` 200 and 503 on EKS, 200, 403, 503 and `<error>` on k3s; `kubeproxy_sync_proxy_rules_iptables_total{table}` filter and nat, `ip_family` empty on EKS 1.32.0 and IPv4, IPv6 on k3s 1.33.5; `kubeproxy_sync_proxy_rules_no_local_endpoints_total{traffic_policy}` internal and external on both, value 2 for external on one k3s node; `storage_operation_duration_seconds_count{volume_plugin}` `kubernetes.io/csi:ebs.csi.aws.com` next to the in-tree plugins on EKS, no CSI value on k3s; `csi_operations_seconds_count{method_name}` the seven `/csi.v1.Node/*` methods with `grpc_status_code="OK"` on EKS.
- In the latest full snapshots (EKS 2026-11-03, k3s 2026-10-27) the queries of this part with no result were, EKS: apiserver_401_rate_1h, apiserver_requested_deprecated_apis, coredns_servfail_ratio_1h, kubeproxy_rest_client_errors_1h, ebscsi_controller_metrics_series, ebscsi_api_errors_24h, ebscsi_api_throttles_24h; k3s: apiserver_401_rate_1h, kubeproxy_restore_failures_24h, kubeproxy_targets, kubeproxy_rest_client_errors_1h. Of these, kubeproxy_restore_failures_24h was the defect above; the three EBS CSI queries could not match anything on driver v1.37.0 whatever the metrics setting; coredns_servfail_ratio_1h was empty because no SERVFAIL series existed; the rest are filters on names that exist.

Corrections found in source:

- `apiserver_storage_objects_top` read a name that 1.37 hides by default; `apiserver_resource_objects_top` reads the 1.34+ replacement and `lib/controlplane.py` prefers it.
- Every apiserver query filters `job="apiserver"`; on k3s the sums were doubled by the kubelet scrape of the same process.
- `kubeproxy_restore_failures_24h` added two series that are absent until their first failure (1.33+) or unregistered (ipvs, nftables); it reads the full counter alone, `kubeproxy_partial_restore_failures_24h` the partial one, and `lib/kubeproxy.py` adds them.
- `kubeproxy_no_local_endpoints_1h` applied `increase()` to a gauge of Services; replaced by `kubeproxy_no_local_endpoint_services`, the current count per node and policy, and the check's evidence names it for what it is.
- `kubeproxy_iptables_rules` took the max over the two IP families since 1.33; it sums them.
- `ebscsi_storage_ops_by_status_24h` and `ebscsi_storage_ops_p99_24h` matched every CSI driver; they match the EBS driver exactly.
- The three EBS CSI controller queries read names that exist from driver v1.38.0 (released as v1.38.1) only, and the presence count also counted the node plugin's NVMe series; they read either name generation and count the controller's request histogram.
- `coredns_servfail_ratio_1h` had no result on a cluster that never answered SERVFAIL; its numerator falls back to 0.

What stays open: an `apiserver_*` or `rest_client_*` rate that is empty because no series of that code ever existed still passes a `type: metric` check with nothing judged (`lib/evaluate.py` emits no finding for an empty result), the same class as #121's open item; `kubeproxy_ct_invalid_dropped_1h` is empty in ipvs and nftables mode and on 1.30 and the check reads that as 0; the nftables-mode counters are not read; `kube-proxy` and CoreDNS job names other than the kube-prometheus-stack ones make the `up` queries empty, which `lib/kubeproxy.py` reports as "not scraped".

## PromQL queries, part 3: Prometheus, Alertmanager, Loki, promtail, Fluent Bit, KEDA, the ingress controllers, and the series Prometheus writes itself (#123)

Every query of `collectors/*/metrics.yaml` that reads a `prometheus_*`, `alertmanager_*`, `loki_*`, `promtail_*`, `fluentbit_*`, `keda_*`, `nginx_ingress_controller_requests` or `traefik_*` name, or a series Prometheus writes for a target or an alert (`up`, `scrape_duration_seconds`, `scrape_samples_scraped`, `ALERTS`, `ALERTS_FOR_STATE`), carries a `source:` list in the format of #121 and #122: 50 queries (32 in prometheus, 11 in core, 5 in loki, and the `up` queries of coredns and kube-proxy), 45 distinct names. With this part every query of the 181 has a list except `core/cardinality_top_metrics`, `prometheus/http_metric_namespaces`, `prometheus/saturation_metric_namespaces`: the first reads every name there is, the other two are heuristic lists of application-metric names that no single project defines (the row below). The ten Loki metrics (twelve spellings) `lib/stackhealth.py` and `lib/logs.py` read from the `/metrics` text Loki serves (`loki/metrics.txt`, not PromQL) live in `collectors/loki/pod-metrics.yaml` in the #120 format, with the one rename as a `since`/`before` pair, and both modules read them through `lib/podmetrics.py`.

| The build-info collector Prometheus registers | `prometheus/common` v0.45.0 (`go.mod@v2.49.1`) | `prometheus/client_golang` v1.23.2 (`go.mod@v3.7.3`) | `prometheus/client_golang` v1.24.1 (`go.mod@v3.15.1`) | — |

| kube-prometheus-stack (`prometheus-community/helm-charts`, the Prometheus and Alertmanager Services and ServiceMonitors) | kube-prometheus-stack-56.0.0 | kube-prometheus-stack-79.5.0 | kube-prometheus-stack-92.17.1 | — |

The rows below are the facts that are not per name; the per-name lines are in the `source:` entries and in `collectors/loki/pod-metrics.yaml`.

| Fact | kubegap | Source | Holds for |
|---|---|---|---|
| The 17 `prometheus_*` names kubegap reads are defined with the same spelling and labels at v2.49.1, v3.7.3 and v3.15.1. The one shape change: `prometheus_notifications_dropped_total` is a plain Counter up to v3.9.1 and a CounterVec with `alertmanager` since v3.10.0, so a `sum()` over it is the same number at every version | `collectors/prometheus/metrics.yaml`, `collectors/core/metrics.yaml` | the `source:` entries; `notifier/metric.go:80@v3.9.1`, `:76@v3.10.0` | every version read |
| kube-prometheus-stack scrapes Prometheus and Alertmanager through ServiceMonitors that name no `jobLabel` and select the Service labelled `self-monitor: "true"`, so `job` is the Service name, `<fullname>-prometheus` and `<fullname>-alertmanager` (the release name when it contains the chart name). Each ServiceMonitor lists two ports of that Service, the web port and `reloader-web` (the config-reloader sidecar), under the one job, so `count(up{job=~".*prometheus$"} == 1)` was the number of scrape targets, 2 per Prometheus pod, not the number of instances. `prometheus_build_info` is registered once per Prometheus process, so there is one series per process per scrape job that reads it: `count()` of it is the instance count when each Prometheus is scraped by one job and none federates or receives remote write from another; the config-reloader sidecar registers only the Go and process collectors | query prom_replicas; `lib/stackhealth.py:_prometheus` (replicas_up) | `charts/kube-prometheus-stack/templates/prometheus/servicemonitor.yaml:15-19,24,44@kube-prometheus-stack-56.0.0`, `:15-19,24,44@kube-prometheus-stack-79.5.0`, `:18-22,27,47@kube-prometheus-stack-92.17.1`; `templates/prometheus/service.yaml:6,10`, `templates/alertmanager/servicemonitor.yaml:24,48` (`:27,51@92.17.1`), `templates/_helpers.tpl:14-24` at the three; `prometheus-operator pkg/prometheus/promcfg.go:1373-1376@v0.71.0`, `:2063-2066@v0.86.2`; `prometheus/common version/info.go:40-44@v0.45.0`, `prometheus/client_golang prometheus/collectors/version/version.go:26-30@v1.23.2`, `:39-59@v1.24.1`; `cmd/prometheus/main.go:101@v2.49.1`, `:148@v3.7.3`, `:155@v3.15.1`; `prometheus-operator cmd/prometheus-config-reloader/main.go:139-143@v0.71.0` | the three chart versions; observed 2 on both clusters with one replica each |
| `prometheus_target_scrapes_exceeded_sample_limit_total` is one plain Counter per Prometheus with no target label, incremented when a scrape is failed for the limit; which target it was is in that target's `lastError` (`"sample limit exceeded"`) of `/api/v1/targets`, which kubegap already saves as `prometheus/targets.json` with `scrapeInterval`, `scrapeTimeout` and `lastError` per active target | query prom_sample_limit_exceeded_1h; `lib/stackhealth.py:_scrape_targets`; check `prometheus.scrape-slow-or-limited` | `scrape/metrics.go:181@v2.49.1`, `:190@v3.7.3`, `:216@v3.15.1`; `scrape/scrape.go:1645@v2.49.1`, `:1910@v3.7.3`, `:2067@v3.15.1`; `scrape/target.go:316@v2.49.1`, `:322@v3.7.3`, `:364@v3.15.1`; `web/api/v1/api.go:890,895-896@v2.49.1`, `:1041,1046-1047@v3.7.3`, `:1177,1182-1183@v3.15.1` | every version read |
| `prometheus_target_interval_length_seconds` is a SummaryVec with one label, `interval`, so there is one series set per distinct scrape interval in the configuration and `max()` over the medians is the longest interval, not the most common one. Each job's own interval is in `targets.json`; `lib/stackhealth.py` judges a job's slowest scrape against the shortest interval of that job's targets and falls back to the query only without the file | query prom_scrape_interval_s; `lib/stackhealth.py:_prometheus` (slow_scrape_jobs, scrape_interval_s) | `scrape/metrics.go:173,177@v2.49.1`, `:182,186@v3.7.3`, `:197,201@v3.15.1` | every version read; observed two intervals on each cluster (10s and 30s on k3s, 15s and 30s on EKS) |
| `prometheus_notifications_errors_total{alertmanager}` has a series per configured Alertmanager initialized at 0 when the set is synced, so `increase(...) > 0` is empty only while no send failed; a series of an Alertmanager that no longer exists stays in Prometheus's own `/metrics` until restart | query prom_notifications_errors_1d | `notifier/notifier.go:694-696@v2.49.1`, `notifier/alertmanagerset.go:101-103@v3.7.3`, `notifier/sendloop.go:57-59@v3.15.1` | every version read; observed 3 series for 1 Alertmanager on EKS (two old pod addresses) |
| `prometheus_tsdb_lowest_timestamp_seconds` is the MinTime of the oldest persisted block, or of the head while no block exists (a Prometheus younger than two hours), so `prom_oldest_sample_days` is small on a fresh instance and the retention-shortfall judgement already requires the instance to be older than a day. `prometheus_tsdb_retention_limit_seconds` is 15d when neither a time nor a size retention is set and 0 when only a size is set, where `retention_days` is None and the shortfall is not judged | queries prom_oldest_sample_days, prom_retention_days; `lib/stackhealth.py:_prometheus` (retention_shortfall) | `cmd/prometheus/main.go:1200-1209,542-543@v2.49.1`, `:1457-1466,720-721@v3.7.3`, `:1676-1685,753-754@v3.15.1`; `tsdb/db.go:330@v2.49.1`, `:382@v3.7.3`, `:475@v3.15.1` | every version read |
| `ALERTS` is written by every alerting-rule evaluation per pending or firing alert (value 1, `alertname`, `alertstate`, the alert's labels); `ALERTS_FOR_STATE` is written for the same alerts with the alert's `ActiveAt` as the value and no `alertstate` of its own. An `alertstate` seen on an `ALERTS_FOR_STATE` series comes from a rule whose expression reads `ALERTS` (kube-prometheus-stack's `InfoInhibitor`) | queries alerts_fired_15d, alerts_firing_samples_7d, alerts_firing_now, alerts_pending_now, alerts_firing_stale_days | `rules/alerting.go:41-46,232-234,245-253,462@v2.49.1`, `:41-46,236-238,250-259,520@v3.7.3`, `:41-46,244-246,258-267,541@v3.15.1` | every version read; observed on both clusters |
| `alertmanager_cluster_members` is registered by the cluster delegate, which exists only when `--cluster.listen-address` is not empty; the prometheus-operator passes an empty address for a single replica unless `forceEnableClusterMode` is set, so the series is absent on a one-replica kube-prometheus-stack and `am_cluster_members` is empty there by design (`alertmanager_cluster_enabled`, `cmd/alertmanager/main.go:92@v0.26.0`, `:88@v0.29.0`, set at `app/app.go:246@v0.34.2`, is 0) | query am_cluster_members; `lib/stackhealth.py:_alertmanager` (cluster_members_metric) | `cluster/delegate.go:78@v0.26.0`, `:77@v0.29.0`, `:79@v0.34.2`; `cmd/alertmanager/main.go:260-261@v0.26.0`, `:242-243@v0.29.0`, `app/app.go:214-215@v0.34.2`; `prometheus-operator pkg/alertmanager/statefulset.go:245-246@v0.71.0`, `:254-255@v0.86.2`, `:295-296@v0.94.2` | every version read; observed absent on both clusters |
| The seven `alertmanager_*` names kubegap reads keep their spelling and the labels kubegap uses from v0.26.0 to v0.34.2: `notifications_failed_total` carries `integration, reason` at all three (plus `receiver_name` behind a feature flag since 0.29.0), `alerts_received_total` carries `status` with the ConstLabel `version` (v1 or v2 per API), `alerts` moved from one GaugeFunc per state to a collector with the label `state` by v0.34.2 with the same values (active, suppressed, unprocessed) | queries am_*, alertmanager_* | the `source:` entries; `provider/mem/mem.go:72-74@v0.26.0`, `:72-74@v0.29.0`, `dispatch/metric.go:59-69@v0.34.2` | every version read; observed `version` v1 and v2 on 0.26.0, v2 only on 0.29.0 |
| Loki names are `loki_<name>` from the namespace `loki`; the request histogram `loki_request_duration_seconds{method,route,status_code,ws}` comes from the HTTP server library Loki embeds (weaveworks/common up to 2.9, grafana/dskit since 3.0) under that namespace. Loki 3.0.0 moved the retention run timestamp from `loki_boltdb_shipper_apply_retention_last_successful_run_timestamp_seconds` to `loki_compactor_apply_retention_last_successful_run_timestamp_seconds` while `loki_boltdb_shipper_compactor_running` kept its name; `loki_log_messages_total` is marked deprecated since 2.7.0 in favour of `loki_internal_log_messages_total` and both are still emitted at v3.7.9; the two `loki_distributor_*_received_total` counters are defined in `pkg/loghttp/push/push.go`, not in the distributor package | `collectors/loki/pod-metrics.yaml`; `lib/stackhealth.py:_loki` (retention_applied, metrics_found); `lib/logs.py:analyze_loki` (self_metrics) | `pkg/storage/stores/shipper/compactor/metrics.go:39-45@v2.6.1`, `pkg/storage/stores/indexshipper/compactor/metrics.go:39-45@v2.8.0`, `:39-45@v2.9.2`, `pkg/compactor/metrics.go:52-58@v3.0.0`, `:52-58@v3.7.9`; `pkg/util/log/log.go:79-83@v2.7.0`, `:133-138@v3.7.9`; `pkg/loghttp/push/push.go:35-42@v2.6.1`, `:46-59@v3.7.9`; `pkg/loki/loki.go:91@v2.6.1`, `:145@v3.7.9` | every version read |
| promtail's three names are the same from v2.6.1 to v3.7.9; `promtail_dropped_entries_total` carries `host` only at v2.6.1 and `host, tenant, reason` since v2.9.2, `promtail_request_duration_seconds` carries `status_code, host` throughout, `promtail_targets_active_total` has no labels (one gauge per process, so `sum by (instance)` is per node). promtail is end of life since 2026-03-02; Grafana Alloy replaces it and kubegap does not detect Alloy yet (#130) | `collectors/loki/metrics.yaml` | `clients/pkg/promtail/client/client.go:82-89@v2.6.1`, `:93-110@v2.9.2`, `:93-110@v3.5.12`, `:93-110@v3.7.9`; `clients/pkg/promtail/targets/file/metrics.go:56@v2.6.1`, `:56@v3.7.9`; `docs/sources/send-data/promtail/_index.md:12@v3.7.9` | every version read; not scraped on either cluster |
| Fluent Bit's names are cmetrics counters `fluentbit_<input|output>_<name>` with the label `name` (the plugin instance), created at 0 with each instance; they are served only when the HTTP server is on (`HTTP_Server On`, off by default) at `/api/v2/metrics/prometheus`, so a Fluent Bit without it, or without a scrape configuration, has no series in Prometheus and the four queries are empty while `lib/logs.py` records `metrics_scraped: false` | `collectors/core/metrics.yaml` fluentbit_*; `lib/logs.py:analyze_agents` | `src/flb_input.c:968-970@v3.0.4`, `:1622-1624@v5.1.5`; `src/flb_output.c:1141-1169@v3.0.4`, `:1651-1679@v5.1.5`; `src/flb_config.c:251@v3.0.4`, `:354@v5.1.5`; `src/http_server/api/v2/metrics.c:253@v3.0.4`, `:125@v5.1.5` | both versions read; not scraped on the k3s cluster that runs 3.0.4 |
| `nginx_ingress_controller_requests` and the histogram `nginx_ingress_controller_request_duration_seconds` carry `status, method, path, namespace, ingress, service, canary` (and `host` when enabled); because the exporter sets `namespace` and the scrape target carries its own, Prometheus with `honor_labels` off stores the exporter's as `exported_namespace` (`scrape/scrape.go:592,605@v2.49.1`, `:716,729@v3.7.3`, `:690,703@v3.15.1`), which is why the query groups by both. Traefik's `traefik_service_requests_total{code,method,protocol,service}` and `traefik_entrypoint_requests_total{code,method,protocol,entrypoint}` are unchanged from v3.3.2 to v3.7.17 (the file moved to `pkg/observability/metrics/`) | queries ingress_controller_requests, ingress_nginx_rps_1h, ingress_nginx_p95_1h | `internal/ingress/metric/collectors/socket.go:89-99,133,199,244@controller-v1.14.0`, `:89-99,133,199,244@controller-v1.15.1`, `main.go:20`; `pkg/metrics/prometheus.go:35,51,149,224@v3.3.2`, `pkg/observability/metrics/prometheus.go:35,51,147,222@v3.7.17` | every version read; not scraped on the k3s cluster that runs ingress-nginx 1.14.0 |
| `keda_scaler_detail_errors_total{namespace,scaledObject,...}` exists since KEDA 2.14.0; the older `keda_scaler_errors_total` (gone in 2.17.0) is a plain counter with no labels at every version it exists, so the `or` branch that read it could never attribute an error to a ScaledObject and, on 2.14 to 2.16, appended one unattributable row. The query reads the detail counter only; before 2.14 there is no per-object error counter and the query is empty | query keda_scaler_errors_24h; `lib/scaling.py` | `pkg/metricscollector/prommetrics.go:46,49@v2.12.0`, `:46,49@v2.13.0`, `:107,110,116@v2.14.0`, `:107,110@v2.16.0`, `:84@v2.17.2` (read in #120, `collectors/keda/pod-metrics.yaml`) | every version read; KEDA is not scraped on either cluster |
| `http_metric_namespaces` and `saturation_metric_namespaces` match a list of names that several client libraries use (`http_requests_total`, `http_server_requests_seconds`, `grpc_server_handled_total`, `django_http_requests_total_by_method_total`, `flask_http_request_duration_seconds`, `nginx_http_requests_total`, `express_http_request_duration_seconds`, `fastapi_requests_total`, `starlette_requests_total`, `aspnetcore_requests_total`, and the queue, pool, connection, thread and lag words). They answer "does any workload in this namespace expose an HTTP or saturation metric"; a library that names its metric differently is not seen and the golden-signal judgement calls that namespace uncovered | queries http_metric_namespaces, saturation_metric_namespaces; `lib/observability.py:_golden_signals` | **unverified by design**: a heuristic over many projects, no single source defines the list | — |

Observed on the two clusters (read-only instant queries through kubegap's port-forward, 2026-11-04, and the latest full snapshots):

- Every `prometheus_*` and `alertmanager_*` name of the queries had series on both clusters except `alertmanager_cluster_members` (cluster mode off, see the row) and `prometheus_remote_storage_samples_failed_total` (no remote write configured). `up`, `scrape_duration_seconds`, `scrape_samples_scraped`, `ALERTS` and `ALERTS_FOR_STATE` had series on both.
- No `promtail_*`, `loki_*`, `fluentbit_*`, `keda_*`, `nginx_ingress_controller_*` or `traefik_*` series on either cluster: Prometheus scrapes none of those components there (no ServiceMonitor or PodMonitor), although EKS runs Loki 2.6.1 and promtail 2.9.2 and k3s runs Fluent Bit 3.0.4, KEDA 2.19.0 and ingress-nginx 1.14.0. The 14 queries that read them are verified in source only.
- `count(up{job=~".*prometheus$"} == 1)` was 2 on both clusters with one Prometheus pod each (`reloader-web` and the web port); `count(prometheus_build_info)` is 1 on both.
- `prometheus_target_scrapes_exceeded_sample_limit_total` carries only the Prometheus's own `job` on both; `prometheus_target_interval_length_seconds{quantile="0.5"}` has `interval` 10s and 30s on k3s, 15s and 30s on EKS, and the old query returned 30 on both.
- `alertmanager_notifications_failed_total` had 85 series on k3s (0.29.0) and 33 on EKS (0.26.0), every integration times every reason at 0; `ALERTS_FOR_STATE` carried `alertstate` on 6 of 23 series (k3s) and 2 of 10 (EKS), all of them rules that read `ALERTS`.
- In the latest full snapshots (EKS 2026-11-03, k3s 2026-10-27) 22 of the 50 queries of this part had no result on either cluster: 6 are `> 0` or `== 0` filters on names that exist (`rule_evaluation_failures`, `alertmanager_notifications_failed_1d`, `prom_sample_limit_exceeded_1h`, `prom_notifications_errors_1d`, `scrape_targets_down_by_job`, `targets_down`), 2 read names registered only with a feature on (`am_cluster_members`, `prometheus_remote_write_failed_1h`), 13 read components Prometheus does not scrape there (the four `fluentbit_*`, three `promtail_*`, two `loki_*`, the three `ingress_nginx_*` and `keda_scaler_errors_24h`), 1 found no application exposing the names (`ingress_controller_requests`). `http_metric_namespaces`, outside this part, was empty for the same last reason. None of the 22 reads a misnamed metric.

Corrections found in source:

- `prom_replicas` counted scrape targets (2 per Prometheus pod under kube-prometheus-stack); it counts `prometheus_build_info` series now.
- `prom_sample_limit_exceeded_1h` grouped a label-less counter by the Prometheus's own `job` and the check named Prometheus as the job over its sampleLimit; it is a total now and `lib/stackhealth.py` names the targets from `prometheus/targets.json` (`sample_limit_targets`).
- `prom_scrape_interval_s` was the longest interval, not the most common one, and every job was judged against it; `lib/stackhealth.py` judges each job against the shortest `scrapeInterval` of its targets from `targets.json` and keeps the query as the fallback.
- `lib/stackhealth.py` and `lib/logs.py` read `loki_boltdb_shipper_apply_retention_last_successful_run_timestamp_seconds`, gone since Loki 3.0.0, so `retention_applied` was None on every Loki 3 and the `loki.version-old` evidence printed `retention applied None`; both read the Loki names through `collectors/loki/pod-metrics.yaml` now, which carries both spellings.
- `keda_scaler_errors_24h` read the label-less `keda_scaler_errors_total` with `or`, a branch that could never name a ScaledObject and added an unattributable row on KEDA 2.14 to 2.16 (its source entry and `collectors/keda/pod-metrics.yaml` claimed labels it never had); it reads the detail counter only.
- `tests/test_sources.py` recognized `up` only as `up{`, so `targets_down` and `scrape_targets_down_by_job` (`up == 0`) escaped the #122 requirement; it recognizes the bare name now and both queries cite it.

What stays open: the promtail, Loki and Fluent Bit queries cannot be run against a cluster that scrapes those components, so their correctness rests on the source alone until one is available; an empty `fluentbit_*`, `promtail_*` or `keda_*` result still passes the checks that read it with nothing judged, the class of #141; kubegap detects promtail but not Grafana Alloy, its replacement, which is #130's component-detection work.

## Store HTTP APIs: Prometheus, Alertmanager, Loki and Grafana endpoints and response shapes (#126), with the two checks of #140

21 of the 22 `type: http` steps of `collectors/{prometheus,alertmanager,loki,grafana}/collect.yaml` carry a `source:` line naming the server's route registration and the response type at the versions read, the fields kubegap reads and the library that reads them; the 22nd, `loki.metrics`, reads Loki's own `/metrics`, whose names are cited in `collectors/loki/pod-metrics.yaml` (#123) while the route itself is registered by the server library Loki vendors and is not read. `tests/test_sources.py` requires the line (or the table reference). The checks that read a summary section built from these responses number 42: `stackhealth.*` 20, the `observability.*` checks over rules, Alertmanager, alerts, failure modes, SLO, golden signals and dashboards 13, `logs.*` over Loki 9 (counting every check whose `each:` or `inputs:` names such a section). The endpoints `lib/monitoring.py` calls at setup to find and probe the stores are the rows below.

Versions read:

| Project | EKS cluster | k3s cluster | Newest release | Read for a boundary |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Prometheus (`prometheus/prometheus web/api/v1/api.go`, `cmd/prometheus/main.go`, `rules/`, `scrape/target.go`) | v2.49.1 | v3.7.3 | v3.15.1 | — |
| Alertmanager (`prometheus/alertmanager api/v2/openapi.yaml`, `config/config.go`) | v0.26.0 | v0.29.0 | v0.34.2 | — |
| Loki (`grafana/loki pkg/loki/`, `pkg/loghttp/`, `pkg/validation/limits.go`, the compactor config) | v2.6.1 | — | v3.7.9 | v2.9.2, v3.0.0 |
| Grafana (`grafana/grafana pkg/api/`) | v10.2.3 | v12.2.1 | v13.3.0 | — |
| component-base (`kubernetes/kubernetes staging/src/k8s.io/component-base/metrics/legacyregistry/registry.go`) | v1.30.14 (the oldest supported; the EKS 1.32.13 line is the same and was not re-read) | v1.33.5 | v1.37.2 | v3.8.1 of Prometheus was read for the alert `unknown` state |

| Fact | kubegap | Source | Holds for |
|---|---|---|---|
| The six Prometheus endpoints kubegap calls (`/api/v1/rules`, `/targets`, `/status/runtimeinfo`, `/status/tsdb`, `/status/flags`, `/status/buildinfo`) are registered and shaped the same from v2.49.1 to v3.15.1 for every field kubegap reads; 3.x adds `hostname` and `serverTime` to runtimeinfo (not read); a rule's `lastError` is omitted when empty; the retention flags are marked `[DEPRECATED]` at v3.15.1 but still served in `/status/flags` | steps `prometheus.rules`, `prometheus.targets`, `prometheus.runtime`, `prometheus.tsdb-status`, `prometheus.flags`, `prometheus.buildinfo`; `lib/observability.py:_rules`, `lib/observability.py:_targets`, `lib/stackhealth.py:_prometheus` | the `source:` lines; `web/api/v1/api.go:1335@v2.49.1`, `:1485@v3.7.3`, `:1689@v3.15.1` (`lastError,omitempty`); `cmd/prometheus/main.go:518,521@v3.15.1` | every version read |
| A rule's `health` is `ok`, `err` or `unknown` and an alerting rule's `state` is `inactive`, `pending` or `firing`, plus `unknown` for a rule not yet evaluated since Prometheus 3.8 (present at v3.8.1, absent at v3.7.3); `observability.*` stores the string and counts firing and pending | `lib/observability.py:_rules` | `rules/rule.go:31-33@v2.49.1`, `:31-33@v3.7.3`, `:31-33@v3.15.1`; `rules/alerting.go:54-60,66-70@v2.49.1`, `:54-60,66-70@v3.7.3`, `:53-54@v3.8.1`, `:57-66,71-76@v3.15.1` | every version read |
| The Alertmanager v2 API is defined by `api/v2/openapi.yaml`; the four endpoints kubegap calls and the fields it reads are the same from v0.26.0 to v0.34.2; `status.config.original` is the loaded configuration as YAML text, which kubegap parses with `lib/amconfig` (on `lib/yamlite`) for `receivers`, the routing tree's `receiver`s, `inhibit_rules` and `time_intervals`/`mute_time_intervals`; alert `status.mutedBy` exists since 0.29.0 (not read) | steps `alertmanager.*`; `lib/stackhealth.py:_alertmanager`, `lib/observability.py:_am_config`, `lib/observability.py:_alertmanager`; `lib/monitoring.py:probe` (version, `cluster.status`) | the `source:` lines; `config/config.go:300-308,777-778,791,849-865,894-896@v0.26.0`, `:341-349,885-886,899,957-973,1009-1011@v0.29.0`, `:284-292,887-888,901,973-975@v0.34.2` (`Config`, `Route.receiver` and `routes`, `InhibitRule`, `Receiver.name`), `config/common/inhibitrule.go:27,37,45@v0.34.2` (the inhibit-rule matchers moved there); `api/v2/openapi.yaml:509@v0.29.0`, `:546@v0.34.2` | every version read |
| Loki answers `/ready` with the text `ready` and HTTP 200 once every module is Running and each of the ingester, the pattern ingester (since 3.0), the query frontend and, since 3.7, the ingest-limits services answers its readiness check, else 503 with the reason (a requested shutdown answers 503 first since 2.9); `/loki/api/v1/status/buildinfo` is Prometheus's `PrometheusVersion` struct; the label-values and query responses (`status`, `data`) keep their shape from 2.6.1 to 3.7.9, the routes moving to `pkg/util/constants/api_paths.go` in 3.x | steps `loki.ready`, `loki.buildinfo`, `loki.label_*`, `loki.volume_1h`, `loki.errors_1h`, `loki.json_1h`; `lib/logs.py:analyze_loki`, `lib/stackhealth.py:_loki`, `lib/monitoring.py:probe` | the `source:` lines; `pkg/loki/loki.go:430-463@v2.6.1`, `:572-576,601-615@v3.0.0`, `:684-688,730-746@v3.7.9`; `pkg/loki/version_handler.go:12-20@v2.6.1`, `:12-20@v3.7.9` | every version read |
| `GET /config` renders Loki's running configuration as YAML; `lib/monitoring.py` parses that text (`lib/yamlite.load` after `unfold_plain_scalars`: go-yaml folds plain scalars at 80 columns, and a real 2.6.1 dump loads whole once unfolded) and reads `limits_config.retention_period` and `compactor.retention_enabled` from the structure, by section (`lib/yamlite.mapping_value`) when the text is outside yamlite's subset, and `observability.loki-retention-unbounded` judges them. Both keys exist at every version read; the compactor config moved files (`pkg/storage/stores/shipper/compactor` at 2.6, `indexshipper/compactor` at 2.8 and 2.9, `pkg/compactor` since 3.0) without renaming the key. The dump also carries `table_manager.retention_period` (0s on the EKS cluster), which the whole-text regular expression used until #126 would have read as the global one had Loki printed that section first | `lib/monitoring.py:probe`; check `observability.loki-retention-unbounded` | `pkg/loki/loki.go:318@v2.6.1`, `:438@v2.9.2`, `:428@v3.0.0`, `:538@v3.7.9`; `pkg/loki/config_handler.go:82,122@v2.6.1`, `:87,218@v3.7.9`; `pkg/validation/limits.go:115@v2.6.1`, `:164@v2.9.2`, `:178@v3.0.0`, `:215@v3.7.9`; `pkg/storage/stores/shipper/compactor/compactor.go:68@v2.6.1`, `pkg/storage/stores/indexshipper/compactor/compactor.go:79@v2.9.2`, `pkg/compactor/compactor.go:76@v3.0.0`, `pkg/compactor/config.go:24@v3.7.9` | every version read; the second `retention_period` observed on the EKS 2.6.1 dump and on a 3.7.9 dump taken from a local run of the release binary (3,584 lines, loads whole once unfolded; `tests/fixtures/loki-config-3.7.9.yaml`) |
| Grafana's `/api/health` needs no authentication and answers `database` (`ok` or `failing`), `version` and `commit` unless `[auth.anonymous] hide_version` is set, so `version` can be missing; 13.3.0 adds an `apiserver` field and may answer 503 on apiserver readiness alone; `/api/datasources` and `/api/dashboards/uid/{uid}` need a token with the read permissions (`datasources:read`, `dashboards:read`) and `/api/search` a signed-in token (it returns the dashboards that token may read), which is why they are skipped without `grafana_api_token`; the DTO fields kubegap reads (`name`, `type`, `url`, `isDefault`; `uid`; `dashboard`) are unchanged from 10.2.3 to 13.3.0 | steps `grafana.*`; `lib/stackhealth.py:_grafana`, `lib/observability.py:_grafana`, `lib/monitoring.py:probe` | the `source:` lines; `pkg/api/http_server.go:753@v10.2.3`, `:721@v12.2.1`, `:792,813,828@v13.3.0` (`HideVersion`, the `apiserver` field) | every version read; the three token endpoints were never observed on the two clusters (no token) |
| `lib/monitoring.py` finds the store Services and probes each once at setup: Prometheus `/api/v1/status/buildinfo`, `/status/flags`, `/status/tsdb`; Alertmanager `/api/v2/status`; Loki `/ready`, `/loki/api/v1/status/buildinfo`, `/config`; Grafana `/api/health`; the `stores` of the inventory come from these | `lib/monitoring.py:probe` | `web/api/v1/api.go:389-391@v2.49.1`, `:438-440@v3.7.3`, `:479-481@v3.15.1`; `api/v2/openapi.yaml:21@v0.26.0`, `:21@v0.29.0`, `:21@v0.34.2`; `pkg/loki/loki.go:318,361,369@v2.6.1`, `:538,590,603@v3.7.9`; `pkg/api/http_server.go:747@v10.2.3`, `:714@v12.2.1`, `:806@v13.3.0` | every version read |
| `kubeproxy_iptables_ct_state_invalid_dropped_packets_total` does not exist at Kubernetes 1.30 and since 1.31 is registered in iptables mode only and only when nfacct is available; registered, it is served (at 0 too) once the proxier has created the nfacct counter, and a failed read of the counter leaves the series absent. So an absent series never means "no drops": `lib/kubeproxy.py` sets `ct_invalid_dropped_1h` to null with `ct_invalid_reason` (kube-proxy 1.30, the mode not read, non-iptables mode, or no nfacct) and `kube_proxy_minor` (the DaemonSet image's minor, which on EKS can lag the control plane, else the server's) and `kubeproxy.conntrack-invalid-drops` skips | `lib/kubeproxy.py:analyze`; check `kubeproxy.conntrack-invalid-drops` | `pkg/proxy/metrics/metrics.go:147,302-303,363-377@v1.31.14`, `:147,302@v1.32.13`, `:152,335,402-416@v1.33.5`, `:154,417@v1.37.2`; absent at v1.30.14 (read in #122) | every version read |
| The apiserver scrape's `job` label is not fixed: kube-prometheus-stack gives `apiserver`, Prometheus's own Kubernetes example gives `kubernetes-apiservers`, a ServiceMonitor without `jobLabel` the Service name `kubernetes`. The 14 apiserver queries carry `{apiserver_job}`; `lib/prom.py:resolve_apiserver_job` fills it from `config.yaml` or detects it from the `job` values of `apiserver_request_total`, and with none the queries are saved as failed so the control-plane checks skip | `lib/prom.py:resolve_apiserver_job`, `lib/controlplane.py:analyze` (`apiserver_metrics.job`); `collectors/core/metrics.yaml` | `documentation/examples/prometheus-kubernetes.yml:24@v2.49.1`, `:24@v3.15.1`; the operator's job rule `pkg/prometheus/promcfg.go:1373-1376@v0.71.0`, `:2063-2066@v0.86.2` and the kube-prometheus-stack row of part 2 | every version read |

Observed on the two clusters (the latest full snapshots, 2026-10-27 k3s and 2026-11-03 EKS, and read-only calls on 2026-11-04):

- Every field the parsers read is present in the saved responses of Prometheus 2.49.1 and 3.7.3, Alertmanager 0.26.0 and 0.29.0, Loki 2.6.1 (EKS only) and Grafana 10.2.3 and 12.2.1 (`/api/health` only); the Grafana datasource, search and dashboard calls were never made (no token), and no Loki 3.x was available, so those rest on the source alone.
- Loki 3.7.9's `/config` (the release binary run locally with a minimal file configuration) is 3,584 lines, has the same folded plain scalars as 2.6.1 and the same two `retention_period` keys (`limits_config`, `table_manager`) plus `enforce_retention_period` and a per-stream `retention_period` list, and loads whole once unfolded; its buildinfo carries an empty `goVersion`.
- The apiserver job is `apiserver` on both clusters (kube-prometheus-stack); the detection returns it as the first candidate (on k3s `kubelet` is the other candidate, the kubelet scrape of the same registry).
- `kubeproxy_iptables_ct_state_invalid_dropped_packets_total` is served on EKS (kube-proxy 1.32.0, iptables mode) and on the k3s nodes (1.33.5, iptables mode).

Corrections found in source:

- `kubeproxy.conntrack-invalid-drops` passed on an absent counter; it skips with the reason.
- The apiserver queries filtered on a fixed `job="apiserver"`; the name is configured or detected per run.
- The one-registry sentence of part 2 cited the k3s side only; the component-base default registry is cited now.

What stays open: `observability.loki-retention-unbounded` still treats an unread `retention_enabled` as true (the #141 class). Grafana's three token endpoints and Loki 3.x stay observed-never until a cluster with them is available.

## Standard library and specification behaviours the code paths rely on: http.client, urllib, ssl, ipaddress, tarfile, zipfile, CommonMark, GFM tables, ECMA-48 (#128)

Not checks but code: five modules read their inputs through CPython's standard library or write their output under a text specification, and a false pass can hide there as well as in a metric name (#91 found one in `lib/http.py` by review). CPython was read at the two ends of the supported range, `python/cpython` v3.11.18 and v3.14.10 (`pyproject.toml` says `>=3.11`), plus v3.11.9 and v3.11.10 to date the one table that changed; the Markdown rules come from `commonmark/commonmark-spec` `spec.txt` at 0.31.2 and, for tables, which CommonMark does not have, from the test file of `github/cmark-gfm` at 0.29.0.gfm.13; the terminal rules from ECMA-48 5th edition (June 1991), cited as `ECMA-48.pdf:<printed page>@5th-1991` with the section number in the row, since a PDF has pages and not lines, and the structure of an escape sequence from ECMA-35 6th edition (December 1994), which ECMA-48 §8.3.48 defers to, cited the same way as `ECMA-35.pdf:<printed page>@6th-1994`; v3.12.3 and v3.12.4 of CPython were read for the same table. `lib/portfwd.py`, which the issue listed, runs `kubectl port-forward` and uses neither `ssl` nor `urllib`; it has no row.

| Fact | kubegap | Source | Holds for |
|---|---|---|---|
| `HTTPResponse.read(amt)` returns at most `amt` bytes, clamps `amt` to the bytes Content-Length still owes and subtracts what it returned from `length`; `length` is `None` for a chunked or an EOF-delimited reply, so a positive `length` after a short read means the server closed before the body was complete | `lib/http.py:_read_capped` | `Lib/http/client.py:472-495@v3.11.18`, `:478-501@v3.14.10` (`read`); `:378-389@v3.11.18`, `:384-395@v3.14.10` (`begin` sets `length`); `:609@v3.11.18`, `:615@v3.14.10` (`_read_chunked`) | both ends; 3.14 adds `amt >= 0` to the condition, kubegap passes a positive number |
| A short read is an end of stream, not a partial buffer: the response's `fp` is `sock.makefile("rb")`, a `BufferedReader`, whose `read(n)` issues further raw reads until `n` bytes or EOF | `lib/http.py:_read_capped` | `Lib/http/client.py:275@v3.11.18`, `:281@v3.14.10`; `Lib/_pyio.py:713-714@v3.11.18`, `:683-684@v3.14.10` (the documented contract of the C implementation) | both ends |
| `IncompleteRead` is an `HTTPException`, so raising it from `_read_capped` lands in the `except http.client.HTTPException` branch and the step fails with `incomplete response` | `lib/http.py:get` | `Lib/http/client.py:1550@v3.11.18`, `:1554@v3.14.10` | both ends |
| `build_opener` drops a default handler when a handler passed in is a subclass of it, so `_NoRedirect(HTTPRedirectHandler)` replaces the default redirect handler instead of sitting next to it | `lib/http.py:get` | `Lib/urllib/request.py:589-590@v3.11.18`, `:557-558@v3.14.10` | both ends |
| A reply outside 200-299 goes through `OpenerDirector.error`, which calls `http_error_<code>`; 301, 303, 307 and 308 share `http_error_302`, which calls `redirect_request`, where `_NoRedirect` raises the `HTTPError` that `get()` reports as `redirect refused` | `lib/http.py:get` | `Lib/urllib/request.py:633-634,691,751@v3.11.18`, `:601-602,660,720@v3.14.10` | both ends |
| The default redirect handler would copy every request header except `Content-Length` and `Content-Type` onto the redirected request (the reason redirects are refused: the `Authorization` header would travel to the `Location` host) | `lib/http.py:get` | `Lib/urllib/request.py:679-681@v3.11.18`, `:647-649@v3.14.10` | both ends |
| `HTTPError` is an `addinfourl` over the response object, so `_read_capped(e, …)` reads an error body the same way and `e.length` reaches the response through `_TemporaryFileWrapper.__getattr__` | `lib/http.py:get` | `Lib/urllib/error.py:35,47@v3.11.18`, `:35,47@v3.14.10`; `Lib/urllib/response.py:14@v3.11.18`, `:14@v3.14.10`; `Lib/tempfile.py:490-495@v3.11.18`, `:564-569@v3.14.10` | both ends |
| `timeout=` given to `open()` reaches the connection (`http_class(host, timeout=req.timeout)` and `socket.create_connection(…, self.timeout)`); a failure to connect is raised as `URLError`, a timeout while reading the body as `TimeoutError`, and `get()` catches both | `lib/http.py:get` | `Lib/urllib/request.py:500,1330,1364@v3.11.18`, `:468,1303,1336@v3.14.10`; `Lib/http/client.py:1011@v3.11.18`, `:1052@v3.14.10` | both ends |
| An `https://` URL is verified: with no context given, the default context is `ssl._create_default_https_context()`, which is `create_default_context`, and for `SERVER_AUTH` it sets `verify_mode = CERT_REQUIRED` and `check_hostname = True` and loads the system certificates. At 3.11 `HTTPSHandler` passes `None` and `HTTPSConnection.__init__` builds it; at 3.14 `HTTPSHandler.__init__` builds it itself through `http.client._create_https_context` | `lib/http.py:get` | `Lib/urllib/request.py:1396-1405@v3.11.18`, `:1373-1375@v3.14.10`; `Lib/http/client.py:1490@v3.11.18`, `:845-848@v3.14.10`; `Lib/ssl.py:745,762-763,838@v3.11.18`, `:696,713-714,801@v3.14.10` | both ends, by different paths |
| `IPv4Address.is_unspecified`, `is_loopback`, `is_link_local`, `is_multicast` and `is_reserved` test membership of 0.0.0.0, 127.0.0.0/8, 169.254.0.0/16 (where the instance metadata service lives), 224.0.0.0/4 and 240.0.0.0/4 | `lib/nodecommands.py:pod_ip_allowed` | `Lib/ipaddress.py:1327,1378,1389,1400,1410@v3.11.18` (predicates), `:1570-1604@v3.11.18` (constants); `:1313,1364,1375,1386,1396@v3.14.10`, `:1566-1600@v3.14.10`; `:1543-1570@v3.11.9` | every 3.11.x to 3.14.10: the five constants are the same at v3.11.9, v3.11.18 and v3.14.10 |
| The `is_private`/`is_global` tables (`_private_networks`, `_private_networks_exceptions`) changed within the 3.11 and 3.12 lines, in 3.11.10 and 3.12.4 (the issue's "3.12.4 and 3.13": 3.13.0 carries what 3.12.4 has and was not read separately), which is why `pod_ip_allowed` does not read them and names the pod ranges itself (`POD_NETWORKS`, with 100.64.0.0/10 that `is_private` excludes) | `lib/nodecommands.py:pod_ip_allowed` | `Lib/ipaddress.py:1597-1600@v3.11.10` and `:1597-1600@v3.12.4` (`_private_networks_exceptions`), absent at v3.11.9 (`:1551-1570@v3.11.9`, where `192.0.0.0/29` stood for the later `/24`) and at v3.12.3; `:1580-1605@v3.11.18` and `:1576-1601@v3.14.10` are identical | not relied on; the same table at v3.11.18 and v3.14.10 |
| `TarFile.getmembers()`, `TarInfo.isfile()` (regular files only) and `extractfile()` read a member's bytes without writing a path: a member named `../../x/<bin>` is read as content and written to kubegap's own target path, and a symbolic-link member is not `isfile()` so it is never chosen (`extractfile` would follow a link inside the archive, not on disk) | `lib/tools.py:_place_binary` | `Lib/tarfile.py:2075,1666,2485,2503-2511@v3.11.18`, `:2205,1739,2633,2651-2659@v3.14.10` | both ends |
| The `filter=` argument of `extract`/`extractall` (3.12; the default becomes `data` in 3.14, `fully_trusted` before) does not apply: kubegap never extracts a tar to disk | `lib/tools.py:_place_binary` | `Lib/tarfile.py:2304-2308@v3.11.18` (`fully_trusted_filter`), `:2452-2456@v3.14.10` (`data_filter`) | not relied on |
| `ZipFile.namelist()` and `read(name)` read a member's bytes without writing a path; a symbolic-link entry is read as the link text | `lib/tools.py:_place_binary` | `Lib/zipfile.py:1497,1564@v3.11.18`; `Lib/zipfile/__init__.py:1642,1712@v3.14.10` | both ends |
| `ZipFile.extractall(path)` writes every member under `path`: `_extract_member` drops the drive and root of the member name and every `..`, `.` and empty part, and creates regular files only (`open(targetpath, "wb")` and a copy; no `os.symlink`), so the aws CLI zip cannot write outside `.tools/aws-cli-src`; pinned by `test_aws_zip_extraction_stays_inside_the_work_folder_and_makes_no_link` | `lib/tools.py:_aws_install` | `Lib/zipfile.py:1726,1758,1775-1781,1803@v3.11.18`; `Lib/zipfile/__init__.py:1885,1918,1935-1941,1972@v3.14.10` | both ends; 3.14 uses `os.path.splitroot` where 3.11 used `splitdrive` plus a leading-separator test |
| In a GFM table a `\|` is a pipe inside the cell, also inside a code span, and a newline ends the row | `lib/render.py:_cell` | `test/extensions.txt:261-270@0.29.0.gfm.13` (`Within \`\|code\|\` is okay`) | GFM only; CommonMark has no tables |
| An inline link is link text followed *immediately* by `(`, and an image is `!` immediately before the link; `] (` and `! [` are neither | `lib/render.py:_cell` | `spec.txt:7521,8531@0.31.2` | 0.31.2 |
| Raw inline HTML starts with `<` followed by an ASCII letter (open tag), `</` (closing tag), `<!--` (comment), `<?` (processing instruction), `<!` plus a letter (declaration) or `<![CDATA[`; an autolink is `<` plus a scheme that starts with an ASCII letter; `< ` is none of them | `lib/render.py:_cell` | `spec.txt:8986,8952,8990,8994,8998,9003,9006-9007,8756,8766@0.31.2` | 0.31.2 |
| A closing code fence must be at least as long as the opening fence and use the same character, so a fence one backtick longer than the longest run inside the text cannot be closed by the text | `lib/render.py:_fence` | `spec.txt:2042,2015@0.31.2` | 0.31.2 |
| `<details>` and `<summary>` are HTML block tags, so the finding details block is HTML and its `<code>` content is written through `html.escape` | `lib/render.py:render_details` | `spec.txt:2399,2405@0.31.2` | 0.31.2 |
| A control sequence is CSI (`ESC [` in a 7-bit code, 09/11 in an 8-bit code), zero or more parameter bytes 03/00-03/15, zero or more intermediate bytes 02/00-02/15 and one final byte 04/00-07/14: the `[0-?]*[ -/]*[@-~]` of the pattern | `lib/mask.py:strip_control` | `ECMA-48.pdf:10-11@5th-1991` (§5.4), `ECMA-48.pdf:36@5th-1991` (§8.3.16 CSI), `ECMA-48.pdf:9-10@5th-1991` (Tables 2a, 2b) | 5th edition |
| OSC, DCS, SOS, PM and APC open a control string that ends with ST (`ESC \` in a 7-bit code, 09/12 in an 8-bit code); the 7-bit forms are `ESC ]`, `ESC P`, `ESC X`, `ESC ^`, `ESC _` and the 8-bit forms 09/13, 09/00, 09/08, 09/14, 09/15 | `lib/mask.py:strip_control` | `ECMA-48.pdf:13@5th-1991` (§5.6), `ECMA-48.pdf:51@5th-1991` (§8.3.89 OSC), `ECMA-48.pdf:70@5th-1991` (§8.3.143 ST), `ECMA-48.pdf:10@5th-1991` (Table 2b) | 5th edition |
| An OSC may also end with BEL (07/00); terminal emulators accept it (xterm's OSC 8 hyperlinks are written that way in practice) | `lib/mask.py:strip_control` | **unverified** in ECMA-48, which names ST only (`ECMA-48.pdf:13@5th-1991`, §5.6): the BEL terminator is xterm's convention and its source is not pinned by kubegap; the pattern accepts both | — |
| An escape sequence other than CSI and the control strings is ESC, zero or more intermediate bytes from column 02 (02/00-02/15) and one final byte from columns 03 to 07 excluding 07/15 (03/00-07/14): `ESC c`, `ESC ( B`, `ESC =`. ECMA-48 defines ESC and defers its use to ECMA-35 | `lib/mask.py:strip_control` | `ECMA-35.pdf:28@6th-1994` (§13.1 Structure of escape sequences); `ECMA-48.pdf:43@5th-1991` (§8.3.48 ESC, "the use of ESC is defined in Standard ECMA-35") | ECMA-35 6th edition, ECMA-48 5th edition |
| C0 is 00/00-01/15, C1 in an 8-bit code is 08/00-09/15, and DEL is 07/15, not a control function of ECMA-48 (ISO/IEC 646 defines it; ECMA-48 names it in Annex F); newline (00/10) and tab (00/09) are C0 and are the two kept | `lib/mask.py:strip_control` | `ECMA-48.pdf:8@5th-1991` (Table 1, §5.2), `ECMA-48.pdf:9@5th-1991` (§5.3 b), `ECMA-48.pdf:10@5th-1991` (Table 2b), `ECMA-48.pdf:89@5th-1991` (Annex F.8.1 DEL) | 5th edition |


## Component detection rules, the two version tables, and where the pod-metrics steps read (#130, #136)

Three places held facts written from memory: the 112 evidence entries of `lib/components.py` (now 146, every one with a `source` on the entry itself, read against the official install manifests and Helm charts of 88 repositories at their newest tags, with the older tag where a name changed; `tests/test_sources.py` requires the citation and `tests/test_components.py` pins the release-name shapes), `rubric/k8s-versions.yaml` and `rubric/karpenter-compat.yaml` (every row cites its line), and the namespace, selector and port of the eleven `type: pod-metrics` steps (each step's `source:` cites the flag default, the chart's container port and the pod labels; the values are discovered at run time). The rows below are the facts that are not on one entry: what was read, what changed, and the version boundaries.

Versions read: the full table is long (88 repositories named in the `source` strings) and lives there; the ones a boundary depends on are named here. Three sources have no tags (the EKS user guide, rke2-charts, the old grafana/helm-charts README) and are cited at the commit read.

| Fact | kubegap | Source | Holds for |
|---|---|---|---|
| A Helm chart names its workload `<release>` when the release name contains the chart name and `<release>-<chart>` otherwise (the `fullname` helper), so the chart name is a suffix of the workload name and a detection pattern must start with `*`; the matcher is `fnmatch` on each side of the first `/` | `lib/components.py:match_name`, every workload entry (daemonset, deployment, statefulset, cronjob) | the `_helpers.tpl:14-22` (or nearby) of every chart cited on the entries, e.g. `kubernetes-sigs/aws-load-balancer-controller helm/aws-load-balancer-controller/templates/_helpers.tpl:14-25@v3.6.2`, `prometheus-community/helm-charts charts/kube-state-metrics/templates/_helpers.tpl:14-22@kube-state-metrics-9.0.0`; two helpers prefix the release name unconditionally (`stakater/Reloader deployments/kubernetes/chart/reloader/templates/_helpers.tpl:14-21@v1.4.23`, `rancher/rke2-charts charts/rke2-coredns/rke2-coredns/1.49.002/templates/_helpers.tpl:13-20@cf8a16b`) and cluster-autoscaler prefixes it unless the release name equals `<cloudProvider>-cluster-autoscaler` (`kubernetes/autoscaler cluster-autoscaler/charts/cluster-autoscaler/templates/_helpers.tpl:13-24@cluster-autoscaler-chart-9.60.0`); a release name that merely contains the chart name keeps its own name, which is why the patterns end in `*` too | every chart read |
| The Kubernetes end-of-life dates are `endOfLifeDate` of `data/releases/eol.yaml` (minors out of support) and `data/releases/schedule.yaml` (minors in support) of the kubernetes.io site, which schedule-builder maintains; 1.30 ends 2025-07-15, 1.31 2025-11-11, 1.34 2026-10-27, 1.37 (released 2026-08-26) 2027-10-28; the table also carries a `maintenanceModeStartDate` two months before, not read | `rubric/k8s-versions.yaml`, check `platform.k8s-version-eol` | `kubernetes/website data/releases/eol.yaml:7-21@32a3493`, `data/releases/schedule.yaml:7,17-19,41-43,81-83,131-132@32a3493`; release dates `kubernetes/sig-release releases/release-1.29/README.md:56@49c673c` to `releases/release-1.34/README.md:61@49c673c` | commit 32a3493 of 2026-11-04; the file changes with every release, one row per new minor |
| Karpenter's compatibility matrix is generated from `hack/docs/compatibilitymatrix_gen/compatibility.yaml`: each version names a min and a max Kubernetes minor, 1.4.x supports up to 1.32 (not 1.33), 1.0.5 extends 1.0 from 1.30 to 1.31, 1.8 ends at 1.34, 1.9 to 1.12 at 1.35, 1.13 and 1.14 at 1.36; the generated table at v1.15.1 does not list 1.15.x | `rubric/karpenter-compat.yaml`, `lib/scaling.py:compat_row`, check `platform.karpenter-version-compat` | `aws/karpenter-provider-aws hack/docs/compatibilitymatrix_gen/compatibility.yaml:51-108@v1.15.1`, `website/content/en/docs/upgrading/compatibility.md:18-20@v1.15.1` | v1.15.1 |
| Karpenter's metrics port default is 8000 through v0.37 and 8080 from v1.0.0; the chart exposes it as the container port `http-metrics` | step `karpenter.controller-metrics` | `kubernetes-sigs/karpenter pkg/operator/options/options.go:88@v0.37.0`, `:89@v1.0.0`, `:216@v1.15.1`; `aws/karpenter-provider-aws charts/karpenter/values.yaml:165-167@v1.15.1`, `charts/karpenter/templates/deployment.yaml:109-110,221-224@v1.15.1` | the port is read from the container; the step's 8000 is the pre-1.0 fallback |
| MetalLB's speaker serves `/metrics` on `--port` 7472 over plain HTTP through 0.15 and on 9120 over TLS with authentication and authorization from 0.16.0 (controller-runtime SecureServing with `WithAuthenticationAndAuthorization`; container port `metricshttps`), which neither the apiserver pod proxy nor a node curl can read; the chart labels its pods `app.kubernetes.io/component=speaker`, the native manifest `component=speaker` | step `metallb.metallb-speaker-metrics` (skips on 0.16+ with the reason), `lib/metallbctl.py:analyze` (reads the status resources instead, #155) | `metallb/metallb speaker/main.go:79@v0.13.12`, `:84@v0.14.9`, `:86@v0.15.3`, `:94@v0.16.0`, `:99@v0.16.2`; `internal/k8s/k8s.go:159-163@v0.16.2`; `config/manifests/metallb-native.yaml:2286-2297,2333-2334@v0.16.2`; `charts/metallb/values.yaml:40-42@v0.16.2`, `charts/metallb/templates/speaker.yaml:161-163,273-275@v0.16.2`; `manifests/metallb.yaml:329@v0.12.1` | 0.12.1 to 0.16.2 |
| The 0.16 endpoint authenticates a bearer token through TokenReview and authorizes `get` on the non-resource path `/metrics` through SubjectAccessReview, so a client certificate is not accepted and the speaker's own ServiceAccount needs both `create` rights. `metallb-native.yaml` does not grant them, the chart and the `*-prometheus.yaml` manifests do. The API server deletes `Authorization` after authenticating the request, so the pod proxy cannot carry a token. kubegap sends no credential of the user to a pod; it reads the status resources instead (#155) | `lib/metallbctl.py` (docstring) | `kubernetes-sigs/controller-runtime pkg/metrics/filters/filters.go:43,48,63,65,103-106,118@v0.25.1`; `metallb/metallb go.mod:31@v0.16.2`; `metallb/metallb charts/metallb/templates/rbac.yaml:43,46,70,73@v0.16.2`; `metallb/metallb config/prometheus-native/rbac-patch.yaml:7,16@v0.16.2`; `kubernetes/kubernetes staging/src/k8s.io/apiserver/pkg/endpoints/filters/authentication.go:90@v1.37.2`, `:87@v1.30.14` | v0.16.2; observed on kind: native manifest 500 for any token; with the two rights added, no token 401, client certificate 401, shipped ClusterRole 403, `nonResourceURLs: ["/metrics"]` 200; through the API server proxy 401 with the same token |
| The KEDA operator serves `/metrics` only with `--enable-prometheus-metrics` (true by default in the binary since v2.12.0), and the Helm chart passes `--enable-prometheus-metrics=false` by default: the operator step's skip is the chart's normal case | step `keda.operator-metrics` | `kedacore/keda cmd/operator/main.go:104,107,200@v2.21.0`, `cmd/operator/main.go:82-84@v2.12.0`; `kedacore/charts keda/values.yaml:793-797@v2.21.0`, `keda/templates/manager/deployment.yaml:113-116,163-171@v2.21.0` | v2.12.0 to v2.21.0 |
| No Helm chart read hard-codes its namespace (`.Release.Namespace`, or a `namespaceOverride`); `argocd`, `cert-manager`, `karpenter`, `keda`, `external-secrets`, `monitoring`, `metallb-system`, `kube-system` are the static manifests' or the READMEs' conventions | `lib/podmetrics.py:discover_targets` (reads the namespace from the workload) | `grafana-community/helm-charts charts/grafana/templates/_helpers.tpl:56-62@grafana-14.3.1`, `cert-manager/cert-manager deploy/charts/cert-manager/templates/_helpers.tpl:172-174@v1.21.3`, `external-secrets/external-secrets deploy/charts/external-secrets/templates/_helpers.tpl:43-49@v2.14.0`, `argoproj/argo-helm charts/argo-cd/templates/_helpers.tpl:308-310@argo-cd-10.12.0`, `aws/karpenter-provider-aws charts/karpenter/README.md:16@v1.15.1`, `metallb/metallb config/manifests/metallb-native.yaml:2274-2280@v0.16.2` | every chart read |
| The EKS managed add-ons (coredns, kube-proxy, aws-ebs-csi-driver, aws-efs-csi-driver, eks-pod-identity-agent, amazon-cloudwatch-observability) have no public manifest; their workload names are the upstream projects' and are observed on the EKS cluster | the rules coredns, kube-proxy, ebs-csi, efs-csi, eks-pod-identity-agent, cloudwatch-agent | **unverified in a public manifest**: the EKS user guide shows service accounts and pod names only (`awsdocs/amazon-eks-user-guide latest/ug/storage/ebs-csi.adoc:84-85@mainline`, `latest/ug/manage-access/aws-access/pod-id-agent-setup.adoc:78-79@mainline`); the upstream names are cited on the entries | — |
| The Bitnami `nginx-ingress-controller` image was deprecated and its directory removed from bitnami/containers on 2026-04-20 | the ingress-nginx rule's second image entry (kept for clusters still running it) | **unverified at a current tag**: the last commit on `bitnami/nginx-ingress-controller` in `bitnami/containers` is d2724e8d02 "Deprecate nginx-ingress-controller (#92562)"; no file exists at a tag after it | — |

## Kubernetes object fields the summaries read, against the API of 1.30 to 1.37 and the operator CRD types (#124)

`lib/*.py` build `summary.json` from `objects/<stem>.json` (every listable kind, `kubectl get -o json`), `events.json` (`kubectl get events -A -o json`, which returns core/v1 Events), `logs/node/<node>/kubelet-configz.json` (`/configz`, the KubeletConfiguration the kubelet runs with) and the kube-proxy ConfigMap. The tables below hold one row per Kind and field path those modules read (31 modules: resilience, events, logs, podmetrics, kubeproxy, metallbctl, coredns, hygiene, summarize, security, diff, evaluate, collect, discover, setup, storage, ebscsi, localpath, k3s, traffic, stackhealth, nodeos, scaling, karpenterctl, albctl, vpccni, kedactl, certmanager, esoctl, argocdctl, tenancy): the function that reads it, what it assumes, the Go struct line that declares the field (its `json:` tag) at the oldest and the newest supported tag plus any boundary tag, the controller line that sets a status field or the defaults.go line of a default, and the verdict. The AWS API response fields the same modules read are #125; the tool output files are the #119 tables and the store APIs the #126 table.

Versions read: kubernetes/kubernetes v1.30.14, v1.31.14, v1.32.13, v1.33.13, v1.34.13, v1.35.10, v1.36.6, v1.37.2 (the newest patch of every supported minor; the EKS cluster runs 1.32, the k3s cluster 1.33); kubernetes-sigs/karpenter and aws/karpenter-provider-aws v0.37.0 (`karpenter.sh/v1beta1`, the EKS cluster), v1.0.0 (first `v1`), v1.15.1; kedacore/keda v2.17.2, v2.19.0, v2.21.0; cert-manager/cert-manager v1.14.4, v1.21.3; external-secrets/external-secrets v0.9.13 (`v1beta1`), v0.16.0 (first `apis/externalsecrets/v1`), v0.17.0, v2.1.0, v2.14.0; argoproj/argo-cd v2.13.0, v2.14.0, v3.0.0, v3.1.0, v3.2.0, v3.6.1 (gitops-engine at the SHAs its go.mod pins); metallb/metallb v0.14.5, v0.16.2; kubernetes-sigs/aws-load-balancer-controller v2.7.2, v3.6.2; aws/amazon-vpc-cni-k8s v1.19.0, v1.23.2; aws/aws-network-policy-agent v1.1.5, v1.4.3; kubernetes-sigs/aws-ebs-csi-driver v1.37.0, v1.67.2 with kubernetes-csi/external-attacher v4.7.0, v4.13.0; kubernetes-csi/external-snapshotter v8.1.0, v8.7.0; rancher/local-path-provisioner v0.0.31, v0.0.37 with sig-storage-lib-external-provisioner v10.0.1, v11.0.1; actions/actions-runner-controller v0.27.6 (summerwind) and gha-runner-scale-set-0.10.1, 0.15.1; k3s-io/k3s v1.33.5+k3s1, v1.37.2+k3s1 and k3s-io/api v0.1.3, v0.2.1 (ETCDSnapshotFile); containerd v1.7.27, v1.7.29, v2.0.5, v2.1.3, v2.2.3 and api v1.8.0; cri-tools v1.32.0; kubernetes/ingress-nginx controller-v1.12.1; traefik v3.3.6, v1.7.34; amazon-eks-pod-identity-webhook v0.6.5, v0.6.19; helm v3.22.1, v4.3.1. The observed layer is the two snapshots of 2026-11-05 (EKS `onoma-cluster/2026-11-05T02-54-30`, k3s `default/2026-11-05T02-07-16`): served `apiVersion` of every CRD, presence of every field.

Five facts hold across the tables. (1) No core API field the modules read was added, removed or renamed between v1.30.14 and v1.37.2: the `json:` tags of every struct read were compared at all eight tags; the only relevant addition is the HPA condition `ScaledToZero` (v1.36.6, not read), and core/v1 `Endpoints` is deprecated from 1.33 but still served and reconciled at 1.37. (2) Every API default the modules fall back on (probe timings 1/10/3, `terminationGracePeriodSeconds` 30, `revisionHistoryLimit` 10, webhook `failurePolicy` Fail and `timeoutSeconds` 10, CronJob history 3/1, `backoffLimit` 6, Deployment `maxUnavailable` 25%, DaemonSet 1, kubelet and kube-proxy defaults) is the same at both ends and is materialized on every served object, so the fallbacks never fire. (3) `kubectl get events` returns core/v1; an Event written through the events.k8s.io recorder (the scheduler's `Scheduled`, `FailedScheduling`, `Preempted`) has no `count` there and keeps its repeats in `series.count` (`pkg/apis/events/v1/conversion.go:40-42@v1.37.2`), which is why every reader counts through `events.count_of`. (4) The CRD paths that depend on the API version: Karpenter v1beta1→v1 moves `expireAfter` from `spec.disruption` to `spec.template.spec`, renames `nodeClassRef.apiVersion` to `group`, respells `consolidationPolicy` `WhenUnderutilized` as `WhenEmptyOrUnderutilized` and drops the NodeClaim condition `Expired`; ESO v1beta1→v1 (first at v0.16.0; v1beta1 `served: false` at 2.x) keeps every path read; Argo CD 3.1.0 adds `syncPolicy.automated.enabled` and 3.0.0 halves the default resync to 120 s; aws-load-balancer-controller 3.0.0 makes `targetGroupARN` optional next to `targetGroupName`; KEDA 2.17.0 adds `fallback.behavior`. (5) StatefulSet and DaemonSet declare `status.conditions` but no controller writes them; their rollouts are dated by their ControllerRevisions.

Reading the types found 40 defects, all fixed in this issue's pull request except three recorded only: K1-1 (the CronJob file name since #156: every CronJob check passed on nothing), K1-2/K3-6 (Event `count`), K1-3 (StatefulSet `updateStrategy`), K1-4 (`DisruptionAllowed` reason), K1-5 (`matchFields`), K1-6 (PV pin key), K2-1 (StatefulSet/DaemonSet change time), K2-2 (`spec.ordinals.start`), K2-3 (`managedFields[].time` note), K2-4 (`eks.amazonaws.com/component`), K2-5 (Argo CD's default label), K2-6 (`diff.BODY_KEYS`), K2-7 (Endpoints deprecation, recorded), K3-1 (local-path `path`), K3-2 (EBS parameter case), K3-3 (`allowlist-source-range`), K3-4 (`targetGroupName`), K3-5 (`ExternalExpanding`), K3-7 (k3s token mask), N-1 (`automated.enabled`), N-2 (Argo CD 3 resync default), N-3 (CertificateRequest `Pending`), N-4 (v1beta1 `status.resources.nodes`), N-5 (summerwind RunnerSpec), N-6 (`Runner` owner), N-7/N-8 (Go durations), N-9 (Order `expired`), N-10 (`issuing_stuck` age), N-14 (sidecar requests), N-15 (`roleRef.kind`), O-1 (v1 `expireAfter`), O-2 (v1 `Expired`), O-3 (`rootVolume`), O-4 (DisruptionBlocked texts), O-5 (`WARM_PREFIX_TARGET`), O-6 (CNI booleans), O-7 (the v1beta1 CRD default hop limit 2, recorded), O-8 (`consolidationPolicy` spelling, recorded: no check compares the value). The row's verdict cell names the id. A row whose source could not be found in any repository says **unverified** with the reason (the EKS service labels and the `eks.amazonaws.com/addon` annotation, the Flux and k8s-sidecar label keys, `owner: helm`, the k3s role-label writers, the EBS `DriverName` constant at v1.67.2, the IRSA annotation key of the pod-identity webhook, Argo CD's notification key prefixes and flag defaults, the ESO `secretStoreRef.kind` default, three un-located boundary tags).
